The construction supplies a refreshed Markov model with finite contraction, one-step policy overlap, and the sufficient stationary density-ratio bound \(C=720\), hence \(q_C=719/720\approx0.9986\). Its immediate-weighting diagnostic remains separated from zero under the displayed interval arithmetic because current-action weighting averages the observed-glucose reward under behavior-stationary occupancy rather than target-stationary occupancy. The example therefore exhibits, in a stylized treatment-policy model, the occupancy mechanism that the depth parameter of the partial-history estimator is designed to control. Its certified content is the four structural constants and the bias interval; establishing the full model-class predicate for this grid --- in particular fixing an initial law and verifying the stationary-start, Markov-kernel, sequential-ignorability, and bounded-reward conditions at \(t_0=1/\log2\) and \(\zeta=\log(10/3)\) --- is a separate verification we do not carry out here, so the frontier theorem is not being applied to this grid.

\paragraph{Limitations.}
The signed-depth converse in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} uses a latent alphabet whose terminal depth grows with \(T\), so the lower-bound construction is calibrated to a sequence of finite experiments. Confidence intervals and policy-learning regret are separate research directions built on top of the value-estimation problem studied here. The finite insulin adaptation in \cref{obj:def:insulin-grid} is a controlled finite-state demonstration designed to instantiate the paper's overlap and contraction constants within an interpretable treatment-policy example. Three limits of that demonstration are worth stating. Its certificate covers the four structural constants and the bias interval, not the full model-class predicate, so no rate guarantee of this paper is asserted for it. It is not a finite-sample comparison of estimators, and it does not show that the depth calibrated from the sufficient bound \(C=720\) is the practically preferable depth on this grid. And a data-driven choice of depth for an unknown radius remains open here as elsewhere in the paper.


\appendix

\section*{Appendices}

\section{Proofs and auxiliary constructions}

This appendix collects the auxiliary statements used to support the main rate calculations. The first group records the observable filtration and the finite-prefix quantities that enter the signed-depth likelihood comparison.

\begin{definitionv}{def:filter-quantities}[Filter quantities \(\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}},\ell_t,q_t^v,a_t^{(Q)}\)]
Define
\[
\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}
:=
\sigma(A_0,Y_0,\ldots,A_{t-1},Y_{t-1}),
\]
\[
\ell_t:=\min\{Q,t\},
\]
\[
q_t^v
:=
\Pr_v(J_t=Q\mid\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}),
\]
and
\[
a_t^{(Q)}
:=
L^{-(Q-\ell_t)}
\prod_{j=t-\ell_t}^{t-1}\mathbf 1\{A_j=1\},
\]
where the empty product is equal to one.
\end{definitionv}

The quantities in \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities} separate two sources of information in the observed history. The posterior \(q_t^v\) captures the chance that the hidden depth has reached its terminal value, while the factor \(a_t^{(Q)}\) records the visible action pattern needed for that event under the rare-action construction. This separation is the device that makes the observed likelihood calculation finite-prefix and observable.

The upper-bound argument begins with local second-moment and covariance controls for the PHIW scores. These statements isolate, respectively, the contribution from a single weighted window and the dependence between overlapping windows.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-window-second-moment}[Window second moment]
Let \(T,n_X,n_H\in\mathbb N\), let \(k\in\mathbb N\), let \(L\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M\) be a raw POMDP experiment on finite observed and hidden alphabets. Suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Sequential ignorability.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}.
\item \textbf{(Policy overlap.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} with constant \(L\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap level.)} \(L\ge 1\).
\item \textbf{(Kernel law.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}.
\item \textbf{(Bounded rewards.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}.
\item \textbf{(Window position.)} \(t\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\) and \(k\le t\).
\end{itemize}
For the depth-\(k\) score \(Z_t(k)\) of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, under the observed trajectory law of \(M\),
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[Z_t(k)^2\bigr]\le L^{k+1}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-window-second-moment}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Write an observed trajectory as
\[
o=((x_j,a_j,y_j))_{j=0}^{T-1}\in \mathsf O_T,
\]
and set
\[
\mathcal W_{t,k}
:=
\{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: j\le t,\ t-j\le k\}.
\]
For every \(j\), define the observable zero-cell policy ratio
\[
\rho_j(o)
:=
\begin{cases}
0, & b(a_j\mid x_j)=0,\\[2mm]
e(a_j\mid x_j)/b(a_j\mid x_j), & b(a_j\mid x_j)\ne 0.
\end{cases}
\]
Then \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator} gives
\[
Z_t(k)(o)=y_t\Pi_{t,k}(o),
\qquad
\Pi_{t,k}(o):=\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{t,k}}\rho_j(o).
\]
Since \(k\le t\), the window is the contiguous block
\[
\mathcal W_{t,k}=\{t-k,t-k+1,\ldots,t\}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiwScore

\item
The window product has observed-law mean one:
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[\Pi_{t,k}\bigr]=1.
\]
Put \(r=t-k\). Since \(k\le t\), this is an integer in \(\{0,\ldots,t\}\), and the window from step 1 is exactly the interval \(\{r,r+1,\ldots,t\}\). First establish the corresponding identity under the full trajectory law. For a full trajectory \(\tau\), write
\[
\rho_j(\tau)
:=
\begin{cases}
0, & b(A_j(\tau)\mid X_j(\tau))=0,\\[1mm]
e(A_j(\tau)\mid X_j(\tau))/b(A_j(\tau)\mid X_j(\tau)),
& b(A_j(\tau)\mid X_j(\tau))\ne 0,
\end{cases}
\]
so the full-law block identity to prove is
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[\prod_{j=r}^{t}\rho_j(\tau)\right]=1.
\]
The argument uses the theorem hypothesis that the target carrier is a policy vector, so for every observed state \(x\),
\[
e(a\mid x)\ge 0\quad(a\in\mathcal A),
\qquad
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}e(a\mid x)=1,
\]
together with the domination inequality supplied by \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap}. Under \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, conditional on the past and current joint state at epoch \(m\), the action has behavior probabilities \(b(\cdot\mid X_m)\). If \(b(a\mid x)=0\), then \(0\le e(a\mid x)\le Lb(a\mid x)=0\), hence \(e(a\mid x)=0\); therefore, for every observed state \(x\),
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid x)\,
\begin{cases}
0, & b(a\mid x)=0,\\[1mm]
e(a\mid x)/b(a\mid x), & b(a\mid x)\ne 0
\end{cases}
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}e(a\mid x)=1.
\]
Thus, for every \(m\in\{r,\ldots,t\}\), conditioning on the history and current joint state up to epoch \(m\) gives
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[\left.\rho_m(\tau)\,\right|\,
X_{0:m},H_{0:m},A_{0:m-1},Y_{0:m-1}\right]=1.
\]
Peeling the factors one epoch at a time, with \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} identifying the next history law after each peeled action, gives
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[\prod_{j=r}^{m}\rho_j(\tau)\right]
=
\mathbb E\!\left[\prod_{j=r}^{m-1}\rho_j(\tau)\right],
\qquad m=r,\ldots,t,
\]
where the product over \(\{r,\ldots,r-1\}\) is the empty product. Iterating from \(m=t\) down to \(m=r\) leaves the empty product, whose expectation is \(1\), and hence
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[\prod_{j=r}^{t}\rho_j(\tau)\right]=1.
\]
It remains to pass this identity to observed trajectories. Let \(O\) be the observation map
\[
O(\tau)=((X_j(\tau),A_j(\tau),Y_j(\tau)))_{j=0}^{T-1},
\]
so that the observed law is the pushforward of the full law by \(O\). By definition of \(\Pi_{t,k}\) and the interval identity \(\mathcal W_{t,k}=\{r,
\ldots,t\}\), for every full trajectory \(\tau\),
\[
\Pi_{t,k}(O(\tau))
=
\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{t,k}}\rho_j(O(\tau))
=
\prod_{j=r}^{t}\rho_j(\tau).
\]
The usual integration formula for a pushforward measure therefore gives
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[\Pi_{t,k}\bigr]
=
\int \Pi_{t,k}(o)\,d\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(o)
=
\int \Pi_{t,k}(O(\tau))\,d\mathbb P(\tau)
=
\int \prod_{j=r}^{t}\rho_j(\tau)\,d\mathbb P(\tau)
=1.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_phiwWindowProduct_eq_one

\item
For every observed trajectory \(o\) and every \(j\),
\[
0\le \rho_j(o)\le L.
\]
The lower bound uses the behavior-policy vector property from \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability} and the theorem hypothesis that the target carrier is a policy vector. The upper bound is immediate in a zero cell and otherwise follows from the domination inequality in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} by dividing \(e(a_j\mid x_j)\le Lb(a_j\mid x_j)\) by the positive number \(b(a_j\mid x_j)\). Hence
\[
0\le \Pi_{t,k}(o)\le L^{|\mathcal W_{t,k}|}.
\]
The map \(j\mapsto t-j\) injects \(\mathcal W_{t,k}\) into \(\{0,\ldots,k\}\), so
\[
|\mathcal W_{t,k}|\le k+1.
\]
Since \(L\ge 1\),
\[
0\le \Pi_{t,k}(o)\le L^{k+1}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiwWindowProduct_le_pow_card

\item
By \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}, the full trajectory law has \(|Y_t|\le 1\) almost surely; pushing forward to the observed law gives
\[
|y_t|\le 1
\qquad \mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}\text{-almost surely}.
\]
Together with the preceding product bound, this also gives
\[
|Z_t(k)|\le L^{k+1}
\qquad \mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}\text{-almost surely},
\]
so the square and the comparison function \(L^{k+1}\Pi_{t,k}\) are integrable under the observed law.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiwScore_memLp

\item
For \(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}\)-almost every observed trajectory,
\[
Z_t(k)^2
=
y_t^2\Pi_{t,k}^2
\le
1\cdot \Pi_{t,k}^2
\le
L^{k+1}\Pi_{t,k},
\]
where the last inequality uses \(0\le \Pi_{t,k}\le L^{k+1}\). Integrating and using the normalization from step 2,
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[Z_t(k)^2\bigr]
\le
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[L^{k+1}\Pi_{t,k}\bigr]
=
L^{k+1}\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[\Pi_{t,k}\bigr]
=
L^{k+1}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_sq_phiwScore_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-overlapping-window-covariance}[Overlapping-window covariance]
Let \(M\) be a raw POMDP experiment of length \(T\) on finite observed and hidden alphabets. Let \(k,h\in\mathbb N\), let \(L\in\mathbb R\), and let \(t,u\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\). Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Model conditions.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} with overlap constant \(L\), \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}, and \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}.
\item \textbf{(Overlap constant.)} \(L\ge 1\).
\item \textbf{(Initial window.)} \(k\le t\).
\item \textbf{(Lag identity.)} \(u=t+h\).
\item \textbf{(Overlapping lag.)} \(1\le h\le k\).
\end{itemize}
Then the depth-\(k\) scores \(Z_t(k)\) of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, evaluated under the observed trajectory law of \(M\), satisfy
\[
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\bigr|
\le
2L^{k+1-h}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-overlapping-window-covariance}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Let
\[
\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}:=\mathcal L_{\mathrm{obs}}(M)
\]
be the observed-trajectory law of \(M\). Since it is the pushforward of the probability law of the full raw experiment under the observation map, \(\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\) is a probability measure. Also, from \(k\le t\), \(u=t+h\), and \(h\ge 1\), we have
\[
k\le u .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.abs_covariance_phiwScore_le_overlapDecay

\item We use the following covariance reduction. If \(\mu\) is a probability law, \(\xi,\eta\in L^2(\mu)\), and a number \(B\) satisfies
\[
\int |\xi|\,d\mu\le 1,\qquad
\int |\eta|\,d\mu\le 1,\qquad
\int |\xi\eta|\,d\mu\le B,\qquad
1\le B,
\]
then
\[
|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mu}(\xi,\eta)|\le 2B .
\]
Indeed,
\[
|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mu}(\xi,\eta)|
\le
\int |\xi\eta|\,d\mu
+
\left(\int |\xi|\,d\mu\right)
\left(\int |\eta|\,d\mu\right),
\]
by expanding the covariance and applying the triangle inequality. The two \(L^1\)-bounds give
\[
\left(\int |\xi|\,d\mu\right)
\left(\int |\eta|\,d\mu\right)\le 1,
\]
because both factors are nonnegative. Hence
\[
|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mu}(\xi,\eta)|
\le B+1\le 2B .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.abs_covariance_le_two_mul

\item Apply this reduction with
\[
\xi(w):=Z_t(k)(w),
\qquad
\eta(w):=Z_u(k)(w),
\qquad
B:=L^{k+1-h},
\]
for observed trajectories \(w\). The two scores are square-integrable under \(\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\). To see this, write the one-step ratio in \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator} as
\[
\rho_j(w)
:=
\begin{cases}
 e(A_j(w)\mid X_j(w))/b(A_j(w)\mid X_j(w)),& b(A_j(w)\mid X_j(w))>0,\\
 0,& b(A_j(w)\mid X_j(w))=0,
\end{cases}
\]
for every observed trajectory \(w\). The policy-vector parts of \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:policy-overlap} give \(b(a\mid x),e(a\mid x)\ge 0\). If \(b(a\mid x)>0\), \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} gives
\[
0\le {e(a\mid x)\over b(a\mid x)}\le L .
\]
If \(b(a\mid x)=0\), then \(e(a\mid x)\le Lb(a\mid x)=0\), hence \(e(a\mid x)=0\), and the zero-cell convention sets the ratio equal to zero. Thus, for every observed trajectory \(w\),
\[
0\le \rho_j(w)\le L
\qquad\text{for every }j .
\]
Also \(|Y_r|\le 1\) almost surely by \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}. Hence, for every time \(r\),
\[
|Z_r(k)(w)|
\le
\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_r}\rho_j(w)
\le
L^{|\mathcal W_r|}
\le
L^{k+1},
\qquad
\mathcal W_r:=\{j: j\le r,\ r-j\le k\},
\]
outside the null set where the reward bound fails. The window \(\mathcal W_r\) is a subset of the integer interval \(\{r-k,\ldots,r\}\), so \(|\mathcal W_r|\le k+1\). The map \(w\mapsto Z_r(k)(w)\) is measurable: the observed trajectory space carries real rewards and is not finite, so measurability is not a counting argument, but each coordinate map \(w\mapsto(X_j(w),A_j(w))\) and \(w\mapsto Y_j(w)\) is measurable, the ratio \(\rho_j\) is a measurable function of the first of these by the two-case definition above, and \(Z_r(k)\) is a finite product of these measurable maps. Since \(\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\) is a probability law, the almost-sure bound above implies \(Z_r(k)\in L^2(\mu_{\mathrm{obs}})\), and in particular it applies to \(r=t\) and \(r=u\).

Moreover, \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} and the initial-window conditions \(k\le t\) and \(k\le u\) give
\[
\int |Z_t(k)|\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\le 1,
\qquad
\int |Z_u(k)|\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\le 1 .
\]
For a single time \(r\) with \(k\le r\), this last estimate is obtained as follows. For integers \(r_{\mathrm{lo}},r_{\mathrm{hi}}\) with \(0\le r_{\mathrm{lo}}\le r_{\mathrm{hi}}<T\), define
\[
\Gamma_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},r_{\mathrm{hi}}}(w)
:=
\prod_{j=r_{\mathrm{lo}}}^{r_{\mathrm{hi}}}\rho_j(w),
\]
with the empty product equal to one when the lower endpoint is above the upper endpoint. This chronological block has unit expectation:
\[
\int \Gamma_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},r_{\mathrm{hi}}}(w)\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}(w)=1 .
\]
To prove this, pull the integral back from \(\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}\) to the full trajectory law, so that \(X_j,A_j\), and \(Y_j\) are full-trajectory coordinates. For \(s\in\{r_{\mathrm{lo}},\ldots,r_{\mathrm{hi}}\}\), let
\[
\mathscr G_s
:=
\sigma(X_0,H_0,A_0,Y_0,\ldots,A_{s-1},Y_{s-1},X_s,H_s)
\]
and
\[
\Gamma^-_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},s}
:=
\prod_{j=r_{\mathrm{lo}}}^{s-1}\rho_j,
\]
where the product is one for \(s=r_{\mathrm{lo}}\). The factor \(\Gamma^-_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},s}\) is \(\mathscr G_s\)-measurable. By \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, conditionally on \(\mathscr G_s\) the action \(A_s\) has law \(b(\cdot\mid X_s)\). The zero-cell convention and the implication \(b(a\mid x)=0\Rightarrow e(a\mid x)=0\) computed above give
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid X_s)\rho(a,X_s)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid X_s)
=
1,
\]
and hence
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[
\Gamma^-_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},s}\rho_s
\mid \mathscr G_s
\right]
=
\Gamma^-_{r_{\mathrm{lo}},s} .
\]
The remaining coordinates after the action at time \(s\) are integrated with the probability kernel in \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}; integrating first over the fibre containing \((Y_s,X_{s+1},H_{s+1})\) preserves the expectation of any function of \(\mathscr G_s\). Applying the displayed conditional identity successively for \(s=r_{\mathrm{hi}},r_{\mathrm{hi}}-1,\ldots,r_{\mathrm{lo}}\) removes the factors one at a time and leaves the empty product, whose expectation is one. Taking \(r_{\mathrm{lo}}=r-k\) and \(r_{\mathrm{hi}}=r\) gives
\[
\int \prod_{j=r-k}^{r}\rho_j(w)\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}(w)=1 .
\]
Now
\[
Z_r(k)(w)
=
Y_r(w)\prod_{j=r-k}^{r}\rho_j(w),
\]
and \(|Y_r|\le 1\) almost surely by \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}. Therefore
\[
\int |Z_r(k)|\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}
\le
\int \prod_{j=r-k}^{r}\rho_j(w)\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
1 .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_abs_phiwScore_le_one

\item It remains to bound the cross moment. For the two overlapping windows define
\[
\mathcal W_t:=\{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: t-k\le j\le t\},
\qquad
\mathcal W_u:=\{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: u-k\le j\le u\},
\qquad
\mathcal W_{\cup}:=\mathcal W_t\cup\mathcal W_u .
\]
Since \(t\) and \(u\) are elements of \(\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\), their integer values are valid finite-time endpoints. The assumptions \(k\le t\), \(u=t+h\), and \(h\le k\) give \(t-k\le u\) and identify the union in the formal finite index set as
\[
\mathcal W_t\cup\mathcal W_u
=
\{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: t-k\le j\le u\}.
\]
The same endpoint inequalities give the cardinality bound for the overlap,
\[
|\mathcal W_t\cap\mathcal W_u|\le k+1-h .
\]
Using the same zero-cell ratio \(\rho_j\) as above, the policy-vector and overlap parts of \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:policy-overlap}, together with \(L\ge 1\), give
\[
0\le \rho_j(w)\le L
\qquad\text{for every }j,w .
\]
Therefore
\[
\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_t\cap\mathcal W_u}\rho_j(w)
\le
L^{|\mathcal W_t\cap\mathcal W_u|}
\le
L^{k+1-h},
\]
the second inequality by the cardinality bound \(|\mathcal W_t\cap\mathcal W_u|\le k+1-h\) together with the monotonicity of \(n\mapsto L^{n}\) for \(L\ge1\); the intersection need not have exactly \(k+1-h\) indices, so this step is an inequality and not an identity.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_abs_phiwScore_mul_le_overlapDecay

\item Since \(|Y_t|\le 1\) and \(|Y_u|\le 1\) almost surely,
\[
|Z_t(k)Z_u(k)|
\le
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_t}\rho_j\right)
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_u}\rho_j\right).
\]
The product over two windows decomposes into the product over the union times the product over the overlap:
\[
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_t}\rho_j\right)
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_u}\rho_j\right)
=
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{\cup}}\rho_j\right)
\left(\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_t\cap\mathcal W_u}\rho_j\right).
\]
Combining this with the previous display gives the pointwise almost-sure bound
\[
|Z_t(k)Z_u(k)|
\le
L^{k+1-h}
\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{\cup}}\rho_j .
\]
The finite-window identity from the preceding step rewrites the union product in exactly chronological form:
\[
\mathcal W_{\cup}
=
\{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: t-k\le j\le u\},
\qquad
\prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{\cup}}\rho_j(w)
=
\prod_{j=t-k}^{u}\rho_j(w).
\]
The interval-normalization argument proved above applies with lower endpoint \(t-k\) and upper endpoint \(u\). The lower endpoint is nonnegative because \(k\le t\), the upper endpoint is in \(\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\) because \(u\) is a time index, and \(t-k\le u\) follows from \(u=t+h\). Thus
\[
\int \prod_{j\in\mathcal W_{\cup}}\rho_j(w)\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int \prod_{j=t-k}^{u}\rho_j(w)\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
1 .
\]
Hence
\[
\int |Z_t(k)Z_u(k)|\,d\mu_{\mathrm{obs}}
\le
L^{k+1-h}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_abs_phiwScore_mul_le_overlapDecay

\item Finally,
\[
1\le L^{k+1-h},
\]
because \(L\ge 1\). The covariance reduction from Step 2, applied with \(B=L^{k+1-h}\), yields
\[
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k),Z_u(k))\bigr|
\le
2L^{k+1-h},
\]
which is the claimed bound.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.abs_covariance_phiwScore_le_overlapDecay
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The analysis of separated windows uses both the observed law and the full trajectory law. The next definitions fix those probability spaces and the observation map connecting them.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_15}[Observed-trajectory law]
For a raw POMDP experiment \(M=(\mathcal X,\mathcal H,K,b,e,\mathcal L(\mathsf O_T))\), let
\[
\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}:=\operatorname{Law}^{\mathrm{obs}}_M
\]
denote the probability law of the observed trajectory
\[
\mathsf O_T=((X_t,A_t,Y_t))_{t=0}^{T-1}
\]
induced by the behavior-policy experiment \(M\). Equivalently, under \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\), \(S_0\sim d_b\), \(A_t\sim b(\cdot\mid X_t)\), and \(K\) generates \((Y_t,S_{t+1})\) from \((S_t,A_t)\), with only \((X_t,A_t,Y_t)_{t=0}^{T-1}\) retained.
\end{definitionv}

\begin{definitionv}{synth_17}[Full trajectory law and observation map]
For a raw POMDP experiment \(M=(\mathcal X,\mathcal H,K,b,e,\mathcal L(\mathsf O_T))\) with joint state space \(\mathcal S=\mathcal X\times\mathcal H\), let \(\mathbb P_M\) denote the full behavior-policy trajectory law on
\[
(S_0,A_0,Y_0,S_1,\ldots,A_{T-1},Y_{T-1},S_T).
\]
Under \(\mathbb P_M\), the initial state satisfies \(S_0\sim d_b\), each action is drawn as \(A_t\sim b(\cdot\mid X_t)\), and the kernel \(K\) generates \((Y_t,S_{t+1})\) from \((S_t,A_t)\), for \(t=0,\ldots,T-1\).

Let \(O\) be the observation map from a full trajectory to its observed trajectory,
\[
O(S_0,A_0,Y_0,\ldots,A_{T-1},Y_{T-1},S_T)
:=((X_t,A_t,Y_t))_{t=0}^{T-1},
\]
where \(S_t=(X_t,H_t)\). The observed behavior-policy law is the pushforward
\[
\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}:=\mathbb P_M\circ O^{-1}.
\]
\end{definitionv}

Together, \cref{obj:synth_15,obj:synth_17} let the covariance calculation move between observable scores and latent-state transition operators. The observed law is the sampling law for the estimator, while the full law retains the latent state needed to apply contraction after a gap.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-separated-window-peeling}[Separated window peeling]
Let \(M\) be a raw POMDP experiment with trajectory length \(T\), and let \(L\), the policy-overlap constant, be real. Let \(Z_s(k)\) denote the depth-\(k\) partial-history weighted reward score from \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}. Suppose:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Model conditions.)} \(M\) satisfies \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:policy-overlap,obj:ass:pomdp-kernel,obj:ass:bounded-reward} with overlap constant \(L\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap scale.)} \(1\le L\).
\item \textbf{(Mature earlier window.)} The history depth \(k\) satisfies \(k\le t\).
\item \textbf{(Separated future window.)} For an epoch \(t\) and a gap \(\gamma\in\mathbb N\), the epoch
\[
u=t+1+\gamma+k
\]
lies in the observed horizon, equivalently \(t+1+\gamma+k<T\).
\end{itemize}
For \(p\in\{b,e\}\), let \(P_p\) be the policy-induced transition operator obtained from the reward-marginalized kernel under policy \(p\), let
\[
g_e(s)=\sum_a e(a\mid x(s))\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a),
\]
and put
\[
\Phi_{\gamma,k}(s)=P_b^\gamma P_e^k g_e(s).
\]
Then, with \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\) the observed law induced by the observed trajectory map \(O\),
\[
\int Z_t(k)(w)Z_u(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-peeling}]
Set
\[
\iota_0=t+1,\qquad \iota_1=t+1+\gamma,\qquad \iota_2=\iota_1+k=u .
\]
The horizon condition gives \(\iota_2<T\), and \(k\le t\) makes the earlier score mature.  For a full trajectory \(\tau\), write the joint state at epoch \(m\) as
\[
S_m(\tau):=(X_m(\tau),H_m(\tau))
\]
and write
\[
\operatorname{hist}_m(\tau)
=
\bigl((S_j(\tau))_{j<m},(A_j(\tau),Y_j(\tau))_{j<m},S_m(\tau)\bigr)
\]
for the state-and-epoch history through epoch \(m\).  For an observed trajectory \(w\), write its epoch-\(j\) coordinates as
\[
w_j=(x_j(w),a_j(w),y_j(w)).
\]
Define the totalized one-step ratio, for an observed state \(x\) and action \(a\), by
\[
\rho(a\mid x)
:=
\begin{cases}
0, & b(a\mid x)=0,\\
e(a\mid x)/b(a\mid x), & b(a\mid x)>0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
\rho_j(w):=\rho(a_j(w)\mid x_j(w)).
\] For
\(0\le j_{\mathrm{lo}}\le j_{\mathrm{hi}}\le T-1\), put
\[
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{j_{\mathrm{lo}}:j_{\mathrm{hi}}}(\tau)
:=
\prod_{j=j_{\mathrm{lo}}}^{j_{\mathrm{hi}}-1}\rho_j(O(\tau)),
\qquad
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{j_{\mathrm{hi}}:j_{\mathrm{hi}}}(\tau):=1 .
\]
Thus the superscript \({<}\) means that the upper endpoint is excluded: the largest ratio in a
nonempty block is \(\rho_{j_{\mathrm{hi}}-1}\). In particular,
\(\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}\) stops at \(\rho_{u-1}\), while the terminal factor
\(\rho_u\) is written separately below. The target-policy one-step reward regression is
\[
g_e(s)=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}e(a\mid x(s))
\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a),
\]
and the separated future function is \(\Phi_{\gamma,k}=P_b^\gamma P_e^k g_e\).  In this proof the policy-induced transition operator is the reward-marginalized kernel
\[
(P_pF)(s)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}p(a\mid x(s))
\int F(s')\,K(dy\times ds'\mid s,a),
\qquad p\in\{b,e\},
\]
for every bounded \(F:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\) is the pushforward of \(\mathbb P_M\) under \(O\),
\[
\int Z_t(k)(w)Z_u(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))Z_u(k)(O(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
The measurability needed for this change of variables follows from the observable definition of \(Z_t(k)\) in \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}.

\item Define lifted carriers on state histories by
\[
\Lambda_0(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0}(\tau))
:=Z_t(k)(O(\tau)),
\]
and, for \(0\le \nu<\gamma+k\), let \(\Lambda_{\nu+1}\) be the lift of \(\Lambda_\nu\) that forgets the last appended action, reward, and next state:
\[
\Lambda_{\nu+1}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0+\nu+1}(\tau))
:=
\Lambda_\nu(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0+\nu}(\tau)).
\]
The base carrier is well defined because \(Z_t(k)\) uses only rewards, actions, and observed states through epoch \(t\), while \(t<\iota_0\); hence every lifted carrier satisfies
\[
\Lambda_\nu(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0+\nu}(\tau))
=
Z_t(k)(O(\tau))
\qquad(0\le \nu\le \gamma+k).
\]
The depth-\(k\) window in \(Z_u(k)\) is inclusive.  Since \(u=\iota_2\) and \(u-k=\iota_1\), the half-open block contains the factors with \(\iota_1\le j<\iota_2\), and the endpoint contributes the separate terminal factor:
\[
Z_u(k)(O(\tau))
=
Y_{\iota_2}(\tau)\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}(\tau)\,
\rho_{\iota_2}(O(\tau)).
\]
Therefore
\[
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))Z_u(k)(O(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\int
\Lambda_{\gamma+k}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_2}(\tau))\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}(\tau)\,
\rho_{\iota_2}(O(\tau))Y_{\iota_2}(\tau)\,
d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
The totalized ratios obey \(0\le\rho(a\mid x)\le L\): on a positive behavior cell this is the
domination inequality divided by \(b(a\mid x)>0\), and on a zero cell it follows from the definition
of \(\rho\). Hence finite state spaces, \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}, and the finite product bound
\(\prod_j\rho_j\le L^{\#\{j\}}\) make all displayed integrands integrable. Empty behavior or target
blocks are interpreted as products equal to one; in particular, when \(k=0\) the block
\(\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}\) is empty and the endpoint factor
\(\rho_{\iota_2}(O(\tau))\) is still present.

\item The terminal reward peel is
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\int
\Lambda_{\gamma+k}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_2})\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}\,
\rho_{\iota_2}Y_{\iota_2}\,d\mathbb P_M  \\
&\qquad =
\int
\Lambda_{\gamma+k}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_2})\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}\,
g_e(S_{\iota_2})\,d\mathbb P_M .
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, condition first on the history and action at \(\iota_2\).  \Cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}
supplies the conditional law of \((Y_{\iota_2},S_{\iota_2+1})\) as
\(K(\cdot\mid S_{\iota_2},a)\). Applying this conditional-law identity to the measurable test
function \((y,s')\mapsto y\), with integrability supplied by \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}, and then
marginalizing over the successor state gives
\[
E[Y_{\iota_2}\mid \operatorname{hist}_{\iota_2},A_{\iota_2}=a]
=
\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid S_{\iota_2},a).
\]
By \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, the action is drawn from \(b(\cdot\mid X_{\iota_2})\).  For every observed state \(x\) and action \(a\), the totalized ratio satisfies
\[
b(a\mid x)\rho(a\mid x)=e(a\mid x).
\]
Indeed, if \(b(a\mid x)>0\), this is the definition of \(\rho\). If \(b(a\mid x)=0\), then the
domination inequality in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} gives \(e(a\mid x)\le0\), while the target policy has nonnegative coordinates, so
\(e(a\mid x)=0\), matching the zero-cell value. Applying this identity at \(x=X_{\iota_2}\) and
summing over \(a\) gives
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}e(a\mid X_{\iota_2})
\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid S_{\iota_2},a)
=g_e(S_{\iota_2}),
\]
by the displayed definition of \(g_e\).

\item For every \(\iota_1\le m<\iota_2\), put \(\nu=m-\iota_0\), so
\(\gamma\le\nu<\gamma+k\).  For every bounded \(F:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\), the target-ratio
peeling step is
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\int
\Lambda_{\nu+1}(\operatorname{hist}_{m+1})\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:m+1}\,
F(S_{m+1})\,d\mathbb P_M \\
&\qquad =
\int
\Lambda_{\nu}(\operatorname{hist}_{m})\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:m}\,
(P_eF)(S_m)\,d\mathbb P_M .
\end{aligned}
\]
The first target peel has \(m=\iota_1\) and lowers the carrier from \(\Lambda_{\gamma+1}\) to
\(\Lambda_\gamma\); the last has \(m=\iota_2-1\) and lowers \(\Lambda_{\gamma+k}\) to
\(\Lambda_{\gamma+k-1}\). Iterating the displayed identity backward over these values of \(m\)
therefore removes exactly the \(k\) target-window ratios. Indeed, \(\Lambda_{\nu+1}\) is the lift of
\(\Lambda_\nu\), and
\[
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:m+1}
=
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:m}\rho_m.
\]
Conditioning on the history and action at \(m\), \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} gives the transition integral over \((Y_m,S_{m+1})\), and the reward coordinate is integrated out:
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}
 b(a\mid X_m)\rho(a\mid X_m)
 \int F(s')\,K(dy\times ds'\mid S_m,a)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}
 e(a\mid X_m)
 \int F(s')\,K(dy\times ds'\mid S_m,a).
\]
The ratio identity displayed in the terminal peel justifies the replacement of \(b\rho\) by \(e\), including zero behavior cells, and the last display is exactly \((P_eF)(S_m)\).  Iterating for \(m=\iota_2-1,\ldots,\iota_1\), starting from \(F=g_e\), yields
\[
\int
\Lambda_{\gamma+k}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_2})\,
\operatorname{rat}^{<}_{\iota_1:\iota_2}\,
g_e(S_{\iota_2})\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\int
\Lambda_{\gamma}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_1})\,
(P_e^kg_e)(S_{\iota_1})\,d\mathbb P_M .
\]
When \(k=0\), this iteration is empty and the displayed identity reduces to the same integrand at \(\iota_1=\iota_2\).

\item For every \(\iota_0\le m<\iota_1\), put \(\nu=m-\iota_0\).  For every bounded \(F:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\), the behavior-gap peeling step is
\[
\int
\Lambda_{\nu+1}(\operatorname{hist}_{m+1})F(S_{m+1})\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\int
\Lambda_{\nu}(\operatorname{hist}_{m})(P_bF)(S_m)\,d\mathbb P_M .
\]
Here \(\operatorname{hist}_{m+1}\) is the history obtained from \(\operatorname{hist}_m\) by appending
\((A_m,Y_m,S_{m+1})\), and \(\Lambda_{\nu+1}\) is the lift of \(\Lambda_\nu\), so
\(\Lambda_{\nu+1}(\operatorname{hist}_{m+1})=\Lambda_\nu(\operatorname{hist}_m)\). Conditioning on the history and action at \(m\), \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability} supplies the behavior action law, while \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} supplies the joint law of \((Y_m,S_{m+1})\) given \((S_m,A_m)\).  Integrating out the reward coordinate gives, for each history through \(m\),
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid X_m)
\int F(s')\,K(dy\times ds'\mid S_m,a)
=
(P_bF)(S_m),
\]
which is the reward-marginalized behavior transition appearing in the displayed identity.  Iterating over \(m=\iota_1-1,\ldots,\iota_0\) with \(F=P_e^kg_e\) gives
\[
\int
\Lambda_{\gamma}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_1})(P_e^kg_e)(S_{\iota_1})\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\int
\Lambda_{0}(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0})
(P_b^\gamma P_e^kg_e)(S_{\iota_0})\,d\mathbb P_M .
\]
When \(\gamma=0\), this iteration is empty.  Since \(\Lambda_0(\operatorname{hist}_{\iota_0}(\tau))=Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\), \(S_{\iota_0}=S_{t+1}\), and \(\Phi_{\gamma,k}=P_b^\gamma P_e^kg_e\), combining the preceding displays proves
\[
\int Z_t(k)(w)Z_u(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-peeling} rewrites a separated score product as an earlier observable score multiplied by a future continuation value evaluated at \(S_{t+1}\). This identity supplies the point where the gap between windows becomes a behavior-transition power, followed by a target-policy window.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence}[Behavior-support operator congruence]
Let \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) satisfy \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with behavior policy \(b\), target policy \(e\), and the policy-overlap condition in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap}.  For \(p\in\{b,e\}\), write
\[
P_p(s,s')=\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} p(a\mid x(s))\,K(\{S'=s'\}\mid s,a),
\]
and let \(P_p^0 h=h\) and \(P_p^{m+1}h=P_p(P_p^m h)\).  Let \(d_b\) denote the behavior stationary law of \(P_b\).

Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Policy choice.)} The policy \(p\) is either \(b\) or \(e\).
\item \textbf{(Terminal agreement.)} The functions \(f,g:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\) satisfy
\[
f(s)=g(s)
\]
for every joint state \(s\) with \(d_b(s)>0\).
\item \textbf{(Initial support.)} The joint state \(s\) satisfies \(d_b(s)>0\).
\end{itemize}
Then, for every \(m\in\mathbb N\),
\[
(P_p^m f)(s)=(P_p^m g)(s).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence}]
Fix a policy \(p\in\{b,e\}\).  The argument first records the support-closure fact used at the induction step.  For joint states \(\sigma,\tau\in\mathcal S\) and an action \(a\in\{0,1\}\), write
\[
\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)
:=
K(\{S'=\tau\}\mid \sigma,a).
\]
Then
\[
P_p(\sigma,\tau)
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}p(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a).
\]

\begin{enumerate}
\item Suppose \(d_b(\sigma)>0\) and \(P_p(\sigma,\tau)>0\).  We claim that
\[
d_b(\tau)>0 .
\]
First show that \(P_b(\sigma,\tau)>0\).  If \(p=b\), this is immediate.  If \(p=e\), suppose instead that \(P_b(\sigma,\tau)=0\).  By \cref{obj:def:model-class}, the behavior policy is a probability vector and \(K\) is a Markov kernel, so every summand in
\[
P_b(\sigma,\tau)
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} b(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)
\]
is nonnegative.  Hence, for each action \(a\),
\[
b(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)=0 .
\]
For such an \(a\), if \(\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)=0\) then
\[
e(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)=0.
\]
If \(\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)>0\), the preceding zero product gives \(b(a\mid x(\sigma))=0\); by \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap},
\[
e(a\mid x(\sigma))
\le
L\,b(a\mid x(\sigma))
=
0,
\]
and the target policy is nonnegative by \cref{obj:def:model-class}, so \(e(a\mid x(\sigma))=0\).  Thus again
\[
e(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)=0 .
\]
Summing over \(a\) gives
\[
P_e(\sigma,\tau)
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}e(a\mid x(\sigma))\,\kappa_{\sigma,\tau}(a)
=
0,
\]
contradicting \(P_p(\sigma,\tau)>0\) in the case \(p=e\).  Therefore \(P_b(\sigma,\tau)>0\).

By \cref{obj:def:model-class}, \(d_b\) is stationary for \(P_b\), so
\[
d_b(\tau)
=
\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal S} d_b(\omega)P_b(\omega,\tau).
\]
All terms in this sum are nonnegative.  Therefore
\[
0
<
d_b(\sigma)P_b(\sigma,\tau)
\le
\sum_{\omega\in\mathcal S} d_b(\omega)P_b(\omega,\tau)
=
d_b(\tau),
\]
which proves the claim. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.behaviorSupport_closed_policyKernel

\item We now prove the asserted identity by induction on \(m\).  For \(m=0\),
\[
(P_p^0f)(s)=f(s)=g(s)=(P_p^0g)(s),
\]
because \(d_b(s)>0\) and \(f=g\) on the behavior support.

Assume the statement holds for \(m\): for every joint state \(\sigma\) with \(d_b(\sigma)>0\),
\[
(P_p^m f)(\sigma)=(P_p^m g)(\sigma).
\]
Let \(s\) satisfy \(d_b(s)>0\).  Using the recursive definition of the iterated operator,
\[
(P_p^{m+1}f)(s)
=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal S}P_p(s,\tau)(P_p^m f)(\tau),
\qquad
(P_p^{m+1}g)(s)
=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal S}P_p(s,\tau)(P_p^m g)(\tau).
\]
Fix \(\tau\in\mathcal S\).  If \(P_p(s,\tau)=0\), then the two \(\tau\)-summands are both zero.  If \(P_p(s,\tau)\neq 0\), the row nonnegativity of \(P_p\) gives \(P_p(s,\tau)>0\).  The support-closure claim from the previous step then gives \(d_b(\tau)>0\), so the induction hypothesis yields
\[
(P_p^m f)(\tau)=(P_p^m g)(\tau).
\]
Multiplying by \(P_p(s,\tau)\), the two \(\tau\)-summands agree.  Since this holds for every \(\tau\), the sums agree:
\[
(P_p^{m+1}f)(s)=(P_p^{m+1}g)(s).
\]
The induction proves the result for every \(m\in\mathbb N\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.markovOperatorIter_congr_on_behaviorSupport
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The support congruence in \cref{obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence} keeps transition-operator calculations on the behavior support. Because the overlap condition makes target transitions compatible with behavior support, functions that agree on the behavior-stationary support continue to agree after iterating either policy operator from a supported state.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-separated-window-covariance}[Separated window covariance]
Let \(T,n_X,n_H,k,h\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) be a raw POMDP experiment as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with the uniform contraction restriction of \cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction}. Write the contraction factor as \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\). Let \(t,u\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\) satisfy
\[
k\le t,\qquad u=t+h,\qquad k+1\le h .
\]
Then the depth-\(k\) scores \(Z_t(k)\) of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, evaluated under the observed trajectory law of \(M\), satisfy
\[
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\bigr|
\le
2\alpha^{h-k-1}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-covariance}]
Put
\[
\gamma:=h-k-1 .
\]
Then \(0\le \gamma\) by \(k+1\le h\), and
\[
u=t+h=t+1+\gamma+k .
\]
Let \(\mathbb P_M\) denote the full trajectory law and \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\) its pushforward under the observation map \(O\). For \(p\in\{b,e\}\), write
\[
P_p(s,s')=\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}p(a\mid x(s))K(\{S'=s'\}\mid s,a),
\qquad
g_e(s)=\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}e(a\mid x(s))\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a),
\]
and define
\[
\Phi_{\gamma,k}(s):=P_b^\gamma P_e^k g_e(s).
\]

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(L=\exp(\zeta)\) and \(\zeta>0\) in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, \(1\le L\). The maturity and separation hypotheses give \(t+1+\gamma+k<T\), so \cref{obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-peeling} applies and yields
\[
\int Z_t(k)(w)Z_u(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
For the companion mean identity, let \(r_{+}:=t+1\) and \(q_{\gamma}:=r_{+}+\gamma\), so \(u=q_{\gamma}+k\). Since \(u=q_{\gamma}+k\), the depth-\(k\) window in \(Z_u(k)\) is exactly \(q_{\gamma},q_{\gamma}+1,\ldots,u\). We claim
\[
\int Z_u(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int \Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
To prove this identity, condition first on \(S_{q_{\gamma}}\). Write
\[
\rho(a,x):=
\begin{cases}
 e(a\mid x)/b(a\mid x),& b(a\mid x)>0,\\
 0,& b(a\mid x)=0.
\end{cases}
\]
The zero-cell convention and \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} imply \(e(a\mid x)=0\) whenever \(b(a\mid x)=0\). Hence, for each pre-terminal epoch \(m_{\mathrm{win}}\in\{q_{\gamma},\ldots,u-1\}\),
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid x(s))\rho(a,x(s))
        K(\{S'=s'\}\mid s,a)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x(s))K(\{S'=s'\}\mid s,a)
=P_e(s,s'),
\]
while at the terminal epoch
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid x(s))\rho(a,x(s))
        \int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x(s))
        \int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a)
=g_e(s).
\]
Thus the target-weighted terminal window has conditional mean \(P_e^k g_e(S_{q_{\gamma}})\), with the terminal display alone applying when \(k=0\). Conditioning backward over the gap epochs \(t+1,\ldots,q_{\gamma}-1\), no score ratio appears, and \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} give the behavior propagation \(P_b^\gamma\); when \(\gamma=0\), this is the identity operator. Bounded rewards and the finite state and action spaces justify the displayed integrals. Therefore the conditional mean given \(S_{t+1}\) is \(P_b^\gamma P_e^k g_e(S_{t+1})=\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1})\), proving the claim. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_phiwScore_eq_futureFunction
Also, by definition of \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\),
\[
\int Z_t(k)(w)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(w)
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
Thus the covariance equals the centered full-law product
\[
\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k),Z_u(k))
=
\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
-
\Bigl(\int Z_t(k)(O(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)\Bigr)
\Bigl(\int \Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)\Bigr).
\]
The covariance identity is legitimate because
\[
Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\in L^2(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}).
\]
Indeed, write the window product in \(Z_m(k)\) as
\[
R_m^{(k)}(w):=\prod_{\substack{j\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}:\, j\le m,\ m-j\le k}}
\rho_j(w),
\qquad m\in\{t,u\}.
\]
The product contains at most \(k+1\) factors. The zero-cell convention and \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} make each factor nonnegative and at most \(L\) on observed behavior-support cells, while \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward} gives \(|Y_m|\le 1\) almost surely. Hence \(|Z_m(k)|\le L^{k+1}\) almost surely under \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\), and the two scores are square-integrable. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_phiwScore_mul_eq_futureFunction; CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_phiwScore_eq_futureFunction

\item The first score has unit \(L^1\) norm:
\[
\int |Z_t(k)(O(\tau))|\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)\le 1 .
\]
Indeed, with
\[
R_t^{(k)}(w):=\prod_{j=t-k}^{t}\rho_j(w),
\]
the zero-cell convention and \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} give
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left[R_t^{(k)}\right]=1
\]
by peeling the ratios from \(j=t\) down to \(j=t-k\): conditionally at each peeled epoch,
\[
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} b(a\mid X_j)\rho_j(a,X_j)
=
\sum_{a:\,b(a\mid X_j)>0} e(a\mid X_j)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid X_j)
=1 .
\]
By \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}, \(|Z_t(k)|\le R_t^{(k)}\) almost surely, which proves the displayed \(L^1\) bound after passing between the observed law and the full law by the observation map. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_abs_phiwScore_le_one

\item Let \(d_b\) be the stationary law of \(P_b\), and define the support-truncated regression on all of \(\mathcal S\) by
\[
\widetilde g_e(s):=
\begin{cases}
g_e(s),& d_b(s)>0,\\
0,& d_b(s)=0.
\end{cases}
\]
For every \(s\) with \(d_b(s)>0\),
\[
|g_e(s)|\le 1 .
\]
To see this, fix such an \(s\). For an action \(a\) with \(b(a\mid x(s))>0\), consider epoch \(0\). The history before epoch \(0\) is the unique empty history, so the full history-action singleton with current state \(s\) and action \(a\) has probability
\[
d_b(s)b(a\mid x(s))>0,
\]
by \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start} and the epoch-zero action factorization in \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}. Under the history-next-pair factorization supplied by \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}, \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward} gives, for almost every history-action pair, a kernel row whose reward coordinate is almost surely in \([-1,1]\). Since this epoch-zero singleton has positive mass, that almost-sure row statement holds at \((s,a)\), and hence
\[
\left|\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a)\right|\le 1 .
\]
If \(e(a\mid x(s))>0\), then \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} forces \(b(a\mid x(s))>0\). Therefore
\[
|g_e(s)|
\le
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x(s))
\left|\int y\,K(dy\times\mathcal S\mid s,a)\right|
\le
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x(s))
=1.
\]
For \(d_b(s)=0\), the definition gives \(|\widetilde g_e(s)|=0\). Consequently
\[
\sup_{s,s'}|\widetilde g_e(s)-\widetilde g_e(s')|\le 2 .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.rewardRegression_abs_le_one_of_behaviorSupport

\item Since \(t_0>0\), \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\) satisfies \(0\le \alpha\le 1\). For a function \(f:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\), write
\[
\operatorname{osc}(f):=\sup_{s,s'\in\mathcal S}|f(s)-f(s')|.
\]
For each \(p_\star\in\{b,e\}\), the rows of \(P_{p_\star}\) are probability vectors. Nonnegativity follows from the nonnegativity of the policy probabilities and of the kernel rows. For the row sums,
\[
\sum_{s'\in\mathcal S}P_{p_\star}(s,s')
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}p_\star(a\mid x(s))
   \sum_{s'\in\mathcal S}K(\{S'=s'\}\mid s,a)
=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}p_\star(a\mid x(s))
=1.
\]
Here \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} supplies the kernel row mass, \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability} supplies the behavior probability vector when \(p_\star=b\), and the target-carrier part of \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} supplies the target probability vector when \(p_\star=e\).

We first convert the total-variation contraction in \cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction} into the oscillation contraction used below. Let \(\nu\) and \(\nu'\) be probability vectors on the finite state space, and let \(f:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\). Put
\[
f_{\min}:=\min_{x\in\mathcal S} f(x),
\qquad
f_0(x):=f(x)-f_{\min}.
\]
Then \(0\le f_0(x)\le \operatorname{osc}(f)\) for every \(x\), and, since \(\nu\) and \(\nu'\) have equal total mass,
\[
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S} f(x)(\nu(x)-\nu'(x))
=
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S} f_0(x)(\nu(x)-\nu'(x)).
\]
The layer-cake identity on the finite state space gives
\[
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S} f_0(x)(\nu(x)-\nu'(x))
=
\int_{0}^{\operatorname{osc}(f)}
\bigl(\nu\{x:f_0(x)>\lambda_{\mathrm{lev}}\}
      -\nu'\{x:f_0(x)>\lambda_{\mathrm{lev}}\}\bigr)
\,d\lambda_{\mathrm{lev}},
\]
and therefore
\[
\left|
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S} f(x)(\nu(x)-\nu'(x))
\right|
\le
\operatorname{osc}(f)\,\|\nu-\nu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\]
Now fix states \(s,s'\). Applying \cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction} to the point masses \(\delta_s\) and \(\delta_{s'}\) gives
\[
\|\delta_s P_{p_\star}-\delta_{s'}P_{p_\star}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\,\|\delta_s-\delta_{s'}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le \alpha .
\]
Combining this with the preceding dual bound yields the one-step estimate
\[
|P_{p_\star}f(s)-P_{p_\star}f(s')|
\le
\alpha\,\operatorname{osc}(f).
\]
Taking the supremum over \(s,s'\) and iterating gives the finite-state oscillation form: whenever \(\operatorname{osc}(f)\le B_{\mathrm{osc}}\) for some \(B_{\mathrm{osc}}\ge0\),
\[
\operatorname{osc}(P_{p_\star}^{m_{\mathrm{iter}}}f)
\le
\alpha^{m_{\mathrm{iter}}}B_{\mathrm{osc}},
\qquad m_{\mathrm{iter}}\in\mathbb N.
\]
Applying this first with \(p_\star=e\), \(f=\widetilde g_e\), and \(B_{\mathrm{osc}}=2\), and then using \(\alpha^k\le1\), gives
\[
\sup_{s,s'}|P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s)-P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s')|\le 2.
\]
Applying it next with \(p_\star=b\) to \(P_e^k\widetilde g_e\) gives
\[
\sup_{s,s'}
\left|
P_b^\gamma P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s)
-
P_b^\gamma P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s')
\right|
\le
2\alpha^\gamma .
\] % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Probability.FiniteMarkovOscillation.oscillationBound_markovOperatorIter

\item Define
\[
\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(s):=P_b^\gamma P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s).
\]
Because \(g_e=\widetilde g_e\) on \(\{s:d_b(s)>0\}\), \cref{obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence} applied with policy \(e\) gives, for every behavior-supported state \(x\),
\[
P_e^k g_e(x)=P_e^k\widetilde g_e(x)
\qquad\text{whenever }d_b(x)>0.
\]
This pointwise behavior-supported equality is the terminal-agreement hypothesis needed for the second application of \cref{obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence}, now with policy \(b\). Thus, for every initial state \(s\) with \(d_b(s)>0\),
\[
\Phi_{\gamma,k}(s)=P_b^\gamma P_e^k g_e(s)
=P_b^\gamma P_e^k\widetilde g_e(s)
=\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(s).
\]
We next record the behavior-stationarity identity used at time \(t+1\). For every bounded \(F:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\) and every epoch \(m_{\mathrm{time}}\),
\[
\int F(S_{m_{\mathrm{time}}}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_b(s)F(s).
\]
This follows by induction on \(m_{\mathrm{time}}\). At the initial epoch the identity is \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start}. For the induction step, \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability} and \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} give the behavior peeling identity
\[
\int F(S_{m_{\mathrm{time}}+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\int \sum_{x\in\mathcal S}P_b(S_{m_{\mathrm{time}}}(\tau),x)F(x)\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
Using the induction hypothesis with the bounded function \(s\mapsto \sum_x P_b(s,x)F(x)\), the right-hand side becomes
\[
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_b(s)\sum_{x\in\mathcal S}P_b(s,x)F(x)
=
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S}\left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_b(s)P_b(s,x)\right)F(x)
=
\sum_{x\in\mathcal S}d_b(x)F(x),
\]
where the last equality is the stationarity equation for the behavior stationary law \(d_b\), part of the model-class data in \cref{obj:def:model-class}. Taking the zero-support indicator
\[
\chi_{\mathrm{null}}(s):=\mathbf 1\{d_b(s)=0\}
\]
at \(m_{\mathrm{time}}=t+1\) gives
\[
\int \mathbf 1\{d_b(S_{t+1}(\tau))=0\}\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)=0,
\]
so \(d_b(S_{t+1}(\tau))>0\) for \(\mathbb P_M\)-almost every \(\tau\). Hence
\[
\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))
=
\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))
\quad\text{for }\mathbb P_M\text{-almost every }\tau .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.integral_curState_eq_behaviorStationary

\item Put
\[
A(\tau):=Z_t(k)(O(\tau)),
\qquad
\overline\Phi:=
\int \widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau).
\]
Using the almost-sure replacement from the previous step, both
\[
\int A(\tau)\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\int A(\tau)\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
\]
and
\[
\int \Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\int \widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
=
\overline\Phi
\]
hold; the integrability needed for these replacements follows from the finite state space and the score bound above. The centered representation from the first step therefore gives
\[
\left|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k),Z_u(k))\right|
=
\left|
\int A(\tau)\bigl(\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))-\overline\Phi\bigr)
\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
\right|.
\]
The oscillation bound in the fourth step implies, for every \(\tau\),
\[
\left|\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}(S_{t+1}(\tau))-\overline\Phi\right|
\le 2\alpha^\gamma,
\]
because \(\overline\Phi\) is an average of values of \(\widetilde\Phi_{\gamma,k}\). Therefore, using the unit \(L^1\) bound from the second step,
\[
\left|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k),Z_u(k))\right|
\le
\int |A(\tau)|\,2\alpha^\gamma\,d\mathbb P_M(\tau)
\le
2\alpha^\gamma
=
2\alpha^{h-k-1}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.abs_integral_mul_sub_mul_integral_le_oscillation
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

Together, \cref{obj:lem:phiw-window-second-moment,obj:lem:phiw-overlapping-window-covariance,obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-peeling,obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence,obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-covariance} decompose the sampling fluctuation into a weighted-window term and a geometric dependence tail. The overlap condition controls the inflation inside the window, while contraction controls pairs whose windows are separated by at least one time step.

The next statement sums those covariance bounds over future times. It is the finite-sample accounting step that turns the local bounds into a variance inequality for the average score.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-future-covariance-sum}[Aggregate future-covariance bound]
Let \(T\ge1\), \(k\in\mathbb N\), \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) be as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}. Write \(L=e^{\zeta}\), \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\), and \(\mathscr T_k=\{t:k\le t\le T-1\}\). Then, for every \(t\in\mathscr T_k\), the depth-\(k\) scores of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator} satisfy, under the observed trajectory law of \(M\),
\[
\sum_{\substack{u\in\mathscr T_k\\ t<u}}
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\bigr|
\le
\frac{2L^{k+1}}{L-1}+\frac{2}{1-\alpha}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-future-covariance-sum}]
Fix \(t\in\mathscr T_k\). By \cref{obj:def:model-class}, \(0<\zeta\) and \(0<t_0\), so
\[
L=e^\zeta>1,
\qquad
0\le \alpha=e^{-1/t_0}<1 .
\]
The same membership supplies the model restrictions used by the covariance estimates below.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Define the future index set and its two lag classes by
\[
\mathscr U_t:=\{u\in\mathscr T_k:t<u\},
\qquad
\lambda_{t,u}:=u-t\quad (u\in\mathscr U_t),
\]
and
\[
\mathscr N_t:=\{u\in\mathscr U_t:\lambda_{t,u}\le k\},
\qquad
\mathscr G_t:=\{u\in\mathscr U_t:k+1\le \lambda_{t,u}\}.
\]
For each \(u\in\mathscr U_t\), the lag \(\lambda_{t,u}\) is a positive integer, hence exactly one of
\(\lambda_{t,u}\le k\) and \(k+1\le\lambda_{t,u}\) holds. Thus
\[
\mathscr U_t=\mathscr N_t\,\dot\cup\,\mathscr G_t .
\]
Writing
\[
\Gamma_t(u):=
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\bigr|,
\qquad u\in\mathscr U_t,
\]
we get
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr U_t}\Gamma_t(u)
=
\sum_{u\in\mathscr N_t}\Gamma_t(u)
+
\sum_{u\in\mathscr G_t}\Gamma_t(u).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_abs_covariance_future_le

\item For \(u\in\mathscr N_t\), the lag identity is \(u=t+\lambda_{t,u}\) and
\(1\le\lambda_{t,u}\le k\). Since \(k\le t\) and \(L\ge1\), \cref{obj:lem:phiw-overlapping-window-covariance} gives
\[
\Gamma_t(u)\le 2L^{k+1-\lambda_{t,u}}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr N_t}\Gamma_t(u)
\le
2L\sum_{u\in\mathscr N_t}L^{k-\lambda_{t,u}}.
\]
The map
\[
j_{\mathrm{near}}(u):=k-\lambda_{t,u},
\qquad u\in\mathscr N_t,
\]
is injective and takes its values in \(\{0,\ldots,k-1\}\). Since \(L\ge0\),
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr N_t}L^{k-\lambda_{t,u}}
\le
\sum_{0\le j<k}L^j .
\]
For \(L>1\),
\[
\sum_{0\le j<k}L^j
=
\frac{L^k-1}{L-1}
\le
\frac{L^k}{L-1}.
\]
Combining the last three displays yields
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr N_t}\Gamma_t(u)
\le
\frac{2L^{k+1}}{L-1}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.geom_partial_le_pow_div

\item For \(u\in\mathscr G_t\), the lag identity is \(u=t+\lambda_{t,u}\) and
\(k+1\le\lambda_{t,u}\). Hence \cref{obj:lem:phiw-separated-window-covariance} gives
\[
\Gamma_t(u)
\le
2\alpha^{\lambda_{t,u}-k-1}.
\]
Thus
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr G_t}\Gamma_t(u)
\le
2\sum_{u\in\mathscr G_t}\alpha^{\lambda_{t,u}-k-1}.
\]
The map
\[
j_{\mathrm{sep}}(u):=\lambda_{t,u}-k-1,
\qquad u\in\mathscr G_t,
\]
is injective and takes its values in \(\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\). Since \(\alpha\ge0\),
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr G_t}\alpha^{\lambda_{t,u}-k-1}
\le
\sum_{0\le j<T}\alpha^j .
\]
Because \(0\le\alpha<1\),
\[
\sum_{0\le j<T}\alpha^j
\le
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha^j
=
\frac{1}{1-\alpha}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\sum_{u\in\mathscr G_t}\Gamma_t(u)
\le
\frac{2}{1-\alpha}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.geom_partial_le_inv

\item Adding the two bounds over the disjoint decomposition of \(\mathscr U_t\) gives
\[
\sum_{\substack{u\in\mathscr T_k\\ t<u}}
\bigl|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\bigr|
=
\sum_{u\in\mathscr U_t}\Gamma_t(u)
\le
\frac{2L^{k+1}}{L-1}+\frac{2}{1-\alpha}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_abs_covariance_future_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:phiw-future-covariance-sum} supplies the two terms that reappear in the assembled variance bound. The geometric sum over overlapping lags gives the policy-overlap contribution, and the contraction tail gives the dependence contribution.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-raw-variance}[Raw variance assembly]
Let \(T\) and \(k\) be nonnegative integers, let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M\) be a raw POMDP experiment. Write \(L=e^\zeta\) for the policy-overlap factor and \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\) for the contraction factor. Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Horizon.)} \(T\ge 1\).
\item \textbf{(Model class.)} \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), with \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
\item \textbf{(History depth.)} \(k\le \lfloor T/2\rfloor\).
\end{itemize}
For the depth-\(k\) scores \(Z_t(k)\) of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, form the unclipped partial-history average
\[
\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t=k}^{T-1} Z_t(k).
\]
Under the observed trajectory law obtained by projecting the full trajectory law of \(M\) onto observed histories, its variance satisfies
\[
\operatorname{Var}\!\left(\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t=k}^{T-1} Z_t(k)\right)
\le
\frac{2}{T}\left\{
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)
+
\frac{4}{1-\alpha}
\right\}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-raw-variance}]
Let \(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}\) denote the observed trajectory law and let
\(\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\) denote expectation under this law.  Let
\(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{full}}\) denote the full trajectory law carried by \(M\),
and let \(\mathbb E_{\mathrm{full}}\) denote expectation under that law.  Put
\[
\mathscr T_k:=\{t\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}: k\le t\},
\qquad
\mathfrak B_k:=
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)+\frac{4}{1-\alpha}.
\]
For \(t\in\mathscr T_k\), write
\[
\Gamma_t^{(k)}
:=
\prod_{\substack{0\le j\le T-1\\ j\le t,\ t-j\le k}}\rho_j,
\qquad
Z_t(k)=Y_t\Gamma_t^{(k)} .
\]
Since \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), \cref{obj:def:model-class} gives
\(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), hence
\[
L=e^\zeta>1,
\qquad
0\le \alpha=e^{-1/t_0}<1.
\]
Also \(k\le \lfloor T/2\rfloor\) implies
\[
|\mathscr T_k|=T-k>0,
\qquad
\frac{T}{2}\le T-k.
\]
In particular \(\mathfrak B_k\ge0\).  The raw average is
\[
\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t=k}^{T-1} Z_t(k)
=
\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k).
\]
% lean: variance_phiwRaw_le

\begin{enumerate}
\item
For each \(t\in\mathscr T_k\), \cref{obj:lem:phiw-window-second-moment} applies with the sequential-ignorability, policy-overlap, kernel-law, and bounded-reward clauses supplied by \cref{obj:def:model-class}, together with \(L=e^\zeta\ge 1\) and \(k\le t\).  It gives
\[
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[Z_t(k)^2\bigr]
\le L^{k+1}.
\]
Using the standard identity
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k))
=
\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl[Z_t(k)^2\bigr]
-\bigl(\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}Z_t(k)\bigr)^2,
\]
we obtain
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k))
\le L^{k+1}.
\]
% lean: integral_sq_phiwScore_le

\item
Fix \(t\in\mathscr T_k\).  The aggregate covariance estimate in \cref{obj:lem:phiw-future-covariance-sum} applies to the same model class and the same future index set \(\mathscr T_k\).  Therefore
\[
\sum_{\substack{u\in\mathscr T_k\\ t<u}}
\left|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\right|
\le
\frac{2L^{k+1}}{L-1}+\frac{2}{1-\alpha}.
\]
% lean: sum_abs_covariance_future_le

\item
Now expand the variance of the finite sum:
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k)\right)
=
\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}\sum_{u\in\mathscr T_k}
\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr).
\]
Bounding each covariance by its absolute value and then using covariance symmetry gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k)\right)
\le
\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z_t(k))
+
2\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}
\sum_{\substack{u\in\mathscr T_k\\ t<u}}
\left|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathrm{obs}}\bigl(Z_t(k),Z_u(k)\bigr)\right|.
\]
Using the diagonal and future-sum bounds,
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k)\right)
\le
|\mathscr T_k|L^{k+1}
+
2|\mathscr T_k|
\left(
\frac{2L^{k+1}}{L-1}
+
\frac{2}{1-\alpha}
\right)
=
(T-k)\mathfrak B_k.
\]
% lean: sum_abs_symmetric_eq_diag_add_two_upper

\item
Finally,
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(
\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k)
\right)
=
\frac{1}{(T-k)^2}
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(\sum_{t\in\mathscr T_k}Z_t(k)\right)
\le
\frac{\mathfrak B_k}{T-k}.
\]
Since \(T/2\le T-k\), \(T>0\), and \(\mathfrak B_k\ge0\),
\[
\frac{\mathfrak B_k}{T-k}
\le
\frac{2\mathfrak B_k}{T}.
\]
Substituting the definition of \(\mathfrak B_k\) gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}_{\mathrm{obs}}\!\left(\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t=k}^{T-1} Z_t(k)\right)
\le
\frac{2}{T}\left\{
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)
+
\frac{4}{1-\alpha}
\right\}.
\]
% lean: variance_phiwRaw_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The two terms in \cref{obj:lem:phiw-raw-variance} have distinct origins: multiplying observable policy ratios over a window produces the \(L^{k+1}\) term, while serial dependence contributes the contraction term. The bound is stated for the unclipped average because the displayed variance is the component used before the clipped estimator's squared-error risk is assembled.

The next statement supplies the fixed-radius upper bound for the partial-history importance-weighted estimator. It gives both the rate bound for the balanced depth and the corresponding variance calculation for the unclipped average.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-upper}[Balanced PHIW upper bound]
Fix constants satisfying
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Mixing scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Policy-overlap scale.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Latent-overlap radius.)} \(C\ge 1\).
\end{itemize}
Let \(k_T\) be the balanced history depth in \cref{obj:def:history-depth}, and set
\[
\beta=\frac{2}{2+t_0\zeta}.
\]
Then there exist constants \(c^\star>0\) and \(T_\star\in\mathbb N\) such that, for every \(T\ge T_\star\), the estimator-specific worst-case squared risk from \cref{obj:synth_13} of the observable PHIW estimator \(\widehat\theta_{k_T}\) from \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator} satisfies
\[
R_T(t_0,\zeta,C;\widehat\theta_{k_T})
\le
c^\star T^{-\beta}.
\]
Moreover, for every \(T\ge T_\star\), every finite alphabet sizes \(n_X,n_H\), and every raw POMDP experiment \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), the variance under the observed trajectory law induced by \(M\) of the unclipped partial-history average
\[
\frac{1}{T-k_T}\sum_{t=k_T}^{T-1} Z_t(k_T)
\]
satisfies
\[
\operatorname{Var}
\!\left(\frac{1}{T-k_T}\sum_{t=k_T}^{T-1} Z_t(k_T)\right)
\le
\frac{2}{T}
\left[
e^{\zeta(k_T+1)}
\left(1+\frac{4}{e^\zeta-1}\right)
+
\frac{4}{1-e^{-1/t_0}}
\right].
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-upper}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Define
\[
\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0),\qquad L=\exp(\zeta),\qquad
\beta=\frac{2}{2+t_0\zeta},
\qquad q_C=\frac{C-1}{C}.
\]
The assumptions \(t_0>0\) and \(\zeta>0\) give
\[
0<\alpha<1,\qquad 1<L,\qquad 0<\beta<1.
\]
Also \(C\ge1\) implies
\[
0\le q_C\le1,\qquad q_C^2\le1.
\]
Let
\[
\mathfrak d
=
2\log(1/\alpha)+\log L
=
\frac{2}{t_0}+\zeta .
\]
Then \(\mathfrak d>0\) and
\[
\beta=\frac{2/t_0}{\mathfrak d}.
\]
Since \(\log T=o(T)\), there is an integer \(T_{\mathrm{bal}}\ge1\) such that, for every
\(T\ge T_{\mathrm{bal}}\),
\[
0\le \frac{\log T}{\mathfrak d}\le \frac{T}{2}.
\]
For such \(T\), \cref{obj:def:history-depth} gives
\[
k_T=\left\lfloor\frac{\log T}{\mathfrak d}\right\rfloor ,
\]
and hence
\[
\frac{\log T}{\mathfrak d}-1<k_T\le \frac{\log T}{\mathfrak d}.
\]
Therefore
\[
\alpha^{2k_T}
=
\exp(-2k_T/t_0)
\le
\exp(2/t_0)\,T^{-\beta},
\]
and
\[
\frac{L^{k_T+1}}{T}
=
\exp\{(k_T+1)\zeta-\log T\}
\le
\exp(\zeta)\,T^{-\beta}.
\]
With
\[
c_{\mathrm{bal}}:=\exp(2/t_0)+\exp(\zeta)>0,
\]
we have, for every \(T\ge T_{\mathrm{bal}}\),
\[
\alpha^{2k_T}+\frac{L^{k_T+1}}{T}
\le
c_{\mathrm{bal}}T^{-\beta}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.historyDepth_rate

\item Set
\[
\mathsf A:=1+\frac{4}{L-1},
\qquad
\mathsf B:=\frac{4}{1-\alpha},
\qquad
\mathsf D:=\max\{4,2\mathsf A\},
\]
and define
\[
c^\star:=\mathsf D\,c_{\mathrm{bal}}+2\mathsf B,
\qquad
T_\star:=T_{\mathrm{bal}}.
\]
Since \(L>1\) and \(\alpha<1\), the constants \(\mathsf A,\mathsf B,\mathsf D\) are positive, and so
\(c^\star>0\).
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiw_upper

\item Fix \(T\ge T_\star\). Then \(1\le T\), \(k_T\le T/2\), and \(k_T<T\). For every
\(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), \cref{obj:lem:radius-sensitive-phiw}, applied with
\(k=k_T\), gives
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[
\{\widehat\theta_{k_T}-\theta(K,e)\}^2
\right]
\le
4q_C^2\alpha^{2k_T}
+
\frac{2}{T}\left(L^{k_T+1}\mathsf A+\mathsf B\right).
\]
Using \(q_C^2\le1\), \(\mathsf D\ge4\), and \(\mathsf D\ge2\mathsf A\),
\[
4q_C^2\alpha^{2k_T}
+
\frac{2}{T}\left(L^{k_T+1}\mathsf A+\mathsf B\right)
\le
\mathsf D\left(\alpha^{2k_T}+\frac{L^{k_T+1}}{T}\right)
+
2\mathsf B\,T^{-1}.
\]
The balance bound from the first step and the inequality \(T^{-1}\le T^{-\beta}\), valid because
\(T\ge1\) and \(0<\beta<1\), yield
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[
\{\widehat\theta_{k_T}-\theta(K,e)\}^2
\right]
\le
\mathsf D c_{\mathrm{bal}}T^{-\beta}
+
2\mathsf B T^{-\beta}
=
c^\star T^{-\beta}.
\]
There are two cases. If the indexed class \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) is nonempty, the pointwise bound over every member of the class, together with the estimator-specific risk definition in \cref{obj:synth_13}, gives
\[
R_T(t_0,\zeta,C;\widehat\theta_{k_T})
\le
c^\star T^{-\beta}.
\]
If the indexed class is empty, the real-valued worst-case-risk functional used for \(R_T\) has empty supremum equal to \(0\). Since \(c^\star>0\), \(T\ge1\), and \(T^{-\beta}\ge0\), the bound also holds in this case.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiw_upper

\item It remains to record the variance assertion.
For every \(T\ge T_\star\), the definition of \(T_\star\) gives \(1\le T\), and the minimum in \cref{obj:def:history-depth} gives
\(k_T\le\lfloor T/2\rfloor\).  Apply \cref{obj:lem:phiw-raw-variance} with
\(k=k_T\), \(L=e^\zeta\), and \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\).  Its conclusion is exactly
\[
\operatorname{Var}_M\!\left(
\frac1{T-k_T}\sum_{t=k_T}^{T-1}Z_t(k_T)
\right)
\le
\frac{2}{T}\left[
e^{\zeta(k_T+1)}\left(1+\frac{4}{e^\zeta-1}\right)
+\frac{4}{1-e^{-1/t_0}}
\right],
\]
uniformly over the finite alphabet sizes and every
\(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), as required.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.variance_phiwRaw_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The bound in \cref{obj:lem:phiw-upper} identifies the observable estimator used for the upper side of the fixed-\(C\) comparison. The variance display isolates the cost of multiplying observable policy ratios over a window of length \(k_T\) and the serial-dependence contribution controlled by contraction. Balancing those terms gives the exponent \(\beta\) under fixed overlap and fixed latent-stationary domination.

For the lower side, the appendix uses the signed-depth alternatives introduced in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}. The next two statements record the contraction and stationary-law calculations that make the construction a member of the same finite-POMDP class.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-constant-policy-contraction}[Contraction of the constant-policy signed-depth kernel]
Let \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), \(C\ge1\), \(Q\ge1\), \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\) and \(r\in[0,1]\), and write \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\). Let \(P^{(r)}\) be the transition kernel induced on the joint state space \(\mathcal S_Q=\{0\}\times(\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\})\) of the signed-depth alternative of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} with terminal depth \(Q\) and index \(v\) by the constant policy \(a_r\) that plays action one with probability \(r\). Then, for all probability vectors \(\mu,\mu'\) on \(\mathcal S_Q\),
\[
\|\mu P^{(r)}-\mu'P^{(r)}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\|\mu-\mu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}} .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-constant-policy-contraction}]
Let
\[
\varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\},\qquad
\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0).
\]
For \(\sigma=(0,j,u)\) and \(\sigma'=(0,j',u')\) in \(\mathcal S_Q\), define the refresh law
\[
\eta_{C,Q}(\sigma')
=
\mathbf 1\{j'=0\}\,\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2},
\]
and define the residual kernel
\[
\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma')
=
\begin{cases}
\eta_{C,Q}(\sigma'), & j=Q,\\[2mm]
\dfrac{1-r}{2}+r\,\mathbf 1\{u'=u\}, & j<Q \text{ and } j'=j+1,\\[2mm]
0, & \text{otherwise.}
\end{cases}
\]
Expanding the transition rule in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} under the constant policy \(a_r(1)=r\), \(a_r(0)=1-r\), gives, for every \(\sigma,\sigma'\in\mathcal S_Q\),
\[
P^{(r)}(\sigma,\sigma')
=
(1-\alpha)\eta_{C,Q}(\sigma')
+
\alpha\,\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma').
\]
Indeed, at terminal depth \(j=Q\) both sides equal \(\eta_{C,Q}(\sigma')\); at depths \(j<Q\), the reset part contributes \((1-\alpha)\eta_{C,Q}(\sigma')\), while the advance-to-\(j+1\) part contributes \(\alpha[(1-r)/2+r\mathbf 1\{u'=u\}]\). % lean: signedDepth_policyKernel_refresh

The residual kernel is stochastic. Since \(C\ge1\), one has \(\varepsilon_C\in[0,1/2]\), so the two reset weights are nonnegative and
\[
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2}=1.
\]
If \(j=Q\), summing \(\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\cdot)\) over \(\mathcal S_Q\) therefore gives \(1\). If \(j<Q\), the only nonzero entries have \(j'=j+1\), and their two sign weights sum to
\[
\left(\frac{1-r}{2}+r\right)+\frac{1-r}{2}=1,
\]
with nonnegativity following from \(r\in[0,1]\). Thus
\[
\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma')\ge0
\quad\text{and}\quad
\sum_{\sigma'\in\mathcal S_Q}\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma')=1
\]
for every \(\sigma\in\mathcal S_Q\). % lean: signedDepthResidualKernel_probabilityVector

For any signed vector \(\lambda\) on \(\mathcal S_Q\), stochasticity of \(\Gamma_{C,r,Q}\) gives the total-variation nonexpansiveness bound
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\lambda\Gamma_{C,r,Q}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
&=
\frac12\sum_{\sigma'}\left|
\sum_{\sigma}\lambda(\sigma)\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma')
\right| \\
&\le
\frac12\sum_{\sigma'}\sum_{\sigma}
|\lambda(\sigma)|\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma') \\
&=
\frac12\sum_{\sigma}|\lambda(\sigma)|
\sum_{\sigma'}\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma')
=
\|\lambda\|_{\mathrm{TV}} .
\end{aligned}
\]
% lean: tvNorm_applyKernel_le

Now take probability vectors \(\mu,\mu'\). Because \(\sum_\sigma\mu(\sigma)=\sum_\sigma\mu'(\sigma)=1\), the common refresh term cancels:
\[
\begin{aligned}
(\mu P^{(r)}-\mu'P^{(r)})(\sigma')
&=
\alpha\sum_{\sigma}
(\mu(\sigma)-\mu'(\sigma))\Gamma_{C,r,Q}(\sigma,\sigma') .
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)>0\), the preceding display and nonexpansiveness yield
\[
\|\mu P^{(r)}-\mu'P^{(r)}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
=
\alpha\|(\mu-\mu')\Gamma_{C,r,Q}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\|\mu-\mu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\]
This is the claimed contraction. % lean: signedDepth_uniformContraction_actionOne
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-stationary-law}[Closed form of the signed-depth stationary law]
Let \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), \(C\ge1\), \(Q\ge1\), \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\) and \(r\in[0,1]\). Write \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\), \(\varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\), and \(w_j=(1-\alpha)\alpha^{j}/(1-\alpha^{Q+1})\). Let \(P^{(r)}\) be the kernel induced on \(\mathcal S_Q\) by the constant policy \(a_r\) for the signed-depth alternative of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} with terminal depth \(Q\) and index \(v\), and let \(d_r\) be the stationary law selected for \(P^{(r)}\). Then, for every hidden state \(h=(j(h),u(h))\),
\[
d_r(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}\,\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon_Cr^{j(h)}}{2}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-stationary-law}]
Write
\[
\bar d_r(0,(j,u))
:=
w_j\,\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C r^j}{2},
\qquad
j\in\{0,\ldots,Q\},\quad u\in\{-1,+1\}.
\]
We prove that this probability vector is stationary for \(P^{(r)}\), and then use the
contraction statement to identify it with the selected stationary law.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(t_0>0\), the value \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\) lies in \((0,1)\). Also
\(C\ge 1\) gives \(0\le \varepsilon_C\le 1/2\), and \(r\in[0,1]\) gives
\(0\le r^j\le 1\). Hence every factor
\((1+u\varepsilon_C r^j)/2\) is nonnegative. For each fixed depth \(j\),
\[
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
w_j\,\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C r^j}{2}
=w_j,
\]
and the geometric normalization gives
\[
\sum_{j=0}^Q w_j
=
\frac{1-\alpha}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}\sum_{j=0}^Q \alpha^j
=1.
\]
Thus \(\bar d_r\) is a probability vector on \(\mathcal S_Q\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthStationaryWeight_probabilityVector

\item The state kernel induced by the constant policy \(a_r\) has the following
hidden-state transition probabilities, obtained from the signed-depth dynamics in
\cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} by averaging the action-one and action-zero
advance weights. If \(j<Q\), then
\[
P^{(r)}\bigl((0,(j,u)),(0,(0,u'))\bigr)
=(1-\alpha)\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2},
\]
and
\[
P^{(r)}\bigl((0,(j,u)),(0,(j+1,u'))\bigr)
=
\alpha\,\frac{1+uu'r}{2}.
\]
If \(j=Q\), then
\[
P^{(r)}\bigl((0,(Q,u)),(0,(0,u'))\bigr)
=
\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2}.
\]
The remaining transitions have weight zero. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_policyKernel_apply

For a target state of depth \(0\), summing the displayed reset probabilities gives
\[
(\bar d_rP^{(r)})(0,(0,u'))
=
\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2}
\left\{
w_Q+(1-\alpha)\sum_{j=0}^{Q-1}w_j
\right\}.
\]
Since \(\sum_{j=0}^Q w_j=1\),
\[
w_Q+(1-\alpha)\sum_{j=0}^{Q-1}w_j
=
(1-\alpha)+\alpha w_Q
=
(1-\alpha)+
\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^{Q+1}}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}
=
\frac{1-\alpha}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}
=w_0.
\]
Therefore
\[
(\bar d_rP^{(r)})(0,(0,u'))
=
w_0\,\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C}{2}
=
\bar d_r(0,(0,u')).
\]

For a target state of depth \(m\in\{1,\ldots,Q\}\), only depth \(m-1\) can advance
to it, so
\[
(\bar d_rP^{(r)})(0,(m,u'))
=
\alpha w_{m-1}
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C r^{m-1}}{2}
\frac{1+uu'r}{2}.
\]
The sign sum is
\[
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C r^{m-1}}{2}
\frac{1+uu'r}{2}
=
\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C r^m}{2}.
\]
Using \(w_m=\alpha w_{m-1}\), this yields
\[
(\bar d_rP^{(r)})(0,(m,u'))
=
w_m\,\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C r^m}{2}
=
\bar d_r(0,(m,u')).
\]
Thus \(\bar d_rP^{(r)}=\bar d_r\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthStationaryWeight_isStationary

\item The contraction supplied by \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-constant-policy-contraction}
implies uniqueness of stationary probability vectors. Indeed, if \(\lambda\) and
\(\lambda'\) are stationary for \(P^{(r)}\), then
\[
\|\lambda-\lambda'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
=
\|\lambda P^{(r)}-\lambda'P^{(r)}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\|\lambda-\lambda'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\]
Because \(\alpha<1\) and total variation is nonnegative, the norm is zero, hence
\(\lambda=\lambda'\) on the finite state space. The selected law \(d_r\) is
stationary because the stationary vector \(\bar d_r\) exists, so uniqueness gives
\(d_r=\bar d_r\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.stationary_unique_of_contraction

\item Evaluating the identity \(d_r=\bar d_r\) at the hidden state
\(h=(j(h),u(h))\) gives
\[
d_r(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}\,\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon_C r^{j(h)}}{2},
\]
as claimed. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_stationaryLaw_apply
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-constant-policy-contraction} gives the uniform total-variation contraction of the signed-depth transition under any constant action-one probability. \Cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-stationary-law} then gives the stationary law explicitly: the depth mass is a truncated geometric sequence, and the sign imbalance is scaled by the action-one probability to the current depth.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-membership}[Signed-depth membership]
For every \(T\ge 1\), \(Q\ge 1\), \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), \(C>1\), and \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), let \(M_{Q,v}^T\) be the signed-depth experiment of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  Put
\[
\varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\},
\qquad
L=\exp(\zeta),
\qquad
w_j=\frac{(1-\alpha(t_0))\alpha(t_0)^j}{1-\alpha(t_0)^{Q+1}},
\]
where \(\alpha(t_0)\) is the mixing factor and \(j=0,\ldots,Q\).  Then:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Membership.)} \(M_{Q,v}^T\in \mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) in the sense of \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
\item \textbf{(Behavior stationary law.)} For every hidden state \(h\), with decoded depth \(j(h)\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\) and sign \(u(h)\in\{-1,+1\}\),
\[
d_b(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon_C L^{-j(h)}}{2}.
\]
\item \textbf{(Constant action-one stationary laws.)} For every \(r\in[0,1]\), if \(d_r\) denotes the stationary law induced by the constant policy that chooses action one with probability \(r\), then for every hidden state \(h\),
\[
d_r(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon_C r^{j(h)}}{2}.
\]
\item \textbf{(Target-over-behavior domination.)} For every joint state \(s\),
\[
d_e(s)\le (1+\varepsilon_C)d_b(s),
\qquad
1+\varepsilon_C\le C.
\]
\end{itemize}
The stationary target value of \cref{obj:def:target-value} is
\[
\theta(K,e)
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,\varepsilon_C\,w_Q .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-membership}]
Write \(\alpha=\alpha(t_0)=\exp(-1/t_0)\), \(L=\exp(\zeta)\), and
\(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_C\).  Since \(t_0>0\) and \(\zeta>0\), we have
\(0<\alpha<1\) and \(1<L\).  Also \(C>1\) gives \(C\ge 1\), so
\[
0\le \varepsilon=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\le \frac12 .
\]
For a hidden state \(h\), abbreviate \(j=j(h)\) and \(u=u(h)\in\{-1,+1\}\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item For \(r\in[0,1]\), let \(a_r\) be the constant policy with
\(\Pr(A_t=1)=r\), and let \(P^{(r)}\) be the induced transition kernel on the
joint state space
\[
\mathcal S_Q=\{(0,h):h\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\}\}.
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-constant-policy-contraction}, for all probability
laws \(\mu\) and \(\mu'\) on \(\mathcal S_Q\),
\[
\|\mu P^{(r)}-\mu'P^{(r)}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\|\mu-\mu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\]
The behavior policy in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} is the case
\(r=L^{-1}\), while the target policy is the case \(r=1\).  The displayed
inequality therefore gives the contraction required in
\cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction} for both policies. % lean: signedDepth_uniformContraction

\item For \(r\in[0,1]\), \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-stationary-law} identifies
the selected stationary law of the constant-policy kernel \(P^{(r)}\): for
every hidden state \(h\),
\[
d_r(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon r^{j(h)}}2 .
\]
Taking \(r=L^{-1}\) gives the behavior stationary law because the behavior
policy in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} chooses action one with
probability \(1/L\), and \((L^{-1})^{j(h)}=L^{-j(h)}\).  Thus
\[
d_b(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon L^{-j(h)}}2 .
\]
Taking \(r=1\) gives the target stationary law
\[
d_e(0,h)
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon}{2}.
\] % lean: signedDepth_stationaryLaw_apply

\item We now verify the model-class predicates in \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
The embedded experiment is generated by the initial law, the behavior policy,
and the time-homogeneous reward-transition kernel in
\cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  Therefore, for each epoch
\(t=0,\ldots,T-1\),
\[
\mathcal L(Y_t,X_{t+1},H_{t+1}\mid X_{0:t},H_{0:t},A_{0:t},Y_{0:t-1})
=
K(\cdot\mid X_t,H_t,A_t),
\]
which is \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}.  The behavior action law is
\[
b(1\mid0)=L^{-1},
\qquad
b(0\mid0)=1-L^{-1},
\qquad
b(0\mid0)+b(1\mid0)=1,
\]
and these two masses are nonnegative because \(L>1\).  Since the observed
state is the singleton \(\{0\}\), the chronological law factors at every epoch
\(t=0,\ldots,T-1\) as
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\mathbb P\!\left\{
\bigl((X_s,H_s)_{s<t},(A_s,Y_s)_{s<t},(X_t,H_t),A_t\bigr)
\in d\mathsf h\,da
\right\} \\
&\qquad=
