\mathbb P\!\left\{
\bigl((X_s,H_s)_{s<t},(A_s,Y_s)_{s<t},(X_t,H_t)\bigr)
\in d\mathsf h
\right\}\,b(da\mid X_t(\mathsf h)),
\end{aligned}
\]
which is \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}.  The finite reward symbols are
decoded as \(-1\) and \(+1\), so
\[
\Pr(|Y_t|\le1)=1,
\]
giving \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}.  The policy-overlap inequality is
\[
e(1\mid0)=1=L\,b(1\mid0),
\qquad
e(0\mid0)=0\le L\,b(0\mid0),
\]
so \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} holds with factor \(L\).  The initial law is
\[
\Pr\{S_0=(0,h)\}
=
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon L^{-j(h)}}2
=
d_b(0,h),
\]
where the last equality is the stationary-law formula from the case
\(r=L^{-1}\).  Thus \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start} holds.

For the latent stationary overlap, first identify the two selected stationary
laws and the initial law on the same cells.  The target and behavior formulas
from Step 2 give
\[
d_e(0,h)
=
w_j\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon}{2},
\qquad
d_b(0,h)
=
w_j\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon L^{-j}}{2}.
\]
The displayed initialization in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} gives
\[
\Pr\{S_0=(0,h)\}
=
w_j\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon L^{-j}}{2}
=
d_b(0,h),
\]
where the last equality is the behavior stationary-law formula from Step 2.
The depth mass is nonnegative: since
\[
0<\alpha<1,
\qquad
1-\alpha^{Q+1}>0,
\]
we have
\[
w_j
=
\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^j}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}
\ge0 .
\]
Here \(L^{-j}=(L^j)^{-1}\).  Since \(L>1\), we have \(0<L^{-j}\le1\).
For \(u(h)=-1\),
\[
d_e(0,h)
=
w_j\frac{1-\varepsilon}{2}
\le
w_j\frac{1-\varepsilon L^{-j}}2
=
d_b(0,h)
\le
(1+\varepsilon)d_b(0,h),
\]
where the first inequality uses \(\varepsilon L^{-j}\le\varepsilon\), and the
last uses \(\varepsilon\ge0\) and nonnegativity of the behavior cell.  For
\(u(h)=+1\),
\[
d_e(0,h)
=
w_j\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}
\le
w_j\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}(1+\varepsilon L^{-j})
=
(1+\varepsilon)d_b(0,h),
\]
because \(w_j\ge0\) and \(1+\varepsilon\ge0\), so the multiplier
\(w_j(1+\varepsilon)/2\) is nonnegative, while
\(1+\varepsilon L^{-j}\ge1\).  Multiplying this last inequality by that
nonnegative multiplier gives the displayed inequality, and its right-hand
side is \((1+\varepsilon)d_b(0,h)\) by the formula for \(d_b\).  Every joint state has the unique
observed coordinate \(0\), so these two sign cases give
\[
d_e(s)\le (1+\varepsilon)d_b(s)
\qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathcal S_Q.
\]
Moreover
\[
1+\varepsilon
=
1+\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}
\le C,
\]
because either \(\varepsilon=(C-1)/2\), giving
\(1+\varepsilon=(C+1)/2\le C\), or \(\varepsilon=1/2\), which is possible only
when \(C\ge2\).  Since \(d_b(s)\ge0\),
\[
d_e(s)\le (1+\varepsilon)d_b(s)\le C d_b(s),
\]
so \cref{obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap} holds.

It remains to collect the remaining requirements of \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
The horizon requirement \(1\le T\) is the hypothesis \(T\ge1\) of the present
lemma.  The observed alphabet of \(M_{Q,v}^T\) is the singleton \(\{0\}\), the
latent alphabet is the \(2(Q+1)\)-element set
\(\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\}\), and the action alphabet is \(\{0,1\}\); all
three are finite, and the action alphabet has exactly two elements, as the
class requires.  The parameter requirements \(0<t_0\) and \(0<\zeta\) are
hypotheses of the lemma, and they fix \(L=\exp(\zeta)\) and
\(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\) as the overlap and contraction constants used
throughout.  Together with the seven assumptions verified above -- the Markov
kernel, sequential ignorability, stationary start, bounded rewards, policy
overlap, uniform contraction, and latent stationary overlap -- this is every
defining property of \cref{obj:def:model-class}, and therefore
\(M_{Q,v}^T\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\).
% lean: signedDepth_membership

\item It remains to compute the target value.  The two-point alternative label
enters the reward law only through its numeric sign: the positive label
contributes \(+1\), the negative label contributes \(-1\), and in the statement
this multiplier is denoted by \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\).  Under the target policy the
action is always one.  From the reward weights in
\cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family},
\[
g_e(0,h)
=
\sum_a e(a\mid0)\,E_K[Y_t\mid S_t=(0,h),A_t=a]
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,u(h)\,\mathbf 1\{j(h)=Q\}.
\]
Using \cref{obj:def:target-value} and the already computed target stationary
law,
\[
\theta(K,e)
=
\sum_h
w_{j(h)}
\frac{1+u(h)\varepsilon}{2}\,
v\,c_0(t_0)\,u(h)\,\mathbf 1\{j(h)=Q\}.
\]
Only depth \(Q\) contributes, and summing over the two signs gives
\[
\theta(K,e)
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,w_Q
\left[
\frac{1+\varepsilon}{2}
-
\frac{1-\varepsilon}{2}
\right]
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,\varepsilon\,w_Q .
\]
This is the claimed value formula. % lean: signedDepth_targetValue
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The stationary laws in \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-membership} show how the lower construction keeps the target-over-behavior ratio bounded while concentrating the signal at terminal depth. The terminal value is proportional to the stationary depth mass \(w_Q\), so increasing \(Q\) makes the two alternatives harder to separate from observed data while preserving the fixed latent-overlap radius.

The likelihood calculation rests on finite-prefix posterior bounds, a rare-action second moment, a bootstrap factor that makes the posterior control uniform in the terminal depth, signed-mass identities at terminal depth, exact conditional reward means, and finite-word KL inequalities. The next group develops these ingredients in the order in which the observed-path comparison uses them.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:posterior-lower-window-power}[Posterior window power bound]
Let \(N,Q,k,d\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C,b\in\mathbb R\), let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), and let \(o=((a_0,y_0),\ldots,(a_{k-1},y_{k-1}))\) be an observed prefix for the signed-depth family of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  Write
\[
c_0(t_0)=\frac{1-\alpha(t_0)}{4},
\]
and, for \(0\le m\le k\), write \(o_{\le m}\) for the first \(m\) observed pairs.  For any observed prefix \(o'\), define the raw terminal-depth posterior by
\[
\pi_v(o')
=
\frac{\sum_h \mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\lambda_v(h;o')}
     {\sum_h \lambda_v(h;o')}.
\]
Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Nonnegative amplitude.)} \(0\le c_0(t_0)\).
\item \textbf{(Nonnegative base.)} \(0\le 1-c_0(t_0)b\).
\item \textbf{(Window length.)} \(d\le k\).
\item \textbf{(Strict-prefix posterior bound.)} For every \(i\in\{0,\ldots,k-1\}\),
\[
\pi_v(o_{\le i})\le b.
\]
\end{itemize}
Define the final \(d\)-prefix lower window by
\[
\Lambda_d(o)
=
\prod_{\substack{0\le i<k\\ k-d\le i}}
\left(1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le i})\right).
\]
Then
\[
\bigl(1-c_0(t_0)b\bigr)^d\le \Lambda_d(o).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power}]
We prove the slightly stronger assertion with the prefix length left variable.  For a prefix
\(o\) of length \(n\), write
\[
\Lambda_d^{(n)}(o)
=
\prod_{\substack{0\le i<n\\ n-d\le i}}
\left(1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le i})\right),
\]
with the convention that an empty product is \(1\).

1. If \(d=0\), then the condition \(n\le i<n\) selects no indices, so
\[
\Lambda_0^{(n)}(o)=1=(1-c_0(t_0)b)^0 .
\]
This proves the base case. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.pow_one_sub_le_signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow

2. Assume the claim has been proved for \(d\).  Consider a prefix \(o\) of length \(n\) with
\(d+1\le n\).  If \(n=0\), this inequality is impossible, so there is no case to prove.  Thus write
\(n=k+1\).  Let \(o^{-}\) be the length-\(k\) prefix obtained by dropping the last observed pair:
\[
o^{-}(i)=o(i),\qquad 0\le i<k .
\]
For every \(0\le i<k\), the first \(i\) observations of \(o^{-}\) are exactly the first \(i\)
observations of \(o\):
\[
(o^{-})_{\le i}=o_{\le i}.
\]
Hence the strict-prefix posterior hypothesis for \(o\) gives
\[
\pi_v\bigl((o^{-})_{\le i}\bigr)=\pi_v(o_{\le i})\le b,
\qquad 0\le i<k .
\]
Since \(d+1\le k+1\), we have \(d\le k\), so the induction hypothesis applied to \(o^{-}\) yields
\[
(1-c_0(t_0)b)^d\le \Lambda_d^{(k)}(o^{-}).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.observedPrefixTake_castSucc

3. The final-window product for length \(k+1\) splits into the length-\(k\) final-window product
for \(o^{-}\) and the last factor:
\[
\Lambda_{d+1}^{(k+1)}(o)
=
\Lambda_d^{(k)}(o^{-})
\left(1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le k})\right).
\]
Indeed, the threshold satisfies
\[
(k+1)-(d+1)=k-d,
\]
so the eligible indices before the last one are precisely the eligible indices in the length-\(k\)
window, and the remaining eligible index is \(i=k\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow_succ

4. The posterior hypothesis at the last strict prefix gives \(\pi_v(o_{\le k})\le b\).  Because
\(0\le c_0(t_0)\), multiplying preserves the inequality,
\[
c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le k})\le c_0(t_0)b,
\]
and subtracting from \(1\) reverses the side on which the smaller product appears:
\[
1-c_0(t_0)b\le 1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le k}).
\]
Together with the assumed nonnegativity \(0\le 1-c_0(t_0)b\), we have
\[
0\le (1-c_0(t_0)b)^d .
\]
Combining this nonnegativity, the induction bound, and the last-factor bound gives
\[
(1-c_0(t_0)b)^{d+1}
=
(1-c_0(t_0)b)^d(1-c_0(t_0)b)
\le
\Lambda_d^{(k)}(o^{-})
\left(1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le k})\right)
=
\Lambda_{d+1}^{(k+1)}(o).
\]
This completes the induction, and hence the desired bound for the original prefix of length \(k\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.pow_one_sub_le_signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:terminal-posterior-mature-window}[Terminal posterior window bound]
Let \(N,Q\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\).  Put \(\alpha=\exp\{-(1/t_0)\}\).  In the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, for an observed prefix \(o=((a_1,y_1),\ldots,(a_k,y_k))\), write
\[
\pi_v(o)
=
\frac{
\sum_{h\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\}}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\lambda_v(h;o)
}{
\mathsf M_v(o)
}.
\]
For \(d\in\mathbb N\), define the lower posterior window
\[
\Lambda_d(o)
=
\prod_{\substack{0\le i<k\\ k-d\le i}}
\left(1-c_0(t_0)\,\pi_v(o_{\le i})\right),
\]
where \(o_{\le i}\) is the prefix of \(o\) containing its first \(i\) action--reward pairs.

Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive tilt.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap level.)} \(C>1\).
\item \textbf{(Mature window.)} \(Q\le k\).
\end{itemize}
Then the terminal-depth posterior satisfies
\[
\pi_v(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_Q(o)}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-mature-window}]
Set \(o^-:=o_{\le k-Q}\), and introduce
\[
\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}
:=
\prod_{k-Q\le i<k}
 b(a_{i+1})\,\frac12,
\qquad
\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}:=\mathsf M_v(o^-),
\]
\[
\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}
:=
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}\lambda_v((0,u);o^-),
\qquad
\mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}}
:=
\prod_{k-Q\le i<k}
\frac{
\mathsf M_v(o_{\le i+1})
}{
b(a_{i+1})\,\mathsf M_v(o_{\le i})
},
\]
and
\[
\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}:=\Lambda_Q(o).
\]
Here the products are over the final \(Q\) action--reward pairs; if \(Q=0\), they are empty products and equal one.

1. The terminal-depth numerator after the full prefix factors as
\[
\sum_{h\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\}}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\alpha^Q\,\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\,\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}} .
\]
Indeed, summing over the two terminal signs gives the unsigned mass at depth \(Q\).  Over a mature window of length \(Q\), the only way to arrive at terminal depth \(Q\) is to start the window at depth \(0\) and advance once at each of the \(Q\) updates; this contributes \(\alpha^Q\), while the action and sign-symmetric factors over the same window contribute exactly \(\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\).  The remaining mass is the unsigned depth-zero mass \(\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}\) at the beginning of the window. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.terminalFilterMass_eq_fairWindow_of_le

2. The total mass over the observed prefix factors through the same window as
\[
\mathsf M_v(o)
=
\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\,
\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}\,
\mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}} .
\]
This follows by iterating the one-step mass recursion across the last \(Q\) observations: each step extracts the behavior/fair-sign factor and leaves the corresponding reward-correction factor.  For \(Q=0\) the window is empty and the identity reduces to \(\mathsf M_v(o)=\mathsf M_v(o)\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthTotalMass_eq_windows

3. The unsigned depth-zero mass at the window start is bounded by the total mass there:
\[
\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}
=
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}\lambda_v((0,u);o^-)
\le
\sum_{j=0}^Q\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}\lambda_v((j,u);o^-)
=
\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}} .
\]
All summands are nonnegative, since each prefix weight is a sum of nonnegative path weights. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthUnsignedMass_le_totalMass

4. The lower posterior window is positive and is dominated by the correction window:
\[
0<\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}\le \mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}} .
\]
For each factor in \(\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}\),
\[
0\le \pi_v(o_{\le i})\le 1,
\qquad
0\le c_0(t_0)<1,
\]
so \(1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le i})>0\).  The one-step reward-correction factor at the same prefix is at least
\[
1-c_0(t_0)\pi_v(o_{\le i}),
\]
and taking products over the final window gives the displayed inequality. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow_pos_le_correction

5. The remaining factors have the needed signs:
\[
\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}>0,\qquad
\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}>0,\qquad
\mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}}>0,\qquad
\alpha^Q\ge 0 .
\]
The first inequality is the product of positive behavior weights and \(1/2\) factors, while the second is positivity of the finite total mass at the window start.  The third follows from Step 4 by \(0<\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}\le \mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}}\).  Finally, \(\alpha=\exp\{-(1/t_0)\}>0\), so its \(Q\)-th power is nonnegative. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthFairWindowWeight_pos; CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthTotalMass_pos; CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.mixingAlpha_pos

6. Combining the two window factorizations with the monotonicity bounds gives
\[
\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}
\le
\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}\mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}},
\]
because \(0\le \mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}\le \mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}\), \(0<\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}\le \mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}}\), and \(\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}\ge0\).  Multiplying by the nonnegative factor
\(\alpha^Q\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\) yields
\[
\alpha^Q\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\mathsf U_{\mathrm{pre}}\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}
\le
\alpha^Q\mathsf W_{\mathrm{fair}}\mathsf M_{\mathrm{pre}}\mathsf R_{\mathrm{corr}} .
\]
Using the identities from Steps 1 and 2, this is
\[
\left(
\sum_h \mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\lambda_v(h;o)
\right)\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}
\le
\alpha^Q\,\mathsf M_v(o).
\]
Since \(\mathsf M_v(o)>0\) and \(\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}>0\), division gives
\[
\pi_v(o)
=
\frac{
\sum_h \mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\lambda_v(h;o)
}{
\mathsf M_v(o)
}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\mathsf P_{\mathrm{low}}}
=
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_Q(o)} ,
\]
as claimed. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_product_of_le
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:terminal-posterior-initial-window}[Initial-window posterior bound]
Let \(N,Q\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), let \(k\in\mathbb N\), and let
\[
o:\{0,\ldots,k-1\}\to \{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}
\]
be an observed action/reward prefix for the signed-depth construction of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  Write \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\).  For any prefix \(o'\), define the raw terminal-depth posterior by
\[
\pi_v(o')
=
\frac{\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o')}
     {\sum_h \lambda_v(h;o')}.
\]
Define the initial lower posterior window over the available prefixes by
\[
\Lambda_k(o)
=
\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}
\left(1-c_0\,\pi_v(o_{\le i})\right),
\]
where \(o_{\le i}\) is the prefix of \(o\) of length \(i\).

Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive overlap exponent.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Nontrivial overlap radius.)} \(C>1\).
\item \textbf{(Initial window.)} \(k<Q\).
\end{itemize}
Then the terminal-depth posterior after observing \(o\) satisfies
\[
\pi_v(o)\le \frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_k(o)}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-initial-window}]
Write \(o_i=(a_i,y_i)\) for \(0\le i<k\), and let \(o_{\le 0}\) denote the empty prefix.  For any prefix \(\omega\), set
\[
\mathsf{Mass}_v(\omega):=\sum_h \lambda_v(h;\omega),
\qquad
\mathsf{Term}_v(\omega):=\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;\omega).
\]
Let \(b_\zeta(a)\) be the behavior-policy probability of action \(a\) from \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, and define
\[
\mathsf{Fair}_k(o)
:=
\prod_{i=0}^{k-1} b_\zeta(a_i)\,\frac12 .
\]
For a prefix \(\omega\) and a next observation \((a,y)\), define the reward-correction factor
\[
\Gamma_v(\omega,(a,y))
:=
\frac{
  2\sum_h \lambda_v(h;\omega)\,
  \frac{1+(2y-1)v c_0 U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}}{2}
}{
  \mathsf{Mass}_v(\omega)
},
\]
whenever \(\mathsf{Mass}_v(\omega)>0\), with any fixed value when the denominator is zero.  Along the observed prefixes below the denominator is positive.  Finally set
\[
\mathsf{Corr}_k(o):=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\Gamma_v(o_{\le i},o_i),
\qquad
\mathsf{Low}_k(o):=\Lambda_k(o).
\]

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(k<Q\), a hidden path contributing to \(\mathsf{Term}_v(o)\) starts, after summing over the sign coordinate, from depth \(Q-k\) and then makes \(k\) successive depth-increment moves.  Thus the terminal numerator factors as
\[
\mathsf{Term}_v(o)
=
\alpha^k w_{Q-k}\,\mathsf{Fair}_k(o),
\]
where
\[
w_j=\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^j}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}},
\qquad 0\le j\le Q,
\]
is the signed-depth initial depth mass from \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  Since \(k<Q\),
\[
\alpha^k w_{Q-k}
=
\alpha^k\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^{Q-k}}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}
=
\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^Q}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}
=
w_Q,
\]
and therefore
\[
\mathsf{Term}_v(o)=w_Q\,\mathsf{Fair}_k(o).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.terminalFilterMass_eq_fairWindow_of_lt

\item Summing the one-step kernel over the next hidden state leaves total hidden-transition mass one.  Hence each extension satisfies
\[
\mathsf{Mass}_v(o_{\le i+1})
=
b_\zeta(a_i)\,\frac12\,
\Gamma_v(o_{\le i},o_i)\,
\mathsf{Mass}_v(o_{\le i}).
\]
Multiplying this identity for \(i=0,\ldots,k-1\), with the empty-product convention when \(k=0\), gives
\[
\mathsf{Mass}_v(o)
=
\mathsf{Fair}_k(o)\,
\mathsf{Mass}_v(o_{\le 0})\,
\mathsf{Corr}_k(o).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthTotalMass_eq_windows

\item The empty-prefix mass is one.  Indeed, the initial weights sum to
\[
\sum_{j=0}^Q
w_j
\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C L^{-j}}2
=
\sum_{j=0}^Q w_j
=
1.
\]
Thus
\[
\mathsf{Mass}_v(o_{\le0})=1.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthTotalMass_of_length_eq_zero

\item The lower window is positive and is bounded by the correction window:
\[
0<\mathsf{Low}_k(o),
\qquad
\mathsf{Low}_k(o)\le \mathsf{Corr}_k(o).
\]
To see this, \(t_0>0\) gives \(0<\alpha<1\), hence
\[
0\le c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}<1.
\]
For each observed prefix \(o_{\le i}\), the filter weights are nonnegative and their total mass is positive, so
\[
0\le \pi_v(o_{\le i})\le 1.
\]
Consequently every factor \(1-c_0\pi_v(o_{\le i})\) is positive.  Moreover, for each \(i\),
\[
\Gamma_v(o_{\le i},o_i)
\ge
(1-\pi_v(o_{\le i}))\cdot 1
+
\pi_v(o_{\le i})(1-c_0)
=
1-c_0\pi_v(o_{\le i}),
\]
because the doubled reward factor equals \(1\) off terminal depth and is at least \(1-c_0\) on terminal depth.  Multiplying these pointwise inequalities gives the displayed product bound.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow_pos_le_correction

\item The fair-window factor is strictly positive:
\[
0<\mathsf{Fair}_k(o).
\]
Each behavior probability \(b_\zeta(a_i)\) is positive under \(\zeta>0\), and each reward-symbol base factor \(1/2\) is positive; the empty product is \(1\).
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthFairWindowWeight_pos

\item The terminal depth mass is bounded by \(\alpha^Q\):
\[
w_Q\le \alpha^Q.
\]
Indeed,
\[
w_Q=\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^Q}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}},
\]
and \(0<\alpha<1\) implies \(1-\alpha^{Q+1}>0\) and \(\alpha^{Q+1}\le \alpha\).  After multiplying by the positive denominator, the desired inequality is
\[
(1-\alpha)\alpha^Q
\le
(1-\alpha^{Q+1})\alpha^Q,
\]
which is exactly \(\alpha^{Q+1}\le\alpha\) times the nonnegative factor \(\alpha^Q\).
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.depthMass_terminal_le_pow

\item Combining the previous bounds,
\[
w_Q\,\mathsf{Low}_k(o)
\le
\alpha^Q\,\mathsf{Corr}_k(o).
\]
Multiplying by the nonnegative factor \(\mathsf{Fair}_k(o)\), and using the identities above, yields
\[
\mathsf{Term}_v(o)\,\mathsf{Low}_k(o)
\le
\alpha^Q\,\mathsf{Mass}_v(o).
\]
Since \(\mathsf{Mass}_v(o)>0\) and \(\mathsf{Low}_k(o)>0\), division gives
\[
\pi_v(o)
=
\frac{\mathsf{Term}_v(o)}{\mathsf{Mass}_v(o)}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\mathsf{Low}_k(o)}
=
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_k(o)}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_product_of_lt
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:terminal-posterior-crude-bound}[Crude terminal posterior bound]
Let \(N,Q,k\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\). Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive temperature.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive log-overlap.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap level.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
For the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, write
\[
\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0),
\qquad
c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}.
\]
For every observed prefix \(o\in(\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\})^k\), define the raw terminal-depth posterior by
\[
\pi_v(o)
=
\frac{
\sum_h \mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\,\lambda_v(h;o)
}{
\mathsf M_v(o)
},
\qquad
\mathsf M_v(o)=\sum_h \lambda_v(h;o),
\]
where the sums range over the signed-depth hidden states at depth horizon \(Q\). Then
\[
\pi_v(o)
\le
\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-crude-bound}]
Write \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\) and \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\).

1. Since \(t_0>0\), one has \(0<\alpha<1\). Hence
\[
0\le c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}<1,
\qquad
0<1-c_0,
\qquad
0\le 1-c_0\cdot 1 .
\]
For every observed prefix \(o'\), the raw terminal-depth posterior satisfies
\[
0\le \pi_v(o')\le 1.
\]
Indeed, the numerator is a sum of nonnegative filter masses over terminal-depth states, the denominator \(\mathsf M_v(o')\) is positive, and the terminal-depth mass is bounded by the total mass. % lean: signedDepthRawPosterior_mem_Icc

2. First suppose \(Q\le k\). By \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-mature-window},
\[
\pi_v(o)\le \frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_Q(o)} .
\]
Apply \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power} with \(b=1\) and \(d=Q\). The hypotheses are exactly those checked above: \(0\le c_0\), \(0\le 1-c_0\cdot 1\), \(Q\le k\), and \(\pi_v(o_{\le i})\le 1\) for every strict prefix. Thus
\[
(1-c_0)^Q\le \Lambda_Q(o).
\]
Since \(\alpha^Q\ge 0\) and \(1-c_0>0\), division by the larger positive denominator gives
\[
\pi_v(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{(1-c_0)^Q}
=
\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q .
\]
% lean: signedDepthRawPosterior_le_ratio_pow

3. It remains to consider \(k<Q\). By \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-initial-window},
\[
\pi_v(o)\le \frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_k(o)} .
\]
Apply \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power} with \(b=1\) and \(d=k\). Since \(k\le k\), and the prefix posterior bound \(\pi_v(o_{\le i})\le 1\) holds for every strict prefix, this gives
\[
(1-c_0)^k\le \Lambda_k(o).
\]
Therefore
\[
\pi_v(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{(1-c_0)^k}.
\]
Because \(0<1-c_0\le 1\) and \(k<Q\), monotonicity of powers on \([0,1]\) yields
\[
(1-c_0)^Q\le (1-c_0)^k .
\]
Dividing the nonnegative numerator \(\alpha^Q\) by these positive denominators gives
\[
\frac{\alpha^Q}{(1-c_0)^k}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{(1-c_0)^Q}
=
\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q .
\]
Combining the displayed inequalities proves the claimed bound in the initial-window case as well. % lean: signedDepthRawPosterior_le_ratio_pow
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:refined-terminal-posterior}[Refined terminal posterior bound]
Let \(N,Q,k\) be nonnegative integers. Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive perturbation.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap level.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
Write \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\) for the contraction parameter and
\(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\) for the signed-depth reward-amplitude constant. For an
alternative \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\) in the signed-depth family of
\cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} and any observed prefix
\(o=(a_1,y_1,\ldots,a_k,y_k)\), let \(\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)\) be the raw
terminal-depth posterior: the run-local unnormalized filter mass at latent
depth \(Q\), divided by the total run-local unnormalized filter mass of the
prefix. Then
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}
{\left\{1-c_0\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q\right\}^Q}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:refined-terminal-posterior}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Let
\[
\Gamma:=\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}.
\]
Since \(t_0>0\), the contraction parameter satisfies \(0<\alpha<1\).  Hence
\[
0\le c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}\le \frac14,
\qquad
1-c_0>0,
\]
and therefore \(\Gamma\ge0\).  Moreover,
\[
\Gamma<1
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\alpha<1-\frac{1-\alpha}{4}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\alpha<1.
\]
Thus \(\Gamma^Q\le1\).  Consequently
\[
B_Q:=1-c_0\Gamma^Q
\]
is strictly positive; indeed \(c_0\Gamma^Q\le c_0\le1/4\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_refined

\item
For \(0\le d\le k\), define the lower posterior window
\[
\Lambda_d(o)
:=
\prod_{s=k-d+1}^{k}
\left(1-c_0\,\pi_{N,Q,v}(o_{\le s-1})\right),
\]
where \(o_{\le m}\) denotes the first \(m\) observed action--reward pairs, with \(o_{\le0}\) the empty prefix; for \(d=0\) this is the empty product and equals \(1\).  If \(Q\le k\), \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-mature-window} applies under the present hypotheses and gives
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_Q(o)}.
\]
If \(k<Q\), \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-initial-window} applies under the present hypotheses and gives
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_k(o)}.
\]
These two estimates are exactly the two window cases needed below. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_product_of_le; CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_product_of_lt

\item
The crude terminal-posterior estimate in \cref{obj:lem:terminal-posterior-crude-bound} applies to every observed prefix \(o'\), of any length, under the same assumptions \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), and \(C>1\).  With the present notation it states
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o')
\le
\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q
=
\Gamma^Q.
\]
This supplies the prefix-uniform bootstrap bound used in the lower-window estimate. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_ratio_pow

\item
Apply \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power} with
\[
b:=\Gamma^Q.
\]
Its nonnegative-amplitude hypothesis is \(0\le c_0\), established in the first step, and its nonnegative-base hypothesis is
\[
0\le 1-c_0\Gamma^Q=B_Q,
\]
also established there.  Its strict-prefix posterior hypothesis holds because the preceding step gives, for every prefix appearing in the window,
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o_{\le s-1})\le \Gamma^Q.
\]
The indexing in \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power} ranges over the final \(d\) strict prefixes of \(o\), precisely the factors in \(\Lambda_d(o)\).  Hence, for every \(d\le k\),
\[
B_Q^d
=
\left(1-c_0\Gamma^Q\right)^d
\le
\Lambda_d(o).
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.pow_one_sub_le_signedDepthPosteriorLowerWindow

\item
If \(Q\le k\), the terminal-window bound with \(d=Q\) and the preceding display give
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_Q(o)}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{B_Q^Q}.
\]
If \(k<Q\), the terminal-window bound with \(d=k\) gives
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{\Lambda_k(o)}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{B_Q^k}.
\]
Since \(0<B_Q\le1\) and \(k<Q\), we have \(B_Q^Q\le B_Q^k\), hence
\[
\frac{\alpha^Q}{B_Q^k}
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}{B_Q^Q}.
\]
In both cases,
\[
\pi_{N,Q,v}(o)
\le
\frac{\alpha^Q}
{\left(1-c_0\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}\right)^Q\right)^Q},
\]
which is the claimed bound. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthRawPosterior_le_refined
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The posterior bounds in \cref{obj:lem:posterior-lower-window-power,obj:lem:terminal-posterior-mature-window,obj:lem:terminal-posterior-initial-window,obj:lem:terminal-posterior-crude-bound,obj:lem:refined-terminal-posterior} separate the survival of the terminal depth from the normalization accumulated along the observed prefix. The mature-window and initial-window cases cover prefixes on the two sides of the terminal depth \(Q\), the crude bound supplies a uniform starting point, and the refined statement packages these ingredients into the terminal-posterior control used below.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:rare-action-second-moment-exact}[Rare-action second moment]
Let \(Q,k\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\). Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Time scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap exponent.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Radius constant.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
Write \(L=\exp(\zeta)\), let \(\ell=\min\{Q,k\}\), and consider the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} at horizon \(k+1\). For an action/reward-symbol prefix
\[
o=((a_1,y_1),\ldots,(a_k,y_k)),
\]
let \(\pi_k^v(o)\) be its prefix mass under this model, and define the rare-action-history factor from \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities} by
\[
\mathfrak a_k(o)
=
L^{-(Q-\ell)}
\prod_{i:\,k-\ell<i\le k}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\},
\]
with the empty product equal to one. Then
\[
\sum_{o}\pi_k^v(o)\,\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}
\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{|\{i:\ k-\ell<i\le k\}|},
\]
where the sum ranges over all action/reward-symbol prefixes of length \(k\).
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:rare-action-second-moment-exact}]
Let
\[
S:=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}: k-\ell<i\le k\}.
\]
Reindex each observed prefix \(o\) by its action sequence \(a=(a_1,\ldots,a_k)\) and reward-symbol sequence \(y=(y_1,\ldots,y_k)\). This bijection gives
\[
\sum_o \pi_k^v(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
\sum_a\sum_y
\pi_k^v(a,y)
\left(
L^{-(Q-\ell)}
\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}
\right)^2 .
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.obsPrefixActionRewardEquiv

For each action sequence \(a\), the product
\[
I_S(a):=\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}
\]
is either \(0\) or \(1\). Hence \(I_S(a)^2=I_S(a)\), including the empty-product case \(S=\varnothing\), where \(I_S(a)=1\). Therefore
\[
\sum_o \pi_k^v(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}
\sum_a I_S(a)\sum_y \pi_k^v(a,y).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.rareAction_secondMoment_eq

After summing over the reward symbols with the actions fixed, the observed prefix mass factors as the behavior action probability product:
\[
\sum_y \pi_k^v(a,y)
=
\prod_{i=1}^k b(a_i),
\qquad
b(1)=\frac1L,\quad b(0)=1-\frac1L .
\]
This is the reward-summed prefix identity for the signed-depth family of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}. Thus
\[
\sum_o \pi_k^v(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}
\sum_a
\left(\prod_{i=1}^k b(a_i)\right)
\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}\right).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_rewards_prefixMass_eq_behaviorProduct

It remains to evaluate the action sum. Since the behavior action law is product-form,
\[
\sum_a
\left(\prod_{i=1}^k b(a_i)\right)
\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}\right)
=
\prod_{i=1}^k
\sum_{a_i\in\{0,1\}}
b(a_i)\,
\begin{cases}
\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\},& i\in S,\\
1,& i\notin S.
\end{cases}
\]
For \(i\in S\), the inner sum is \(b(1)=1/L\); for \(i\notin S\), it is \(b(0)+b(1)=1\). Hence
\[
\sum_a
\left(\prod_{i=1}^k b(a_i)\right)
\left(\prod_{i\in S}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}\right)
=
\left(\frac1L\right)^{|S|}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_behaviorProduct_mul_trueIndicator

Combining the preceding displays and recalling
\(S=\{i:k-\ell<i\le k\}\) gives
\[
\sum_o \pi_k^v(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}
\left(\frac1L\right)^{|\{i:\ k-\ell<i\le k\}|},
\]
as claimed.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.rareAction_secondMoment_eq
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:rare-action-second-moment-exact} gives the exact second moment of the visible rare-action factor. The displayed expression isolates the contribution of the forced terminal-depth action pattern, and this is the \(L^{-Q}\) term that enters the observed KL calculation after summing conditional mean differences.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:finite-signed-depth-kl}[Finite observed KL bound]
Let \(T,Q\in\mathbb N\) and \(t_0,\zeta,C,K_0\in\mathbb R\).  Write \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\), \(L=e^\zeta\), \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\), and \(\varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\).  Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive scale.)} \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), \(C>1\), and \(K_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Uniform terminal posterior.)} For every horizon \(N\), every alternative index \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), every time \(t<N\), and every observed prefix \(o\) of actions and two-symbol rewards before \(t\), the finite conditional terminal-depth probability \(q_t^v(o)\) from \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities} satisfies
\[
q_t^v(o)\le K_0\alpha^Q .
\]
\end{itemize}
Then the finite observed-word laws of the signed-depth alternatives of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, taken before the reward symbols are decoded as real rewards, satisfy
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},T}\middle\|\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},T}\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}\,L^{-Q}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:finite-signed-depth-kl}]
Let
\[
D:=\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}.
\]
Since \(t_0>0\), \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\) lies in \((0,1)\), and hence
\[
0<c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}<1,\qquad 0\le 1-c_0^2,\qquad D\ge 0 .
\]
Also \(\varepsilon_C^2\ge 0\), \(\alpha^{2Q}\ge 0\), and \(L^{-Q}\ge 0\).  We prove the claimed bound by induction on the horizon \(T\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_finiteObs_klDiv_le

\emph{Step 1. The zero-horizon case.}
For \(T=0\), the observed word has a single possible value.  The finite-word chain rule gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},0}\middle\|
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},0}\right)=0,
\]
so the desired inequality is immediate. % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.InformationTheory.FiniteWordChainRule.klDiv_eq_chainKL

\emph{Step 2. Full support and projectivity.}
Fix \(k\ge 0\) and assume the bound at horizon \(k\).  Every observed word
\[
w=((x_1,a_1,y_1),\ldots,(x_{k+1},a_{k+1},y_{k+1}))
\]
has positive mass under each signed-depth alternative.  Indeed, the observed coordinate has only one value, and one may choose a hidden trajectory with the displayed observed actions and reward symbols.  The initial weights are positive because \(0<\alpha<1\) and \(0<\varepsilon_C\le 1/2\); the behavior weights are positive because \(L=\exp(\zeta)>1\); and the selected hidden transitions have positive reset or continuation weight, with the case \(Q=0\) handled directly by the reset transition.  Thus
\[
\mathbb P_{Q,v}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}(w)>0,
\qquad v\in\{-1,+1\}.
\]
Moreover, summing the chronological law over the last observed action-reward symbol gives the prefix law:
\[
\operatorname{pref}_k\mathbb P_{Q,v}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}
=
\mathbb P_{Q,v}^{\mathrm{fin},k}.
\]
Consequently the last-coordinate chain rule applies and yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}
 \middle\|\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}\right)  \\
&\quad =
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k}
 \middle\|\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},k}\right)
+
\sum_{w}
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k}(w)\,
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\widetilde P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\middle\|\widetilde P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\right),
\end{aligned}
\]
where \(\widetilde P_{v,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\) is the conditional law of the next full observed symbol \((x,a,r)\in\{0\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\) after prefix \(w\). % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.InformationTheory.FiniteWordChainRule.klDiv_lastCoordinate_eq_add_sum_of_fullSupport

\emph{Step 3. The one-step increment.}
For a prefix \(w\) of length \(k\), write \(o(w)=((a_1,y_1),\ldots,(a_k,y_k))\) for its action/reward-symbol projection and define
\[
\mathfrak a_k(o)
:=
L^{-(Q-\min\{Q,k\})}
\prod_{i:\,k-\min\{Q,k\}<i\le k}\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}.
\]
For \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), let \(P_{v,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}\) be the image of \(\widetilde P_{v,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\) under the bijection
\[
\{0\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\longrightarrow \{0,1\}\times\{0,1\},
\qquad
(0,a,r)\longmapsto(a,r).
\]
The KL divergence is unchanged by this relabelling, so
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\widetilde P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\middle\|\widetilde P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\right)
=
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}\middle\|P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}\right).
\]
For \(a\in\{0,1\}\) and reward symbol \(r\in\{0,1\}\), write \(\bar y(r)=-1\) if \(r=0\) and \(\bar y(r)=1\) if \(r=1\).  Let
\[
m_v(w):=
\mathbb E_v[Y_{k+1}\mid\mathcal F_k^{\mathrm{obs}}=o(w)]
\]
with the conditional ratio evaluated on the finite prefix mass; full support from Step 2 makes this ordinary conditional expectation on the present prefix.  Summing the chronological law over all hidden states at time \(k+1\) gives, for every \((a,r)\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
P_{v,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}(a,r)
&=
\frac{
\Pr_v\{\mathcal F_k^{\mathrm{obs}}=o(w),\ A_{k+1}=a,\ Y_{k+1}=\bar y(r)\}}
{\Pr_v\{\mathcal F_k^{\mathrm{obs}}=o(w)\}}  \\
&=
 b(a)\,\Pr_v\{Y_{k+1}=\bar y(r)\mid\mathcal F_k^{\mathrm{obs}}=o(w)\} \\
&=
 b(a)\,\frac{1+\bar y(r)m_v(w)}{2},
\end{aligned}
\]
where \(b(1)=L^{-1}\) and \(b(0)=1-L^{-1}\).  The second equality uses the behavior policy in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, which chooses the next action with mass \(b(a)\) independently of the alternative after the observed prefix.  The last equality is the two-symbol reward law determined by its mean: for a variable taking values \(\{-1,+1\}\), mass \((1+m)/2\) is assigned to \(+1\) and mass \((1-m)/2\) to \(-1\).  The positivity assumptions give \(|m_v(w)|<1\), so all displayed masses are positive.

By \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-conditional-reward-mean}, these means are
\[
m_+(w)=c_0\varepsilon_C\mathfrak a_k(o(w))q_{k+1}^{+1}(o(w)),
\qquad
m_-(w)=-c_0\varepsilon_C\mathfrak a_k(o(w))q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w)).
\]
Using the preceding mass formula and writing out the finite KL sum,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}\middle\|P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{ar}}\right)
&=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{r\in\{0,1\}}
 b(a)\frac{1+\bar y(r)m_+(w)}{2}
\log\frac{b(a)(1+\bar y(r)m_+(w))/2}{b(a)(1+\bar y(r)m_-(w))/2} \\
&=
\sum_{r\in\{0,1\}}
\frac{1+\bar y(r)m_+(w)}{2}
\log\frac{1+\bar y(r)m_+(w)}{1+\bar y(r)m_-(w)}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(\sum_a b(a)=1\), and the positive common factor \(b(a)\) cancels inside the logarithm.  The standard two-point comparison, valid for \(|m_+(w)|<1\) and \(|m_-(w)|<1\), yields
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\widetilde P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\middle\|\widetilde P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\right)
\le
\frac{(m_+(w)-m_-(w))^2}{1-m_-(w)^2}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_observedNext_klDiv_le

\emph{Step 4. The conditional chi-square calibration.}
Set
\[
d(w):=c_0\varepsilon_C\mathfrak a_k(o(w)).
\]
The signed-depth factors are nonnegative: \(c_0>0\), \(\varepsilon_C\ge 0\), and \(\mathfrak a_k(o(w))\ge 0\).  The action factor also obeys
\[
0\le \mathfrak a_k(o(w))\le 1,
\qquad
\varepsilon_C\mathfrak a_k(o(w))\le 1.
\]
Hence
\[
0\le d(w)
\quad\text{and}\quad
 d(w)^2
=c_0^2\bigl(\varepsilon_C\mathfrak a_k(o(w))\bigr)^2
\le c_0^2.
\]
The terminal posteriors are probabilities, so
\[
0\le q_{k+1}^{v}(o(w))\le 1,
\qquad v\in\{-1,+1\}.
\]
Together with the assumed posterior bound,
\[
q_{k+1}^{v}(o(w))\le K_0\alpha^Q,
\qquad v\in\{-1,+1\},
\]
this gives
\[
q_{k+1}^{+1}(o(w))+q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w))
\le 2K_0\alpha^Q.
\]
Substituting the conditional-mean identities from Step 3,
\[
\begin{aligned}
(m_+(w)-m_-(w))^2
&=d(w)^2\bigl(q_{k+1}^{+1}(o(w))+q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w))\bigr)^2  \\
&=c_0^2\varepsilon_C^2\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2
\bigl(q_{k+1}^{+1}(o(w))+q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w))\bigr)^2 \\
&\le
4c_0^2K_0^2\varepsilon_C^2\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2\alpha^{2Q}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The denominator is compared using both \(d(w)^2\le c_0^2\) and \(q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w))^2\le 1\):
\[
\begin{aligned}
1-m_-(w)^2
&=1-d(w)^2q_{k+1}^{-1}(o(w))^2 \\
&\ge 1-c_0^2.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(0<c_0<1\), the lower bound \(1-c_0^2\) is positive.  Applying the preceding numerator and denominator comparisons to the one-step KL bound from Step 3 gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\widetilde P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\middle\|\widetilde P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\right)
&\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2\varepsilon_C^2\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2\alpha^{2Q}}{1-c_0^2} \\
&=
D\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2 .
\end{aligned}
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_conditional_chiSq_le

\emph{Step 5. Averaging over prefixes.}
For an action/reward-symbol prefix \(o\) of length \(k\), define its finite prefix mass using the horizon \(k+1\) model by
\[
\pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o)
:=
\Pr_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}\!
\bigl((A_1,Y_1),\ldots,(A_k,Y_k)=o\bigr).
\]
For every observed word \(w\) of length \(k\), the observed state coordinate is fixed, and the mass of \(w\) under the horizon-\(k\) observed law is
\[
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k}(w)
=
\pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o(w)).
\]
Thus the average increment is bounded by
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sum_{w}
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k}(w)\,
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\widetilde P_{+,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\middle\|\widetilde P_{-,w}^{\mathrm{next}}\right)\\
&\quad\le
D\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}
\sum_{w}
\pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o(w))\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2 .
\end{aligned}
\]
The map \(w\mapsto o(w)\) is a bijection from length-\(k\) observed words to length-\(k\) action/reward-symbol prefixes, because the observed state coordinate has the unique value.  Reindexing the finite sum gives
\[
\sum_{w}
\pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o(w))\mathfrak a_k(o(w))^2
=
\sum_o \pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2 .
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:rare-action-second-moment-exact}, with \(\ell=\min\{Q,k\}\),
\[
\sum_o \pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}
\left(\frac{1}{L}\right)^{|\{i:\ k-\ell<i\le k\}|}.
\]
The index set \(\{i:\ k-\ell<i\le k\}\) contains exactly the final \(\ell\) positions of the length-\(k\) prefix; if \(\ell=0\) it is empty, and otherwise the shift \(j\mapsto k-\ell+j\), \(1\le j\le\ell\), is a bijection.  Therefore
\[
\sum_o \pi_{k,k+1}^{+1}(o)\mathfrak a_k(o)^2
=
L^{-2(Q-\ell)}L^{-\ell}
=
L^{-2Q+\ell}
\le L^{-Q},
\]
because \(\ell\le Q\) and \(L\ge 1\).  Hence the increment is bounded by
\[
D\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.rareAction_secondMoment_le

\emph{Step 6. Closing the induction.}
Using the induction hypothesis and the increment bound,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}
 \middle\|\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},k+1}\right)
&\le
D\,k\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}
+
D\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q} \\
&=
D\,(k+1)\,\varepsilon_C^2\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Substituting the definition of \(D\) gives the asserted bound for \(T=k+1\), completing the induction. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_finiteObs_klDiv_le
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-bootstrap-factor}[Uniform bootstrap factor]
Let \(t_0>0\) and write \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\), \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\), \(\eta_0=\alpha/(1-c_0)\). Then there exists \(K_0>0\) such that
\[
\frac{1}{\bigl(1-c_0\bigl(\alpha/(1-c_0)\bigr)^{Q}\bigr)^{Q}}
\le
K_0
\qquad(Q\in\mathbb N).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-bootstrap-factor}]
Let
\[
        \alpha=e^{-1/t_0},\qquad c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4},\qquad
        \eta_0=\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}.
\]
We prove the stated uniform bound by constructing the constant explicitly.

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(t_0>0\), one has \(0<\alpha<1\). Hence
\[
        0\le c_0=\frac{1-\alpha}{4}\le \frac14,
        \qquad
        1-c_0=\frac{3+\alpha}{4}>0,
\]
and therefore
\[
        0\le \eta_0=\frac{\alpha}{1-c_0}<1,
\]
because \(\alpha<1-c_0\) is equivalent to \(\alpha<(3+\alpha)/4\), or \(\alpha<1\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.exists_uniform_signedDepth_bootstrap_factor

\item The sequence \(Q\eta_0^Q\) is bounded above. To see this in the same normalization, set
\[
        \bar\eta=\frac{1+\eta_0}{2}.
\]
Then \(0\le \bar\eta<1\) and \(|\eta_0|<\bar\eta\). Writing
\[
        Q\eta_0^Q=\bigl(Q(\eta_0/\bar\eta)^Q\bigr)\bar\eta^Q,
\]
the first factor tends to \(0\) by the ratio test, since
\[
        \frac{(Q+1)(\eta_0/\bar\eta)^{Q+1}}
             {Q(\eta_0/\bar\eta)^Q}
        =\Bigl(1+\frac1Q\Bigr)\frac{\eta_0}{\bar\eta}
        \longrightarrow \frac{\eta_0}{\bar\eta}<1,
\]
while \(\bar\eta^Q\to0\). Thus \(Q\eta_0^Q\to0\), and in particular there is a finite \(M_\star\ge0\) such that
\[
        Q\eta_0^Q\le M_\star\qquad(Q\in\mathbb N).
\]
 % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.exists_uniform_signedDepth_bootstrap_factor

\item Define
\[
        K_0=\exp(2c_0M_\star).
\]
Then \(K_0>0\). Fix \(Q\in\mathbb N\) and put
\[
        x_Q=c_0\eta_0^Q.
\]
From \(0\le c_0\le1/4\) and \(0\le\eta_0<1\), we have
\[
        0\le x_Q\le \frac14,
        \qquad
        1-x_Q>0.
\]
For every \(x\in[0,1/4]\),
\[
        (1-x)^{-1}\le 1+2x\le e^{2x}.
\]
Indeed, the first inequality follows after multiplication by \(1-x>0\), since
\[
        (1+2x)(1-x)=1+x-2x^2\ge1,
\]
and the second is the standard bound \(1+y\le e^y\) with \(y=2x\). Applying this with \(x=x_Q\) and raising to the \(Q\)-th power gives
\[
        \bigl((1-x_Q)^{-1}\bigr)^Q
        \le \bigl(e^{2x_Q}\bigr)^Q
        =e^{2Qx_Q}.
\]
 % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.exists_uniform_signedDepth_bootstrap_factor

\item Finally,
\[
        2Qx_Q=2c_0\,Q\eta_0^Q\le 2c_0M_\star,
\]
so monotonicity of the exponential yields
\[
        e^{2Qx_Q}\le e^{2c_0M_\star}=K_0.
\]
Combining the preceding displays,
\[
\frac{1}{\bigl(1-c_0\eta_0^Q\bigr)^Q}
=
\bigl((1-x_Q)^{-1}\bigr)^Q
\le
K_0.
\]
Since \(Q\in\mathbb N\) was arbitrary, this \(K_0\) proves the claim. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.exists_uniform_signedDepth_bootstrap_factor
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-terminal-sign-invariant}[Terminal signed mass invariant]
Let \(N,Q,k\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), and consider the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}.  For an observed prefix \(o=((a_i,y_i))_{i=1}^k\), write \(\lambda_v(h;o)\) for the unnormalized filter mass of terminal hidden state \(h\), write \(J(h)\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\) and \(U(h)\in\{-1,+1\}\) for its depth and sign coordinates, and set
\[
\varepsilon_C=\min\left\{\frac{C-1}{2},\frac12\right\}.
\]
Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive initial scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive overlap exponent.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Nontrivial overlap radius.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
With \(L=e^\zeta\), define the depth-\(Q\) action-history factor
\[
a^{(Q)}(o)
=
L^{-\max\{Q-k,0\}}
\prod_{\substack{1\le i\le k\\ i>k-\min\{Q,k\}}}
\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}.
\]
Then
\[
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q} U(h)\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\varepsilon_C\,a^{(Q)}(o)
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-sign-invariant}]
Put \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\) and \(L=e^\zeta\).  For a prefix
\(\omega=((\tilde a_i,\tilde y_i))_{i=1}^m\) and a depth
\(j\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\), define
\[
\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega)
:=
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=j}\lambda_v(h;\omega),
\qquad
\operatorname{Sg}_j(\omega)
:=
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=j}U(h)\lambda_v(h;\omega),
\]
and
\[
A_j(\omega)
:=
L^{-(j-\min\{j,m\})}
\prod_{i=m-\min\{j,m\}+1}^{m}\mathbf 1\{\tilde a_i=1\},
\]
with the empty product equal to one.  Thus \(A_Q(o)=a^{(Q)}(o)\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item We first prove the depthwise invariant
\[
\operatorname{Sg}_j(\omega)
=
\varepsilon_C A_j(\omega)\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega)
\qquad
\text{for every }m,\ \omega,\ \text{and }j\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}.
\]
For \(m=0\), the initial mass at depth \(j\) and sign \(u\in\{-1,+1\}\) is
\[
w_j\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C L^{-j}}{2}.
\]
Summing over \(u=+1\) and \(u=-1\) gives
\[
\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega)=w_j,
\qquad
\operatorname{Sg}_j(\omega)
=
w_j\varepsilon_C L^{-j}
=
\varepsilon_C A_j(\omega)\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega),
\]
since \(A_j(\omega)=L^{-j}\) when the prefix is empty.

\item Suppose the invariant holds for every prefix of length \(m\), and let
\(\omega^+=((\tilde a_i,\tilde y_i))_{i=1}^{m+1}\).  Write \(\omega\) for its first \(m\) observations and \(a_+=\tilde a_{m+1}\).  At reset depth \(0\), direct summation of the two reset signs in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} gives, for each previous hidden state \(h\),
\[
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
u'\,p\{J'=0,U'=u'\mid h,a_+\}
=
\varepsilon_C
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
p\{J'=0,U'=u'\mid h,a_+\}.
\]
After multiplying by the previous unnormalized filter mass, the behavior-policy weight, and the reward-emission weight, and then summing over \(h\), this yields
\[
\operatorname{Sg}_0(\omega^+)
=
\varepsilon_C\operatorname{Un}_0(\omega^+).
\]
Because \(A_0(\omega^+)=1\), the invariant holds at depth \(0\).

\item For a positive target depth, write it as \(j+1\) with \(j<Q\).  The last observation of \(\omega^+\) is \((a_+,\tilde y_{m+1})\), and the preceding prefix \(\omega\) is the restriction of \(\omega^+\) to its first \(m\) coordinates.  Let \(\Gamma_{a_+}((\delta,u),(\delta',u'))\) be the hidden-transition weight from \((\delta,u)\) to \((\delta',u')\) under action \(a_+\), for \(\delta,\delta'\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\) and \(u,u'\in\{-1,+1\}\).  The raw filter recursion gives
\[
\operatorname{Un}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
b(a_+)\sum_{\delta=0}^Q\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
\lambda_v((\delta,u);\omega)
\frac{1+\tilde y_{m+1}vc_0u\mathbf 1\{\delta=Q\}}{2}
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
\Gamma_{a_+}((\delta,u),(j+1,u')),
\]
where \(b(a_+)\) is the behavior-policy probability of the observed action and \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\).  By the transition weights in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}, reaching the positive depth \(j+1\) is possible exactly by advancing from depth \(j\), and
\[
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
\Gamma_{a_+}((\delta,u),(j+1,u'))
=
\alpha\mathbf 1\{\delta=j\}.
\]
Because \(j<Q\), the reward factor on the surviving terms is \(1/2\).  Hence
\[
\operatorname{Un}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
b(a_+)\,\alpha\,\frac12\,
\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega).
\]

\item With the same notation, the signed advancing aggregate is obtained by inserting the new sign in the inner sum:
\[
\operatorname{Sg}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
b(a_+)\sum_{\delta=0}^Q\sum_{u\in\{-1,+1\}}
\lambda_v((\delta,u);\omega)
\frac{1+\tilde y_{m+1}vc_0u\mathbf 1\{\delta=Q\}}{2}
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
{u'}\,\Gamma_{a_+}((\delta,u),(j+1,u')) .
\]
Again only \(\delta=j\) survives.  For that depth, the transition weights in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} give
\[
\sum_{u'\in\{-1,+1\}}
{u'}\,\Gamma_{a_+}((\delta,u),(j+1,u'))
=
\alpha\mathbf 1\{\delta=j\}\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\}u.
\]
Indeed, action \(1\) preserves the old sign on an advancing transition, while action \(0\) assigns equal total advancing weight to the two possible new signs.  Using once more that the reward factor is \(1/2\) at depth \(j<Q\), we obtain
\[
\operatorname{Sg}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
b(a_+)\,\alpha\,\frac12\,
\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\}\operatorname{Sg}_j(\omega).
\]

\item The action-history factor has the corresponding one-step recursion.  From the definition of \(A_j\),
\[
A_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
L^{-((j+1)-\min\{j+1,m+1\})}
\prod_{i=m+2-\min\{j+1,m+1\}}^{m+1}
\mathbf 1\{\tilde a_i=1\}.
\]
The identities
\[
\min\{j+1,m+1\}=\min\{j,m\}+1,
\qquad
(j+1)-\min\{j+1,m+1\}=j-\min\{j,m\},
\]
and
\[
m+2-\min\{j+1,m+1\}=m+1-\min\{j,m\}
\]
show that the displayed product is the product defining \(A_j(\omega)\), followed by the last factor \(\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\}\).  Thus
\[
A_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
A_j(\omega)\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\},
\]
with the same empty-product convention as before.

\item Applying the induction hypothesis at depth \(j\) to the preceding prefix \(\omega\) gives
\[
\operatorname{Sg}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
b(a_+)\,\alpha\,\frac12\,
\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\}\,
\varepsilon_C A_j(\omega)\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega).
\]
The unsigned recursion and the action-factor recursion give
\[
\varepsilon_C A_{j+1}(\omega^+)
\operatorname{Un}_{j+1}(\omega^+)
=
\varepsilon_C A_j(\omega)\mathbf 1\{a_+=1\}\,
 b(a_+)\,\alpha\,\frac12\operatorname{Un}_j(\omega),
\]
which is the same quantity.  Equivalently, splitting on the last action, when \(a_+=1\) the common factor is \(b(a_+)\alpha/2\) times the induction hypothesis, and when \(a_+=0\) both sides are zero.  This completes the induction.

\item Finally take \(j=Q\) and \(\omega=o\).  Reindexing the hidden-state sum by its depth and sign coordinates gives
\[
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o)=\operatorname{Un}_Q(o),
\qquad
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}U(h)\lambda_v(h;o)=\operatorname{Sg}_Q(o).
\]
The depthwise invariant at \(j=Q\), together with \(A_Q(o)=a^{(Q)}(o)\), therefore gives
\[
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q} U(h)\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\varepsilon_C\,a^{(Q)}(o)
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o),
\]
as claimed.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-next-reward-numerator}[Next reward numerator]
Let \(U,k,N,Q\in\mathbb N\), let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), and consider the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} with horizon \(N\), terminal depth \(Q\), initial weights \(p_v\), behavior policy \(b\), and reward-symbol/hidden-state kernel \(K_v\). Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive time scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive behavior perturbation.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap radius.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
Write
\[
c_0(t_0)=\frac{1-\alpha}{4},
\]
where \(\alpha\) is the signed-depth mixing parameter, and write \(\phi(0)=-1\), \(\phi(1)=1\).  For any observed action/reward-symbol prefix \(o\in(\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\})^k\), let
\[
\lambda_v(h;o)
\]
denote the unnormalized signed-depth filter mass, after the prefix \(o\), assigned to observed state \(0\) and packed hidden state \(h\in\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}\).  Decode the packed hidden state by
\[
J(h)=\bigl\lfloor h/2\bigr\rfloor,
\qquad
U(h)=
\begin{cases}
+1, & h \equiv 1 \pmod 2,\\
-1, & h \equiv 0 \pmod 2.
\end{cases}
\]
Then
\[
\sum_{\tau}
\mathbf 1\!\left\{ ((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k(\tau)=o\right\}
\,\omega_v(\tau)\,\phi\!\left(r_{k+1}(\tau)\right)
=
v\,c_0(t_0)
\sum_{h=0}^{2(Q+1)-1}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\,U(h)\,\lambda_v(h;o),
\]
where the outer sum is over all finite signed-depth trajectories \(\tau\) of horizon \(U+k+1\), and \(\omega_v(\tau)\) is their chronological path weight under \(p_v\), \(b\), and \(K_v\).
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-next-reward-numerator}]
1.  Let \(\Sigma_k\) be the set of joint-state prefixes
\[
\sigma=(\sigma_0,\ldots,\sigma_k),
\qquad
\sigma_i=(0,h_i),\quad h_i\in\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}.
\]
For such a prefix, write \(W_o(\sigma)\) for the chronological weight of realizing the observed prefix \(o\) and ending with hidden state \(h_k\).  Summing first over all continuations beyond the observed prefix gives
\[
\sum_{\tau}
\mathbf 1\{((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k(\tau)=o\}
\omega_v(\tau)\phi(r_{k+1}(\tau))
=
\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_k}
W_o(\sigma)
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} b(a)
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}}\phi(y)
\sum_{s'} K_v((0,h_k),a)(y,s').
\]
Indeed, partition trajectories by their first \(k\) observed action--reward symbols and by the joint-state prefix through time \(k\).  The chronological product factors into \(W_o(\sigma)\), the next behavior probability, and the next kernel probability; summing over the future hidden state \(s'\) and then over \(a,y\) gives the displayed identity. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_finitePathWeightFrom_prefix_nextPayoff

2.  For every packed hidden state \(h\), the signed-depth reward kernel in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} satisfies
\[
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} b(a)
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}}\phi(y)
\sum_{s'}K_v((0,h),a)(y,s')
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,U(h)\,\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}.
\]
To see this, use the product form of the kernel: the reward-symbol weight depends on \(h\) and \(v\), while the hidden-transition weights sum to one for each current state and action.  Hence the left-hand side reduces to
\[
\Bigl(\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} b(a)\Bigr)
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}}\phi(y)\,
\frac{1+\phi(y)\,v\,c_0(t_0)\,U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}}{2}.
\]
The behavior probabilities sum to one, and since \(\phi(0)=-1\), \(\phi(1)=1\),
\[
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}}\phi(y)\frac{1}{2}=0,
\qquad
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}}\phi(y)^2\frac{v\,c_0(t_0)\,U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}}{2}
=
v\,c_0(t_0)\,U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}.
\]
This proves the displayed one-step payoff identity. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_oneStepRewardPayoff

3.  Substituting the one-step identity into the prefix decomposition gives
\[
\sum_{\tau}
\mathbf 1\{((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k(\tau)=o\}
\omega_v(\tau)\phi(r_{k+1}(\tau))
=
v\,c_0(t_0)
\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_k}
W_o(\sigma)\,U(h_k)\mathbf 1\{J(h_k)=Q\}.
\]
The observed state alphabet has one element, so each terminal joint state is uniquely of the form \((0,h_k)\); this is the only bookkeeping hidden in the notation above. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_prefix_nextRewardNumerator

4.  By definition of the unnormalized filter mass,
\[
\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_k:\,h_k=h} W_o(\sigma).
\]
Therefore
\[
\sum_{h=0}^{2(Q+1)-1}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\,U(h)\,\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_k}
W_o(\sigma)\,U(h_k)\mathbf 1\{J(h_k)=Q\}.
\]
This is just regrouping the preceding prefix sum by its terminal packed hidden state. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_terminalSignedFilterMass_eq_sum_fixedPrefix

5.  Combining the last two displays yields
\[
\sum_{\tau}
\mathbf 1\{((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k(\tau)=o\}
\,\omega_v(\tau)\,\phi(r_{k+1}(\tau))
=
v\,c_0(t_0)
\sum_{h=0}^{2(Q+1)-1}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\,U(h)\,\lambda_v(h;o),
\]
as claimed. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_prefix_nextRewardNumerator
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-terminal-prefix-mass}[Terminal prefix mass identity]
Let \(U,k,Q\in\mathbb N\) with \(U\ge 1\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), let \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), and consider the finite signed-depth model of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} at horizon \(U+k\).  For an action/reward-symbol prefix \(o\) of length \(k\), define the joint prefix-and-terminal-depth mass at the cut after the first \(k\) epochs by
\[
m_v^Q(o)
=
\Pr_v\{(A_1,R_1),\ldots,(A_k,R_k)=o,\ J_{k+1}=Q\}.
\]
For each hidden coordinate \(h\in\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}\), let \(J(h)=\lfloor h/2\rfloor\), and let \(\lambda_v(h;o)\) be the unnormalized filter mass, as in \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities}, assigned after observing \(o\) to the cut state with hidden coordinate \(h\).  Then
\[
m_v^Q(o)
=
\sum_{h=0}^{2(Q+1)-1}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\,\lambda_v(h;o).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-prefix-mass}]
1.  Let \(\Gamma_{k+1}\) denote the set of joint-state prefixes
\[
\gamma=(S_1,\ldots,S_{k+1}),
\qquad
S_i\in \{0\}\times\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}.
\]
For such a prefix, write \(W_v(\gamma;o)\) for the real chronological weight of the prefix whose observed action/reward word is \(o\). Summing the full trajectory law over all continuations after the cut gives
\[
m_v^Q(o)
=
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{k+1}}
\mathbf 1\{J(S_{k+1}^{\mathrm{hid}})=Q\}\,
W_v(\gamma;o).
\]
This is exactly the fixed-prefix expansion of the terminal-depth event at the cut after \(k\) observed epochs; the hypothesis \(U\ge 1\) ensures that this cut is a valid time in the horizon \(U+k\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.terminalPrefixMass_eq_finiteFixedPrefixWeight_sum

2.  For a joint state \(s\in \{0\}\times\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}\), define
\[
\Lambda_v(s;o)
:=
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{k+1}}
\mathbf 1\{S_{k+1}=s\}\,W_v(\gamma;o).
\]
Then
\[
\sum_{s}
\mathbf 1\{J(s^{\mathrm{hid}})=Q\}\Lambda_v(s;o)
=
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{k+1}}
\mathbf 1\{J(S_{k+1}^{\mathrm{hid}})=Q\}W_v(\gamma;o).
\]
Indeed, interchange the two finite sums. For each fixed prefix \(\gamma\), the inner sum over \(s\) has exactly one nonzero contribution, namely \(s=S_{k+1}\), and therefore returns the displayed indicator and weight. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sum_finiteTerminalStatePrefixMass_indicator

3.  Combining the preceding two displays yields
\[
m_v^Q(o)
=
\sum_{s\in \{0\}\times\{0,\ldots,2(Q+1)-1\}}
\mathbf 1\{J(s^{\mathrm{hid}})=Q\}\Lambda_v(s;o).
\]
The observed-state coordinate has the singleton value \(0\), so this sum is
\[
m_v^Q(o)
=
\sum_{h=0}^{2(Q+1)-1}
\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}\Lambda_v((0,h);o).
\]
For the signed-depth filter at the cut after the word \(o\), the unnormalized mass assigned to hidden coordinate \(h\) is the terminal-state prefix mass at the joint state \((0,h)\):
\[
\lambda_v(h;o)
:=
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{k+1}}
\mathbf 1\{S_{k+1}=(0,h)\}\,W_v(\gamma;o).
\]
Thus \(\lambda_v(h;o)=\Lambda_v((0,h);o)\) for every \(h\), and the claimed identity follows. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepthFilterMass
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:signed-depth-conditional-reward-mean}[Conditional reward mean identity]
Let \(T,Q\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(v\) be a Boolean alternative, written through
\[
\sigma(v)=
\begin{cases}
1, & v=\mathrm{true},\\
-1, & v=\mathrm{false}.
\end{cases}
\]
Assume
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Positive baseline.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Positive policy scale.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap radius.)} \(C>1\).
\end{itemize}
For every epoch \(t<T\) and every observed prefix \(o\in\{\,\mathrm{false},\mathrm{true}\,\}^{t}\times\{0,1\}^{t}\), the conditional next-reward mean satisfies
\[
\mathbb E_v[Y_t\mid \mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}=o]
=
\sigma(v)\,c_0(t_0)\,\varepsilon_C(C)\,
a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,q_t^v(o),
\]
where the left-hand side is the totalized ratio defining the finite-prefix conditional reward mean, \(c_0(t_0)=(1-\alpha)/4\), \(\varepsilon_C(C)=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\), and \(a_t^{(Q)}(o)\) and \(q_t^v(o)\) are the rare-action-history factor and terminal posterior of \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities}.
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-conditional-reward-mean}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Write the epoch as a finite ordinal \(n_{\mathrm{pre}}<T\), and set
\[
n_{\mathrm{tail}}:=T-(n_{\mathrm{pre}}+1).
\]
Then \(T=n_{\mathrm{tail}}+(n_{\mathrm{pre}}+1)\). Thus it is enough to prove the identity at the canonical cut after an observed prefix of length \(n_{\mathrm{pre}}\), with at least the next epoch present. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.conditionalRewardMean_signedDepth_all

\item
Fix such a canonical horizon and write the observed prefix as
\(o=((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k\), where \(a_i\in\{0,1\}\), \(r_i\in\{0,1\}\), and the real reward map is
\[
\phi(0)=-1,
\qquad
\phi(1)=1.
\]
Let \(\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}\) be the finite trajectory space with horizon \(n_{\mathrm{tail}}+(k+1)\). Its elements record the initial hidden state, the behavior actions, reward symbols, and subsequent hidden states through the remaining epochs of the finite signed-depth weights displayed below. For every \(Q\in\mathbb N\), the hidden coordinates are \((J,U)\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\times\{-1,+1\}\). When \(Q\ge 1\), these weights are the signed-depth construction of \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}; when \(Q=0\), the same formulas are read on the one-point depth set \(\{0\}\), so only the terminal-depth reset line can occur. For \(\tau\in\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}\), define
\[
\operatorname{Obs}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\tau)
:=((a_1(\tau),r_1(\tau)),\ldots,(a_k(\tau),r_k(\tau))),
\qquad
r_{\mathrm{next}}(\tau):=r_{k+1}(\tau),
\]
and let \(\omega_v(\tau)\) be the product of the signed-depth initial hidden-state weight, the behavior action probabilities, and the reward-symbol/hidden-transition weights along \(\tau\). Thus, if \(h_0,\ldots,h_{n_{\mathrm{tail}}+k+1}\) are the hidden states in \(\tau\),
\[
\omega_v(\tau)
=
\mu_v(h_0)
\prod_{i=1}^{n_{\mathrm{tail}}+k+1}
\pi_\zeta(a_i(\tau))\,
\mathfrak m_v(r_i(\tau),h_i\mid h_{i-1},a_i(\tau)),
\]
where \(\mu_v\) is the initial hidden-state law with weights
\[
\mu_v(j,u)
=
\frac{(1-\alpha)\alpha^j}{1-\alpha^{Q+1}}\,
\frac{1+u\varepsilon_C(C)L^{-j}}{2},
\qquad 0\le j\le Q,
\quad u\in\{-1,+1\},
\]
\(\pi_\zeta(1)=L^{-1}\), \(\pi_\zeta(0)=1-L^{-1}\), and \(\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)\) is the following one-step reward-symbol and next-hidden-state weight. If \(h=(j,u)\), \(h'=(j',u')\), and \(\phi(0)=-1\), \(\phi(1)=1\), then
\[
\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)
=
\frac{1+\phi(r)\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)u\mathbf 1\{j=Q\}}{2}
\,\kappa(h'\mid h,a),
\]
where \(\kappa(0,u'\mid Q,u,a)=(1+u'\varepsilon_C(C))/2\); for \(j<Q\),
\[
\kappa(0,u'\mid j,u,a)=(1-\alpha)\frac{1+u'\varepsilon_C(C)}{2},
\]
and
\[
\kappa(j+1,u'\mid j,u,1)=\alpha\mathbf 1\{u'=u\},
\qquad
\kappa(j+1,u'\mid j,u,0)=\frac{\alpha}{2},
\]
with all other hidden transitions assigned weight zero. These formulas are valid for all \(Q\in\mathbb N\); if \(Q=0\), only the reset line \(j=Q\) occurs. Summing the two reward weights gives one, and the displayed hidden-transition weights sum to one for each hidden state and action. Hence
\[
\sum_{r\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{h'}
\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)=1
\qquad
\text{for every hidden state }h\text{ and action }a.
\]
The numerator of the totalized finite-prefix conditional reward mean is
\[
N_v(o):=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}}
\mathbf 1\{\operatorname{Obs}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\tau)=o\}\,
\phi(r_{\mathrm{next}}(\tau))\,\omega_v(\tau).
\]
For a hidden prefix \(\eta=(h_0,\ldots,h_k)\), define its observed-prefix weight by
\[
\Omega_v(\eta;o)
:=
\mu_v(h_0)
\prod_{i=1}^{k}
\pi_\zeta(a_i)\,
\mathfrak m_v(r_i,h_i\mid h_{i-1},a_i),
\]
with value \(\mu_v(h_0)\) when \(k=0\). The unnormalized filter mass at the cut is
\[
\lambda_v(h;o)
:=
\sum_{\eta:\,h_k=h}\Omega_v(\eta;o),
\qquad
\Lambda_v(o):=\sum_h \lambda_v(h;o),
\qquad
\Lambda_{v,Q}(o):=\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o),
\]
where \(J(h)\in\{0,\ldots,Q\}\) and \(U(h)\in\{-1,+1\}\) are the depth and sign coordinates of the hidden state \(h\).

We next decompose the finite trajectories at the cut. For a hidden state \(h\) and integer \(m\ge0\), set
\[
\Xi_m(h)
:=
\sum_{(a_1,r_1,h_1),\ldots,(a_m,r_m,h_m)}
\prod_{j=1}^{m}
\pi_\zeta(a_j)\,
\mathfrak m_v(r_j,h_j\mid h_{j-1},a_j),
\qquad h_0=h,
\]
with \(\Xi_0(h)=1\). Since \(\sum_a\pi_\zeta(a)=1\) and the displayed normalization of \(\mathfrak m_v\) holds at each state-action pair, induction gives
\[
\Xi_m(h)=1
\qquad
\text{for every }m\ge0\text{ and every }h.
\]
Splitting \(N_v(o)\) according to the hidden prefix \(\eta\), the next action \(a\), the next reward symbol \(r\), and the next hidden state \(h'\), then summing the remaining suffix by \(\Xi_{n_{\mathrm{tail}}}(h')=1\), yields
\[
N_v(o)
=
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}\pi_\zeta(a)
\sum_{r\in\{0,1\}}\phi(r)
\sum_{h'}\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h_k,a).
\]
This is a partition of the same finite set of trajectories: the data \((\eta,a,r,h')\) give the first \(k+1\) epochs, and the suffix sum runs over all chronological continuations from \(h'\).

For fixed \(h\) and \(a\), the displayed one-step formula factors \(\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)\) into the reward weight and a hidden-transition probability. Hence
\[
\sum_{h'}\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)
=
\frac{1+\phi(r)\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}}{2}.
\]
Using \(\sum_a\pi_\zeta(a)=1\), \(\sum_r\phi(r)/2=0\), and \(\sum_r\phi(r)^2/2=1\), the inner one-step payoff is
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{a}\pi_\zeta(a)
\sum_{r}\phi(r)
\sum_{h'}\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h,a)
&=
\sum_a\pi_\zeta(a)
\sum_r \phi(r)
\frac{1+\phi(r)\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}}{2} \\
&=
\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)U(h)\mathbf 1\{J(h)=Q\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Therefore
\[
\begin{aligned}
N_v(o)
&=
\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)U(h_k)\mathbf 1\{J(h_k)=Q\} \\
&=
\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}U(h)\lambda_v(h;o).
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the numerator identity supplied by \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-next-reward-numerator}; the displayed derivation records the finite-trajectory regrouping used at the canonical cut. The denominator and the terminal-depth quotient are identified below.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_prefix_nextRewardNumerator
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.conditionalRewardMean_signedDepth

\item
\Cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-sign-invariant} applies under \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), and \(C>1\), and gives the prefix-level identity
\[
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}U(h)\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\varepsilon_C(C)\,a_{\mathrm{pre}}^{(Q)}(o)
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o),
\]
where, for the length-\(k\) prefix \(o=((a_i,r_i))_{i=1}^k\),
\[
a_{\mathrm{pre}}^{(Q)}(o)
=
L^{-\max\{Q-k,0\}}
\prod_{\substack{1\le i\le k\\ i>k-\min\{Q,k\}}}
\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}.
\]
At the canonical cut, the time-indexed factor of \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities} is the same quantity:
\[
a_t^{(Q)}(o)
=
L^{-(Q-\min\{Q,k\})}
\prod_{\substack{1\le i\le k\\ i>k-\min\{Q,k\}}}
\mathbf 1\{a_i=1\}
=
a_{\mathrm{pre}}^{(Q)}(o),
\]
since \(Q-\min\{Q,k\}=\max\{Q-k,0\}\). Thus
\[
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}U(h)\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\varepsilon_C(C)\,a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,\Lambda_{v,Q}(o).
\]
The empty-prefix case is included by the empty-product convention in \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities}. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_terminalMass_invariant

\item
It remains to identify the denominator and terminal-depth numerator. For a trajectory \(\tau\in\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}\), let \(h_{\mathrm{cut}}(\tau):=h_k(\tau)\) be the hidden state after the first \(k\) observed epochs. In the one-based indexing used for the signed-depth path statement, this same cut state is \(H_{k+1}\); hence the event \(J(h_{\mathrm{cut}}(\tau))=Q\) is exactly the event written there as \(J_{k+1}=Q\). Define the finite prefix mass and the terminal-depth prefix mass by
\[
\Pi_v(o)
:=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}}
\mathbf 1\{\operatorname{Obs}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\tau)=o\}\,\omega_v(\tau),
\]
and
\[
\Pi_{v,Q}(o)
:=
\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal T^+_{n_{\mathrm{tail}},k}}
\mathbf 1\{\operatorname{Obs}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\tau)=o\}\,
\mathbf 1\{J(h_{\mathrm{cut}}(\tau))=Q\}\,\omega_v(\tau).
\]
The same cut decomposition as above, now without weighting by the next reward, gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Pi_v(o)
&=
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)
\sum_{(a,r,h')}\pi_\zeta(a)\,
\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h_k,a)\,\Xi_{n_{\mathrm{tail}}}(h') \\
&=
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)
=
\sum_h\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\Lambda_v(o).
\end{aligned}
\]
Indeed, the first sum partitions trajectories by their length-\(k\) hidden prefix, next action, next reward symbol, and next hidden state; the continuation has total mass \(\Xi_{n_{\mathrm{tail}}}(h')=1\), and the next one-step mass sums to one. Carrying the terminal-depth indicator at the cut through the same partition gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Pi_{v,Q}(o)
&=
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)\mathbf 1\{J(h_k)=Q\}
\sum_{(a,r,h')}\pi_\zeta(a)\,
\mathfrak m_v(r,h'\mid h_k,a)\,\Xi_{n_{\mathrm{tail}}}(h') \\
&=
\sum_{\eta}\Omega_v(\eta;o)\mathbf 1\{J(h_k)=Q\} \\
&=
\sum_{h:\,J(h)=Q}\lambda_v(h;o)
=
\Lambda_{v,Q}(o).
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus \(\Pi_v(o)\) is the mass of the observed-prefix event and \(\Pi_{v,Q}(o)\) is the mass of the same event together with terminal depth at the cut. The latter identity is exactly the terminal-prefix mass identification in \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-prefix-mass} for the canonical horizon.

The finite-prefix conditional reward mean is the totalized quotient
\[
\mathbb E_v[Y_t\mid \mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}=o]
=
\frac{N_v(o)}{\Pi_v(o)},
\]
where real division is totalized by \(x/0=0\). The terminal posterior in \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities} is read at this finite prefix through the same totalized convention:
\[
q_t^v(o)=\frac{\Pi_{v,Q}(o)}{\Pi_v(o)}.
\]
If \(\Pi_v(o)=0\), then \(\Pi_{v,Q}(o)=0\) because the terminal-depth prefix event is contained in the prefix event; for \(\Pi_v(o)>0\), these quotients are the usual conditional averages. Combining the numerator identity, \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-sign-invariant}, and \(\Pi_{v,Q}(o)=\Lambda_{v,Q}(o)\), we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb E_v[Y_t\mid \mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}=o]
&=
\frac{\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)\varepsilon_C(C)\,
a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,\Pi_{v,Q}(o)}{\Pi_v(o)} \\
&=
\sigma(v)c_0(t_0)\varepsilon_C(C)\,
a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,q_t^v(o).
\end{aligned}
\]
This is the claimed identity for the original \(T\) and \(t<T\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.terminalPrefixMass_eq_sum_signedDepthFilterMass
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.conditionalRewardMean_signedDepth_all
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-bootstrap-factor} turns the finite-prefix normalization in \cref{obj:lem:refined-terminal-posterior} into a constant depending on the mixing scale. \Cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-sign-invariant,obj:lem:signed-depth-next-reward-numerator,obj:lem:signed-depth-terminal-prefix-mass} connect the signed terminal mass, the next reward numerator, and the prefix mass at terminal depth. These identities convert the latent terminal sign into the observable conditional reward mean in \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-conditional-reward-mean}, while \cref{obj:lem:finite-signed-depth-kl} converts the resulting terminal-posterior control into a finite observed-word divergence bound. The product \(\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}\) reflects the joint requirement that terminal depth survives and that the visible action history carries the rare sequence needed to reveal it.

The likelihood calculation next fixes the observed signed-depth law and combines the exact observed reward mean with a bound on the divergence between the two signs.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_18}[Observed signed-depth law]
For \(T,Q\in\mathbb N\), \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and two-point alternative index \(v\), define
\[
\mathbb P_{Q,v}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\]
to be the observed-path law obtained by applying the observed-coordinate projection to the trajectory law of the embedded finite signed-depth model with horizon \(T\), parameters \((t_0,\zeta,C,Q)\), and alternative index \(v\).
\end{definitionv}

The law in \cref{obj:synth_18} is the distribution used in the two-point information comparison. It records the observable image of the embedded signed-depth experiment, so the divergence calculation is stated on the same data observed by an estimator.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:observed-path-kl}[Observed-path KL bound]
Fix \(t_0>0\), and write \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\). There exists a constant \(K_0>0\), depending on \(t_0\), such that whenever the following conditions hold:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Overlap exponent.)} \(\zeta>0\), and \(L=\exp(\zeta)\).
\item \textbf{(Reset amplitude.)} \(C>1\), and \(\varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\).
\item \textbf{(Sequential ignorability.)} For every horizon \(T\), terminal depth \(Q\ge 1\), and alternative \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), the signed-depth experiment in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} satisfies \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}.
\item \textbf{(Stationary start.)} For every horizon \(T\), terminal depth \(Q\ge 1\), and alternative \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), the signed-depth experiment in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} satisfies \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start}.
\end{itemize}
there exists a constant \(B_0>0\), depending on \(t_0,\zeta,C\), such that for every \(T\) and every \(Q\ge 1\), with \(\mathbb P_{Q,v}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\) denoting the observed-path law of the signed-depth alternative \(v\),
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\,\middle\|\,\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\right)
\le
B_0\,T\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\]
Moreover, for each \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), each \(t<T\), and each observed prefix \(o\in\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), the conditional reward mean satisfies the exact finite-prefix identity
\[
\mathbb E_v[Y_t\mid \mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}=o]
=
v\,c_0\,\varepsilon_C\,a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,q_t^v(o),
\]
where \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\), and \(a_t^{(Q)}\) and \(q_t^v\) are the rare-action-history factor and terminal-depth posterior from \cref{obj:def:filter-quantities}. The posterior satisfies
\[
q_t^v(o)\le K_0\,\alpha^Q .
\]
The same observed-path laws also satisfy
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\,\middle\|\,\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:observed-path-kl}]
Fix \(t_0>0\), put \(\alpha=\exp(-1/t_0)\), and set \(c_0=(1-\alpha)/4\).

1.  By \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-bootstrap-factor}, there is a constant \(K_0>0\), depending only on \(t_0\), such that for every \(Q\in\mathbb N\),
\[
\frac{1}{\{1-c_0(\alpha/(1-c_0))^Q\}^Q}\le K_0 .
\]
Fix \(\zeta>0\), \(C>1\), and the two stated model-class hypotheses. Let
\[
B_0:=\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}.
\]
Since \(0<\alpha<1\), we have \(0<c_0<1\), hence \(1-c_0^2>0\), and therefore \(B_0>0\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.observed_path_kl

2.  Fix \(T\in\mathbb N\) and \(Q\ge1\). The refined posterior estimate in \cref{obj:lem:refined-terminal-posterior}, combined with the choice of \(K_0\) in step 1, gives the uniform terminal-depth posterior bound
\[
q_t^v(o)\le K_0\alpha^Q
\]
for every auxiliary horizon \(N\), every \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), every \(t<N\), and every observed prefix \(o\in\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}\). In particular, for the present horizon \(T\),
\[
q_t^v(o)\le K_0\alpha^Q,
\qquad
v\in\{-1,+1\},\ t<T,\ o\in\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}} .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.terminalPosterior_le_of_bootstrap_factor

3.  Applying \cref{obj:lem:finite-signed-depth-kl} with the posterior bound from step 2 yields the finite observed-word inequality
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{fin},T}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{fin},T}
\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\]
The observed-path law is obtained from the finite observed-word law by the deterministic reward decoding map in \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family}. The data-processing inequality for this embedding gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\]
Equivalently, by the definition of \(B_0\),
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
B_0\,T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.signedDepth_finiteObs_klDiv_le

4.  Since \(C>1\), the reset amplitude satisfies
\[
0\le \varepsilon_C=\min\{(C-1)/2,1/2\}\le 1 .
\]
Also \(L=\exp(\zeta)>0\), so \(L^{-Q}\ge0\), and \(\alpha^{2Q}\ge0\). Hence
\[
