B_0\,T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}
\le
B_0\,T\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\]
Monotonicity of \(x\mapsto \operatorname{ofReal}(x)\) on this real inequality gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
B_0\,T\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.observed_path_kl

5.  Finally, \cref{obj:lem:signed-depth-conditional-reward-mean} supplies, for each \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), each \(t<T\), and each observed prefix \(o\in\mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), the exact identity
\[
\mathbb E_v[Y_t\mid \mathcal F_{t-1}^{\mathrm{obs}}=o]
=
v\,c_0\,\varepsilon_C\,a_t^{(Q)}(o)\,q_t^v(o).
\]
Together with the posterior bound in step 2 and the sharper KL estimate from step 3, this gives all three asserted conclusions:
\[
q_t^v(o)\le K_0\alpha^Q
\]
and
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0^2K_0^2}{1-c_0^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha^{2Q}L^{-Q}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.conditionalRewardMean_signedDepth_all
\end{proof}

The identity in \cref{obj:lem:observed-path-kl} gives the exact observed conditional mean in terms of a rare visible action run and a terminal-depth posterior. The KL bound then combines the survival factor \(\alpha^{2Q}\) with the policy-overlap factor \(L^{-Q}\), matching the fixed-overlap exponent used in the minimax comparison. This is the point at which latent stationarity and observed histories enter the lower-bound calculation together.

The upper-bound argument also uses the stationary mean of each PHIW score. With the observed law already fixed in \cref{obj:synth_15}, the next statement bridges the observable score to the target transition operator that controls the truncation term.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:phiw-score-stationary-mean}[Stationary PHIW score mean]
Let \(T,n_X,n_H,k\in\mathbb N\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M\) be a raw POMDP experiment. Suppose:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Model class.)} \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
\item \textbf{(Mature time.)} \(t\in\{0,\ldots,T-1\}\) and \(k\le t\).
\end{itemize}
Then the depth-\(k\) score \(Z_t(k)\) of \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, evaluated under the observed trajectory law of \(M\), satisfies
\[
\int Z_t(k)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\sum_s d_b(s)\,(P_e^k g_e)(s).
\]
Here \(d_b\) is the stationary law selected for the behavior-policy transition matrix
\[
P_b(s,s')=\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} b(a\mid x_s)\,K(s,a)\{(r,s''):\ s''=s'\},
\]
and \(P_e^k g_e\) is the \(k\)-fold iterate of the target-policy transition operator applied to the target-policy reward regression
\[
g_e(s)=\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} e(a\mid x_s)\int r\,K(s,a)(d r,d s').
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:phiw-score-stationary-mean}]
Write \(S_j\) for the joint state at zero-based epoch \(j\), on the same time scale as \(Z_t(k)\). For a policy \(p\in\{b,e\}\) and a function \(\varphi\) on joint states, set
\[
(P_p\varphi)(s)
:=
\sum_{s'} P_p(s,s')\varphi(s'),
\qquad
P_p(s,s')
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}p(a\mid x_s)\,
K(s,a)\{(y,s''):\ s''=s'\}.
\]
Also write the observable ratio, with its zero-cell convention, as
\[
\rho_j
=
\rho(X_j,A_j)
:=
\begin{cases}
e(A_j\mid X_j)/b(A_j\mid X_j),& b(A_j\mid X_j)>0,\\
0,& b(A_j\mid X_j)=0.
\end{cases}
\]
By \cref{obj:def:model-class}, the model supplies \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel,obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:stationary-start,obj:ass:bounded-reward,obj:ass:policy-overlap}, with \(L=\exp(\zeta)\). Since \(0<\zeta\), \(1\le L\).

Let
\[
\iota:=t-k .
\]
The hypothesis \(k\le t\) makes \(\iota\) a valid epoch, and \(\iota+k=t<T\). We first compute the mean of the score from the state at the beginning of its ratio window:
\[
\int Z_t(k)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\int (P_e^k g_e)(S_\iota)\,d\mathbb P_M .
\]
Indeed, the score is a function of the observed trajectory, so its observed-law integral is the corresponding full-trajectory integral,
\[
\int Z_t(k)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\int
Y_t\prod_{j=\iota}^{t}\rho_j
\,d\mathbb P_M .
\]
For any bounded \(\varphi\) and any epoch \(j<T-1\), \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:pomdp-kernel,obj:ass:policy-overlap} give
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[\rho_j\,\varphi(S_{j+1})\mid S_j=s\right]
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} e(a\mid x_s)
\int \varphi(s')\,K(s,a)(dy,ds')
=
(P_e\varphi)(s).
\]
The same conditional calculation at the terminal reward gives
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[\rho_t\,Y_t\mid S_t=s\right]
=
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}} e(a\mid x_s)
\int y\,K(s,a)(dy,ds')
=
g_e(s).
\]
The zero-cell convention is compatible with the displayed equalities because \(b(a\mid x_s)=0\) and \(e(a\mid x_s)\le Lb(a\mid x_s)\) imply \(e(a\mid x_s)=0\). Iterating the preceding identities backward from \(t\) to \(\iota\), with boundedness supplied by \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward} and finite state spaces from \cref{obj:def:model-class}, yields the displayed score identity.

It remains to identify the law of \(S_\iota\). For every function \(\varphi\) on joint states,
\[
\int \varphi(S_\iota)\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\sum_s d_b(s)\varphi(s).
\]
At the initial epoch this is \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start}. If the identity holds at epoch \(j\), then \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} give
\[
\int \varphi(S_{j+1})\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\int (P_b\varphi)(S_j)\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\sum_s d_b(s)\sum_{s'}P_b(s,s')\varphi(s').
\]
The selected behavior stationary law is stationary for \(P_b\), so for every \(s'\),
\[
\sum_s d_b(s)P_b(s,s')=d_b(s'),
\]
and therefore
\[
\int \varphi(S_{j+1})\,d\mathbb P_M
=
\sum_{s'}d_b(s')\varphi(s').
\]
Induction gives the claim at \(\iota\).

Applying the last identity with \(\varphi=P_e^k g_e\) and substituting into the score identity gives
\[
\int Z_t(k)\,d\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\sum_s d_b(s)\,(P_e^k g_e)(s),
\]
which is the asserted equality.
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:phiw-score-stationary-mean} states the exact stationary target of each mature score. Under stationarity, a length-\(k\) observable reweighting transports the behavior stationary distribution through \(k\) target-policy transitions, so the remaining discrepancy is the contraction-controlled distance between this finite-lag quantity and the stationary target value.

The bounded-reward normalization also pins down the range of the target value. This range fact is used when risks are compared over estimators whose outputs are clipped to the same unit interval.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:target-value-unit-interval}[Target value is bounded]
Let \(T\ge 1\), let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let
\(M=(\mathcal X,\mathcal H,K,b,e,\mathcal L(\mathsf O_T))\in
\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) be a model in \cref{obj:def:model-class}. Assume the model satisfies the stationary-start, sequential-ignorability, POMDP-kernel, bounded-reward, latent-stationary-overlap, and policy-overlap conditions of
\cref{obj:ass:stationary-start,obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:pomdp-kernel,obj:ass:bounded-reward,obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap,obj:ass:policy-overlap}. Then the stationary target-policy mean reward \(\theta(K,e)\) of \cref{obj:def:target-value} lies in the unit interval:
\[
\theta(K,e)\in[-1,1].
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:target-value-unit-interval}]
Let \(P_b\) and \(P_e\) be the joint-state transition kernels induced by the behavior and target policies, and let \(d_b\) and \(d_e\) be their selected stationary laws. For a joint state \(s=(x,h)\) and action \(a\in\mathcal A\), define the one-step reward mean
\[
\bar r(s,a)
:=
\int r\,K(d r,d x',d h'\mid s,a).
\]
We also use the local notation
\[
g_e(s)
:=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x)\bar r(s,a),
\]
which is the target-policy reward regression appearing inside \(\theta(K,e)\). With this notation, \cref{obj:def:target-value} gives
\[
\theta(K,e)=\sum_s d_e(s)g_e(s).
\]

\begin{enumerate}
\item Since \(T\ge1\), consider epoch \(0\). Let \(\eta_0\) denote the unique empty pre-history value at epoch \(0\), and define the finite state-history and state-action-history spaces
\[
\mathsf H^{\mathrm{st}}_0
:=
\{(\eta_0,s):s\in\mathcal S\},
\qquad
\mathsf H^{\mathrm{act}}_0
:=
\mathsf H^{\mathrm{st}}_0\times\mathcal A.
\]
For \(\upsilon=(\eta_0,s)\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{st}}_0\), write \(\operatorname{cur}(\upsilon)=s\). Let
\[
\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0:=(\eta_0,S_0),
\qquad
\Gamma^{\mathrm{act}}_0:=(\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0,A_0),
\qquad
\mu_0:=\mathcal L(\Gamma^{\mathrm{act}}_0).
\]
For every \(\gamma=(\upsilon,a)\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{act}}_0\) and every measurable \(B\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathcal S\), define the row selected by the whole state-action history view as
\[
\mathsf{row}_0(\gamma;B)
:=
K(B\mid \operatorname{cur}(\upsilon),a).
\]
By the probability-kernel part of \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel}, \(\mathsf{row}_0(\gamma;\cdot)\) is a probability measure for every \(\gamma\). Define the total function
\[
J_0(\upsilon,a)
:=
\int r\,\mathsf{row}_0((\upsilon,a);d r,d x',d h')
=
\int r\,K(d r,d x',d h'\mid \operatorname{cur}(\upsilon),a),
\]
for all \((\upsilon,a)\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{act}}_0\); bounds for \(J_0\) will be asserted only on the full-measure set obtained below. Thus, when \(\upsilon=(\eta_0,s)\), one has \(J_0(\upsilon,a)=\bar r(s,a)\).

By \cref{obj:ass:bounded-reward}, \(|Y_0|\le1\) holds almost surely under the trajectory law, so the event
\[
\mathcal R_0
:=
\{(\gamma,(r,s'))\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{act}}_0\times(\mathbb R\times\mathcal S): |r|\le1\}
\]
has full mass under the law of \((\Gamma^{\mathrm{act}}_0,(Y_0,S_1))\). After both sides are pushed forward to the epoch-\(0\) conditioning view, \cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel} gives the composition-product identity
\[
\mathcal L\{(\Gamma^{\mathrm{act}}_0,(Y_0,S_1))\in d\gamma\,d(r,s')\}
=
\mu_0(d\gamma)\,\mathsf{row}_0(\gamma;d r,d s').
\]
The section argument is now the standard disintegration fact for a composition product: if a set has full mass under the displayed law, then for \(\mu_0\)-almost every \(\gamma\) its section has full mass under the row \(\mathsf{row}_0(\gamma;\cdot)\). Applying this to \(\mathcal R_0\) gives
\[
\mathsf{row}_0\!\left(\gamma;\{(r,x',h'):\ |r|\le1\}\right)=1
\quad\text{for }\mu_0\text{-almost every }\gamma.
\]
For every such \(\gamma=(\upsilon,a)\), the row is a probability measure and the coordinate function \((r,x',h')\mapsto r\) is bounded by \(1\) in absolute value almost surely under that row. The usual norm bound for the integral of an almost-surely bounded real function under a probability measure therefore gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
|J_0(\upsilon,a)|
&=
\left|
\int r\,\mathsf{row}_0((\upsilon,a);d r,d x',d h')
\right| \\
&\le
\int |r|\,\mathsf{row}_0((\upsilon,a);d r,d x',d h')
\le 1 .
\end{aligned}
\]
Equivalently, using the definition of \(\mathsf{row}_0\),
\[
|J_0(\upsilon,a)|
=
\left|
\int r\,K(d r,d x',d h'\mid \operatorname{cur}(\upsilon),a)
\right|
\le1
\quad\text{for }\mu_0\text{-almost every }(\upsilon,a).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.targetValue_mem_unit_of_class

\item Fix \(s=(x,h)\) and \(a\in\mathcal A\) with \(d_b(s)>0\) and \(b(a\mid x)>0\). Put
\[
\upsilon_s:=(\eta_0,s)\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{st}}_0,
\qquad
\gamma_{s,a}:=(\upsilon_s,a)\in\mathsf H^{\mathrm{act}}_0.
\]
The singleton mass of \(\gamma_{s,a}\) is obtained from three separate ingredients, none of which gives it alone. The first is \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, which says only that the law \(\mu_0\) of \(\Gamma^{\mathrm{act}}_0\) factors as the law of \(\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0\) followed by the behavior kernel \(b(\cdot\mid X_0)\), so that
\[
\mu_0\{\gamma_{s,a}\}
=
\Pr\{\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0=\upsilon_s\}\,b(a\mid x).
\]
The second ingredient is the epoch-\(0\) history projection: the pre-history coordinate at epoch \(0\) is forced to be \(\eta_0\), so the event \(\{\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0=\upsilon_s\}\) is exactly \(\{S_0=s\}\). The third is \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start}, which identifies the law of \(S_0\) and gives
\[
\Pr\{\Gamma^{\mathrm{st}}_0=\upsilon_s\}=\Pr(S_0=s)=d_b(s).
\]
Combining the last two displays with the two strict-positivity hypotheses yields
\[
\mu_0\{\gamma_{s,a}\}
=
d_b(s)b(a\mid x)>0.
\]
If a full-\(\mu_0\)-measure set omitted this singleton, its complement would have positive \(\mu_0\)-mass. Hence the almost-sure bound from the previous step holds at \(\gamma_{s,a}\), and therefore
\[
|\bar r(s,a)|\le1
\qquad
\text{whenever } d_b(s)>0 \text{ and } b(a\mid x)>0.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.targetValue_mem_unit_of_class

\item The selected behavior and target stationary laws are probability vectors: by \cref{obj:synth_9} each \(d_p\) lies in \(\Delta(\mathcal S)\), the simplex of nonnegative probability vectors on the joint-state alphabet, fixed by the corresponding policy-induced kernel. Unpacking this simplex membership gives
\[
d_b(s)\ge0,
\qquad
d_e(s)\ge0
\quad\text{for all }s,
\qquad
\sum_s d_e(s)=1.
\]
Now suppose \(d_e(s)>0\) and \(e(a\mid x)>0\). By \cref{obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap},
\[
d_e(s)\le C\,d_b(s).
\]
If \(d_b(s)\) were not positive, its nonnegativity would force \(d_b(s)=0\), and substituting that into the displayed overlap inequality would give \(0<d_e(s)\le C\cdot0=0\), a contradiction. This uses the displayed overlap inequality, the nonnegativity \(d_b(s)\ge0\), and the local hypothesis \(d_e(s)>0\); the radius condition \(C\ge1\) plays no further role in this step. Thus \(d_b(s)>0\).

The action-coordinate argument uses the local hypothesis \(e(a\mid x)>0\), the nonnegativity of the behavior carrier, and the policy-overlap inequality. The inequality comes from \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap}, which for the policy-overlap constant \(L\) gives
\[
e(a\mid x)\le L\,b(a\mid x);
\]
the nonnegativity \(b(a\mid x)\ge0\) comes from \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability}, which is where \(b\) is declared a probability vector. If \(b(a\mid x)\) were not positive, that nonnegativity would force \(b(a\mid x)=0\), whence \(0<e(a\mid x)\le L\cdot0=0\), a contradiction. Thus \(b(a\mid x)>0\). The row bound from the previous step applies, and hence
\[
|\bar r(s,a)|\le1
\qquad
\text{whenever } d_e(s)>0 \text{ and } e(a\mid x)>0.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.targetValue_mem_unit_of_class

\item For any \(s=(x,h)\) with \(d_e(s)>0\), the target reward regression satisfies
\[
\begin{aligned}
|g_e(s)|
&=
\left|\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x)\bar r(s,a)\right|  \\
&\le
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} \left|e(a\mid x)\bar r(s,a)\right| \\
&=
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x)|\bar r(s,a)| \\
&\le
\sum_{a\in\mathcal A} e(a\mid x)
=1.
\end{aligned}
\]
The equality in the third line uses \(e(a\mid x)\ge0\); the last inequality uses the preceding step when \(e(a\mid x)>0\), while the corresponding summand is zero when \(e(a\mid x)=0\); and the final equality is the target-policy probability-vector property in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap}. Two distinct facts meet in that last equality, and they come from different places. Which set the sum runs over is a carrier fact: \cref{obj:synth_1} fixes the action coordinate as the Boolean two-point alphabet identified with \(\mathcal A=\{0,1\}\), and it imposes nothing else --- in particular no nonnegativity and no normalization. That the two values at \(x\) are nonnegative and add to one is the separate probability-vector restriction on \(e\), which is imposed in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap}. So the normalization is the sum of exactly two action probabilities at \(x\), and no larger action space is involved.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.targetValue_mem_unit_of_class

\item Finally,
\[
\begin{aligned}
|\theta(K,e)|
&=
\left|\sum_s d_e(s)g_e(s)\right| \\
&\le
\sum_s |d_e(s)g_e(s)| \\
&=
\sum_s d_e(s)|g_e(s)| \\
&\le
\sum_s d_e(s)
=1.
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(d_e(s)\ge0\) gives the third line; in the fourth line the summand is zero when \(d_e(s)=0\), and otherwise the regression bound from the previous step applies. Thus
\[
-1\le \theta(K,e)\le 1,
\]
which is exactly membership of \(\theta(K,e)\) in the closed real interval \([-1,1]\).
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.targetValue_mem_unit
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:target-value-unit-interval} records the bounded target range implied by the model-class reward normalization and the target stationary distribution. This range is the common scale for the clipped PHIW estimator and for the bounded observable estimators used in the minimax comparison.

The radius-sensitive upper calculation refines the estimator bound by keeping the latent-overlap distance \(q_C\) explicit.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:radius-sensitive-phiw}[Radius-sensitive PHIW risk]
For every trajectory length \(T\), observable alphabet size \(n_X\), latent alphabet size \(n_H\), and history depth \(k\), let
\[
\alpha=\exp\{-(1/t_0)\},\qquad L=\exp\{\zeta\},\qquad q_C=\frac{C-1}{C}.
\]
Suppose that
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Mixing scale.)} \(0<t_0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap scale.)} \(0<\zeta\).
\item \textbf{(Radius parameter.)} \(1\le C\), with \(q_C\) as in \cref{obj:def:overlap-distance-radius}.
\item \textbf{(Trajectory length.)} \(1\le T\).
\item \textbf{(Model class.)} The experiment \(M\) belongs to \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) in the sense of \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
\item \textbf{(History depth.)} \(k\le T/2\).
\end{itemize}
Then the clipped partial-history importance-weighted estimator \(\widehat\theta_k\) from \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}, evaluated under the observed trajectory law induced by \(M\), satisfies
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[\{\widehat\theta_k-\theta(K,e)\}^2\right]
\le
4q_C^2\alpha^{2k}
+\frac{2}{T}\left\{
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)
+\frac{4}{1-\alpha}
\right\},
\]
where \(\theta(K,e)\) is the stationary target-policy mean reward from \cref{obj:def:target-value}.
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:radius-sensitive-phiw}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item
Write \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\) for the observed trajectory law induced by \(M\), and let
\[
q_C:=\frac{C-1}{C}
\]
be the overlap-distance radius from \cref{obj:def:overlap-distance-radius}.  Define the unclipped partial-history average
\[
\widetilde\theta_k
:=
\frac{1}{T-k}\sum_{t=k}^{T-1} Z_t(k),
\qquad
\widehat\theta_k
=
\operatorname{clip}_{[-1,1]}(\widetilde\theta_k),
\]
with \(Z_t(k)\) as in \cref{obj:def:phiw-estimator}.  Since \(1\le T\) and \(k\le \lfloor T/2\rfloor\), one has \(k<T\), so \(T-k\ge 1\).  The conditions defining \(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) in \cref{obj:def:model-class} give \(L=e^\zeta>1\) and \(0<\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}<1\).  The finite observation space makes each score \(Z_t(k)\) measurable.  Moreover \(L=e^\zeta\ge 1\), and the sequential-ignorability, policy-overlap, and bounded-reward restrictions in \cref{obj:def:model-class} imply the window envelope
\[
|Z_t(k)|\le L^{k+1}
\qquad \mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\text{-a.s.},
\]
for every \(t\).  Thus each score is square-integrable under \(\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\), and so is \(\widetilde\theta_k\).  Moreover, \cref{obj:lem:target-value-unit-interval} applies to the present horizon and model class membership, giving
\[
\theta(K,e)\in[-1,1].
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_sensitive_phiw

\item
For every \(u\in\mathbb R\) and every \(\vartheta\in[-1,1]\),
\[
\bigl(\operatorname{clip}_{[-1,1]}(u)-\vartheta\bigr)^2
\le
(u-\vartheta)^2.
\]
Applying this pointwise with \(u=\widetilde\theta_k\) and \(\vartheta=\theta(K,e)\), and then using the usual bias--variance identity for the square-integrable random variable \(\widetilde\theta_k\), yields
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[
\{\widehat\theta_k-\theta(K,e)\}^2
\right]
\le
\operatorname{Var}_M(\widetilde\theta_k)
+
\left(
\mathbb E_M[\widetilde\theta_k]-\theta(K,e)
\right)^2 .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.sqRisk_clipUnit_le_variance_add_bias_sq

\item
We next bound the bias term.  Let \(d_b\) and \(d_e\) be the stationary laws of the behavior- and target-policy transition kernels, and let \(g_e\) be the target-policy reward regression appearing in \cref{obj:def:target-value}.  Define
\[
g_e^\circ(s)
:=
\begin{cases}
g_e(s), & d_b(s)>0,\\
0, & d_b(s)=0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
\Psi_k(s):=(P_e^k g_e^\circ)(s).
\]
On states with \(d_b(s)>0\), the horizon condition and the model-class restrictions give \(|g_e(s)|\le 1\); on states with \(d_b(s)=0\), the definition gives \(g_e^\circ(s)=0\).  Hence \(|g_e^\circ(s)|\le 1\) for every \(s\), and therefore
\[
\operatorname{osc}(g_e^\circ)
:=
\sup_{s,s'} |g_e^\circ(s)-g_e^\circ(s')|
\le 2.
\]
The kernel and policy restrictions in \cref{obj:def:model-class} make \(P_e\) a Markov transition kernel, and \cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction} supplies
\[
\|\lambda P_e-\lambda' P_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\|\lambda-\lambda'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\]
for all probability laws \(\lambda,\lambda'\) on \(\mathcal S\).  Consequently, for any bounded function \(r:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\) and any states \(s,s'\), the dual total-variation inequality gives
\[
\bigl|(P_e r)(s)-(P_e r)(s')\bigr|
\le
\operatorname{osc}(r)\,\|\delta_sP_e-\delta_{s'}P_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le
\alpha\operatorname{osc}(r).
\]
Iterating this one-step oscillation contraction yields
\[
\operatorname{osc}(P_e^k r)
\le
\alpha^k\operatorname{osc}(r).
\]
Applying the last display to \(r=g_e^\circ\) gives
\[
\operatorname{osc}(\Psi_k)
=
\operatorname{osc}(P_e^k g_e^\circ)
\le
2\alpha^k .
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:phiw-score-stationary-mean}, every score in the average defining \(\widetilde\theta_k\) has mean
\[
\mathbb E_M[Z_t(k)]
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S} d_b(s)(P_e^k g_e)(s),
\qquad t=k,\ldots,T-1.
\]
Therefore
\[
\mathbb E_M[\widetilde\theta_k]
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S} d_b(s)(P_e^k g_e)(s).
\]
For each state \(s\) with \(d_b(s)>0\), the functions \(g_e\) and \(g_e^\circ\) agree on the behavior support, so \cref{obj:lem:behavior-support-operator-congruence}, applied with the target policy and initial state \(s\), gives
\[
(P_e^k g_e)(s)=\Psi_k(s).
\]
For states with \(d_b(s)=0\), the common prefactor \(d_b(s)\) makes both weighted summands vanish, regardless of the two function values.  Hence
\[
\sum_s d_b(s)(P_e^k g_e)(s)
=
\sum_s d_b(s)\Psi_k(s).
\]
By \cref{obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap}, included among the restrictions in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, \(d_e(s)\le C d_b(s)\).  Thus \(d_e(s)>0\) implies \(d_b(s)>0\), so
\[
\theta(K,e)
=
\sum_s d_e(s)g_e(s)
=
\sum_s d_e(s)g_e^\circ(s).
\]
By the meaning of the target-policy stationary law used in \cref{obj:def:target-value}, \(d_e\) is invariant for \(P_e\).  Thus, for every function \(r_{\mathrm{test}}:\mathcal S\to\mathbb R\) and every integer \(m\ge0\),
\[
\sum_s d_e(s)r_{\mathrm{test}}(s)
=
\sum_s d_e(s)(P_e^m r_{\mathrm{test}})(s).
\]
Applying this identity with \(r_{\mathrm{test}}=g_e^\circ\) and \(m=k\) yields
\[
\sum_s d_e(s)g_e^\circ(s)
=
\sum_s d_e(s)\Psi_k(s).
\]
Combining the preceding displays,
\[
\mathbb E_M[\widetilde\theta_k]-\theta(K,e)
=
\sum_s \{d_b(s)-d_e(s)\}\Psi_k(s).
\]
For any function with oscillation at most \(2\alpha^k\),
\[
\left|
\sum_s \{d_b(s)-d_e(s)\}\Psi_k(s)
\right|
\le
2\alpha^k\,\|d_b-d_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}.
\]
It remains to prove the stationary-law distance subclaim
\[
\|d_b-d_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le q_C.
\]
This subclaim uses the pointwise comparison \(d_e(s)\le C d_b(s)\) from \cref{obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap}.  Throughout this step,
\[
\|\mu-\eta\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
:=
\frac12\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}|\mu(s)-\eta(s)|
=
\sup_{B\subseteq\mathcal S}|\mu(B)-\eta(B)|
\]
for probability laws on the finite joint-state space.  With this convention, any two probability laws have total-variation distance at most \(1\), since
\[
\frac12\sum_s |\mu(s)-\eta(s)|
\le
\frac12\sum_s\{\mu(s)+\eta(s)\}=1.
\]
If \(C=1\), then \(d_e(s)\le d_b(s)\) for every \(s\), and the two probability laws have equal total mass, so \(d_b=d_e\).  Hence
\[
\|d_b-d_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=0=q_C.
\]
If \(C>1\), define
\[
\eta_C(s):=\frac{C d_b(s)-d_e(s)}{C-1},\qquad s\in\mathcal S .
\]
The latent stationary overlap condition gives \(C d_b(s)-d_e(s)\ge0\) for every \(s\), and
\[
\sum_s \eta_C(s)
=
\frac{C\sum_s d_b(s)-\sum_s d_e(s)}{C-1}
=
1,
\]
so \(\eta_C\) is a probability law.  The identity
\[
d_b=\frac1C d_e+q_C\eta_C,
\qquad q_C=\frac{C-1}{C},
\]
then gives
\[
d_b-d_e
=
q_C(\eta_C-d_e).
\]
Therefore
\[
\|d_b-d_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
=
q_C\|\eta_C-d_e\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
\le q_C.
\]
Thus in all cases,
\[
\left|
\mathbb E_M[\widetilde\theta_k]-\theta(K,e)
\right|
\le
2q_C\alpha^k .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.phiw_bias_le

\item
The variance estimate supplied by \cref{obj:lem:phiw-raw-variance}, with \(L=e^\zeta\) and \(\alpha=e^{-1/t_0}\), gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}_M(\widetilde\theta_k)
\le
\frac{2}{T}\left\{
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)
+
\frac{4}{1-\alpha}
\right\}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.variance_phiwRaw_le

\item
Since \(q_C\ge 0\) and \(\alpha\ge 0\),
\[
\left(
\mathbb E_M[\widetilde\theta_k]-\theta(K,e)
\right)^2
\le
(2q_C\alpha^k)^2
=
4q_C^2\alpha^{2k}.
\]
Substituting this and the variance bound into the clipped bias--variance inequality proves
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[\{\widehat\theta_k-\theta(K,e)\}^2\right]
\le
4q_C^2\alpha^{2k}
+\frac{2}{T}\left\{
L^{k+1}\left(1+\frac{4}{L-1}\right)
+\frac{4}{1-\alpha}
\right\}.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_sensitive_phiw
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

In \cref{obj:lem:radius-sensitive-phiw}, the first term is the squared truncation contribution after \(k\) observable lags, scaled by the radius \(q_C\). The second term is the sampling contribution from estimating with length-\(k\) products of observable policy ratios. Choosing the radius-calibrated depth in \cref{obj:def:radius-adaptive-history-depth} balances these terms in the shrinking-overlap results.

The matching radius-explicit KL statement records the lower-bound calculation with the same radius normalization.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:radius-explicit-observed-kl}[Observed KL radius bound]
Let \(t_0>0\).  Define
\[
\alpha(t_0)=\exp(-1/t_0),\qquad
c_0(t_0)=\frac{1-\alpha(t_0)}{4}.
\]
Then there exists a constant \(K_0=K_0(t_0)>0\) such that, for every \(\zeta>0\), \(C>1\), \(T\in\mathbb N\), and \(Q\in\mathbb N\) with \(Q\ge1\), if
\[
\varepsilon_C=\min\left\{\frac{C-1}{2},\frac12\right\},\qquad
L(\zeta)=\exp(\zeta),
\]
and if \(q_C\) is the radius in \cref{obj:def:overlap-distance-radius}, then
\[
\frac{q_C}{2}\le \varepsilon_C \le q_C
\]
and the observed-path laws \(\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\) and
\(\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\) of the signed-depth alternatives satisfy
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0(t_0)^2K_0^2}{1-c_0(t_0)^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha(t_0)^{2Q}\,L(\zeta)^{-Q}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:radius-explicit-observed-kl}]
Fix \(t_0>0\).

1.  Apply \cref{obj:lem:observed-path-kl} with this value of \(t_0\).  It gives a constant \(K_0=K_0(t_0)>0\), and it remains only to supply the sequential-ignorability and stationary-start hypotheses for the signed-depth experiments over the range requested there.  For every auxiliary horizon \(T'\in\mathbb N\), terminal depth \(Q'\ge1\), and sign alternative \(v\in\{-1,+1\}\), the finite construction underlying \cref{obj:def:signed-depth-family} has the observed-state behavior kernel
\[
\Pr_v\!\left(A_t=a\mid X_{1:t},H_{1:t},A_{1:t-1},Y_{1:t-1}\right)=b(a\mid X_t),
\]
and the embedding into the observable-data experiment preserves this factorization, so \cref{obj:ass:sequential-ignorability} holds.  The same construction starts the joint state from its behavior stationary law,
\[
S_1\sim d_b,
\]
so \cref{obj:ass:stationary-start} holds for the same \((T',Q',v)\), including the zero-horizon case.  Hence \cref{obj:lem:observed-path-kl} supplies, for every \(\zeta>0\), \(C>1\), \(T\in\mathbb N\), and \(Q\ge1\), the sharpened observed-path bound
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{Q,+1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P_{Q,-1}^{\mathrm{obs},T}
\right)
\le
\frac{4c_0(t_0)^2K_0^2}{1-c_0(t_0)^2}\,
T\,\varepsilon_C^2\,\alpha(t_0)^{2Q}\,L(\zeta)^{-Q}.
\]
This KL estimate is one conjunct of the conclusion; the radius comparison is verified separately. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_explicit_observed_kl

2.  It remains to prove
\[
\frac{q_C}{2}\le \varepsilon_C\le q_C,
\qquad
q_C=\frac{C-1}{C},
\qquad
\varepsilon_C=\min\left\{\frac{C-1}{2},\frac12\right\}.
\]
Since \(C>1\), we have \(C>0\) and \(C-1\ge0\).  First suppose \(C\le2\).  Then
\[
\varepsilon_C=\frac{C-1}{2}.
\]
For the lower bound, \(q_C/2\le\varepsilon_C\) is
\[
\frac{C-1}{2C}\le\frac{C-1}{2},
\]
which follows from \(C>0\), \(C\ge1\), and \(C-1\ge0\).  For the upper bound, \(\varepsilon_C\le q_C\) is
\[
\frac{C-1}{2}\le\frac{C-1}{C}.
\]
Multiplying by \(2C>0\), this becomes
\[
C(C-1)\le2(C-1),
\]
and this follows from \(C-1\ge0\) and \(C\le2\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_explicit_observed_kl

3.  Now suppose \(C>2\).  Then
\[
\varepsilon_C=\frac12.
\]
For the lower bound, \(q_C/2\le\varepsilon_C\) is
\[
\frac{C-1}{2C}\le\frac12,
\]
equivalently \(C-1\le C\), which is immediate.  For the upper bound, \(\varepsilon_C\le q_C\) is
\[
\frac12\le\frac{C-1}{C}.
\]
Multiplying by \(2C>0\), this becomes \(C\le2(C-1)\), equivalently \(2\le C\), which follows from \(C>2\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_explicit_observed_kl

Combining the two cases gives the asserted radius comparison, and the KL bound is the one obtained in Step 1 with \(L(\zeta)=\exp(\zeta)\) and \(\alpha(t_0)=\exp(-1/t_0)\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.radius_explicit_observed_kl
\end{proof}

The inequalities in \cref{obj:lem:radius-explicit-observed-kl} connect the construction amplitude \(\varepsilon_C\) to the normalized radius from \cref{obj:def:overlap-distance-radius}. Consequently, the lower calculation can be stated on the same radius scale as the estimator bound in \cref{obj:lem:radius-sensitive-phiw}.

The final group records the parametric two-experiment comparison used to anchor the risk at the usual \(T^{-1}\) scale across all radii in the model class. The first two statements compute the separation in target value and the observed KL budget for the singleton-state pair; the following bounded-risk and information inequalities convert those calculations into risk floors.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:parametric-pair-target-value}[Parametric pair target value]
Let \(T\in\mathbb N\), let \(v\in\{0,1\}\), and define the sign
\[
\sigma(v)=
\begin{cases}
+1, & v=1,\\
-1, & v=0.
\end{cases}
\]
Put
\[
\eta_T=\frac{1}{4\sqrt T},
\]
with value \(0\) when \(T=0\). Suppose the scalar parameters satisfy:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Initial scale.)} \(t_0>0\).
\item \textbf{(Overlap scale.)} \(\zeta>0\).
\item \textbf{(Radius scale.)} \(C\ge 1\).
\end{itemize}
Let \(M_T^v\) be the singleton-state finite experiment with one observed state and one hidden state, fair behavior and target policies \(b(a\mid x_\star)=e(a\mid x_\star)=1/2\), reward support \(\{-1,+1\}\), and reward-transition kernel that assigns, from every state-action pair, probability
\[
\frac{1+\sigma(v)y\eta_T}{2}
\]
to reward \(y\in\{-1,+1\}\) and next state \((x_\star,h_\star)\). Then the stationary target-policy mean reward of \cref{obj:def:target-value}, evaluated in this experiment, satisfies
\[
\theta(K_{M_T^v},e_{M_T^v})=\sigma(v)\eta_T .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-target-value}]
Let \(F=M_T^v\), viewed first as the finite reward model before applying the embedding into the raw experiment.  Write
\[
d_T^v(s)
:=
d_e(s)
\]
for the target-policy stationary law on the singleton joint-state space \(\mathcal S=\{(x_\star,h_\star)\}\) associated with this finite model.  The construction of \(M_T^v\), under \(t_0>0\), \(\zeta>0\), and \(C\ge 1\), belongs to \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\); in particular the target stationary law is a probability vector.  Thus
\[
d_T^v(s)\ge 0
\quad\text{for all }s\in\mathcal S,
\qquad
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_T^v(s)=1 .
\]
The equality between the finite encoding and the raw experiment preserves this stationary law, so the same \(d_T^v\) is the law used in \(\theta(K_{M_T^v},e_{M_T^v})\) as defined in \cref{obj:def:target-value}. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_mem

For every joint state \(s\in\mathcal S\) and action \(a\in\{0,1\}\), the conditional reward mean in the finite kernel is
\[
\sum_{y\in\{-1,+1\}}
y\,\Pr(Y=y\mid S=s,A=a)
=
(-1)\frac{1-\sigma(v)\eta_T}{2}
+
(+1)\frac{1+\sigma(v)\eta_T}{2}
=
\sigma(v)\eta_T .
\]
The displayed calculation is exactly the reward-kernel mean, with the encoding \(0\mapsto -1\) and \(1\mapsto +1\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_kernelMean

By \cref{obj:def:target-value} and the finite-to-raw value identity for this encoding,
\[
\theta(K_{M_T^v},e_{M_T^v})
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}
d_T^v(s)
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}
e_{M_T^v}(a\mid x(s))
\sum_{y\in\{-1,+1\}}
y\,\Pr(Y=y\mid S=s,A=a).
\]
Substituting the preceding reward-mean identity gives
\[
\theta(K_{M_T^v},e_{M_T^v})
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}
d_T^v(s)
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}
e_{M_T^v}(a\mid x(s))\,\sigma(v)\eta_T .
\]
The target policy in \(M_T^v\) is fair, hence for every \(s\),
\[
\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}}e_{M_T^v}(a\mid x(s))=1 .
\]
Therefore
\[
\theta(K_{M_T^v},e_{M_T^v})
=
\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_T^v(s)\,\sigma(v)\eta_T
=
\sigma(v)\eta_T\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}d_T^v(s)
=
\sigma(v)\eta_T ,
\]
using the total mass identity for \(d_T^v\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_targetValue
\end{proof}

\begin{lemmav}{lem:parametric-pair-observed-kl}[Observed KL budget]
Let \(T\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(T\ge 1\), and set \(\eta_T=1/(4\sqrt T)\). Let \(M_T^{-}\) and \(M_T^{+}\) be the two finite singleton-state experiments with one observed state \(x_\star\), one hidden state, initial mass at the single joint state, fair behavior and target policies \(b=e\) on \(\{0,1\}\), and reward code \(\mathsf r\in\{0,1\}\) decoded as \(2\mathsf r-1\). Under \(M_T^\sigma\), for \(\sigma\in\{-1,+1\}\), the reward-transition kernel returns to the single joint state and assigns reward \(y\in\{-1,+1\}\) probability
\[
\frac{1+\sigma y\eta_T}{2}.
\]
Writing \(\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^\sigma}\) for the image of the decoded trajectory law under the observed-path projection, the required KL budget holds in the direction
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^-}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^+}
\right)
\le \frac14 .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-observed-kl}]
Write \(F_{-}\) and \(F_{+}\) for the two finite reward-code models with Boolean indices
\(\mathrm{false}\) and \(\mathrm{true}\), so that the associated signs are \(-1\) and \(+1\).  Let
\[
        \eta_T=\frac{1}{4\sqrt T}.
\]
Since \(T\ge 1\), we have \(0<\sqrt T\) and \(0\le \eta_T\le 1/4\).

\begin{enumerate}
\item
The two finite models use the same reward decoder \(r\mapsto 2r-1\).  Therefore applying the decoder
and then projecting to the observed path is a common measurable map, and KL contracts under this map:
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^-}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^+}
\right)
\le
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
(F_-)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\,\middle\|\,
(F_+)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right),
\]
where \((F_\sigma)^{\mathrm{obs}}\) denotes the finite observed-path law before decoding the reward
code into \(\{-1,+1\}\). % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.embed_klDiv_le

\item
For one epoch define probability laws \(\mu_{-}\) and \(\mu_{+}\) on
\(\{x_\star\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\) by
\[
\mu_\sigma(x_\star,a,r)
=
\frac12\,
\frac{1+\sigma(2r-1)\eta_T}{2},
\qquad
\sigma\in\{-1,+1\},\quad a,r\in\{0,1\}.
\]
The first factor is the fair action law, and the second factor is the reward-code law.  Because
\(0\le \eta_T\le 1/4\), every displayed cell mass is positive. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricEpochPMF_toReal

\item
Multiplying the singleton initial mass, the fair action probabilities, and the displayed
time-homogeneous reward-transition probabilities gives the product form
\[
(F_\sigma)^{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\bigotimes_{t=1}^{T}\mu_\sigma,
\qquad \sigma\in\{-1,+1\}.
\]
The singleton observed state contributes unit mass at each time, so the only nontrivial coordinates in
each factor are the fair action and the reward code. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_obsPMF_eq_pi

\item
The one-epoch divergence is bounded by
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\mu_{-}\|\mu_{+})
\le
4\eta_T^2.
\]
Let \(A\) be the fair law on \(\{0,1\}\), let \(\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u)\) be the law on
\(\{0,1\}\) assigning mass \(u\) to reward code \(1\), and set
\[
u_-:=\frac{1-\eta_T}{2},
\qquad
u_+:=\frac{1+\eta_T}{2}.
\]
Since \(0\le \eta_T\le 1/4\), both \(u_-\) and \(u_+\) lie in \([3/8,5/8]\).  The law
\(\mu_\sigma\) is obtained from \(\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_\sigma)\otimes A\) by the bijective
coordinate map \((r,a)\mapsto (x_\star,a,r)\).  Hence invariance under this reordering and the common
action coordinate give
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\mu_{-}\|\mu_{+})
=
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_- )\otimes A
\,\middle\|\,
\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_+ )\otimes A\right)
=
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_- )
\,\middle\|\,
\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_+ )\right).
\]
The Boolean Bernoulli laws are images of the two-point real laws
\((1-u)\delta_0+u\delta_1\) under the measurable map that records whether the point is \(1\), so data
processing gives the upper bound by the corresponding real two-point divergence.  Evaluating that
finite divergence yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_- )
\,\middle\|\,
\operatorname{Ber}_{\{0,1\}}(u_+ )\right)
&\le
u_-\log\frac{u_-}{u_+}+(1-u_- )\log\frac{1-u_-}{1-u_+} \\
&=
\eta_T\log\frac{1+\eta_T}{1-\eta_T}.
\end{aligned}
\]
For \(0\le s\le 1/4\), the function
\(4s-\log((1+s)/(1-s))\) has value \(0\) at \(s=0\) and derivative
\[
4-\frac{2}{1-s^2}
\ge
4-\frac{2}{1-(1/4)^2}
>
0.
\]
Thus \(\log((1+s)/(1-s))\le 4s\) on this interval, and substituting \(s=\eta_T\) gives the displayed
one-epoch bound. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricEpoch_klDiv_le

\item
Since every one-epoch cell under \(\mu_{+}\) has positive mass, \(\mu_{-}\ll\mu_{+}\); on this finite
space the log-likelihood ratio is integrable.  Tensorization of KL for the \(T\)-fold product laws gives
two finite-valued conclusions: the product divergence is finite, and after applying the usual real value
map to the extended nonnegative divergence,
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\bigotimes_{t=1}^{T}\mu_{-}
\,\middle\|\,
\bigotimes_{t=1}^{T}\mu_{+}
\right)_{\!\mathbb R}
\le
T\,\operatorname{KL}(\mu_{-}\|\mu_{+})_{\mathbb R}.
\]
The one-epoch bound from the previous step is finite, so the same real-value comparison gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\mu_{-}\|\mu_{+})_{\mathbb R}
\le
4\eta_T^2.
\]
The product representation of the finite observed-path law therefore yields
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
(F_-)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\,\middle\|\,
(F_+)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right)_{\!\mathbb R}
\le
T\cdot 4\eta_T^2.
\]
Because the displayed product divergence is finite, comparison with any finite numerical bound is
equivalent to comparison after taking this real value. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_finiteObs_klDiv_le

\item
Finally,
\[
T\cdot 4\eta_T^2
=
T\cdot 4\left(\frac{1}{4\sqrt T}\right)^2
=
\frac14,
\]
using \(0<\sqrt T\) and \((\sqrt T)^2=T\).  The finite-valued comparison from the preceding step gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
(F_-)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\,\middle\|\,
(F_+)^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right)
\le
\frac14.
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_finiteObs_klDiv_le
\end{enumerate}

The contraction inequality from the first step and the finite observed-path bound from the last step
give
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^-}
\,\middle\|\,
\mathbb P^{\mathrm{obs}}_{M_T^+}
\right)
\le
\frac14,
\]
as claimed. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_observed_klDiv_le
\end{proof}

Before applying the two-point inequality, the next statement records a uniform boundedness fact for clipped observable-data estimators. It uses the target-value range from \cref{obj:lem:target-value-unit-interval} and the estimator range built into \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:observed-risk-bounded-by-four}[Observed risk bound]
Let \(T\ge 1\) and let \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\). Let \(\widehat\theta\) be an observable-data estimator of the admissible class used in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}: for each observed-alphabet size and policy pair, \(\widehat\theta\) is a measurable real-valued function of the observed path taking values in \([-1,1]\). For every
\[
M=(\mathcal X,\mathcal H,K,b,e,\mathcal L(\mathsf O_T))\in \mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)
\]
as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}, with \(\widehat\theta\) evaluated at the observed-alphabet size and policies of \(M\), the squared risk for the target value \(\theta(K,e)\) of \cref{obj:def:target-value} satisfies
\[
E_M\bigl[(\widehat\theta-\theta(K,e))^2\bigr]\le 4 .
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:observed-risk-bounded-by-four}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix
\[
M=(\mathcal X,\mathcal H,K,b,e,\mathcal L(\mathsf O_T))\in
\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C),
\]
and write an observed path as
\[
\omega_{\mathrm{obs}}=\bigl((X_t,A_t,Y_t)\bigr)_{t=0}^{T-1}\in \mathsf O_T .
\]
For this path set
\[
r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})
:=
\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})-\theta(K,e).
\]
The admissibility condition in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk} gives
\[
-1\le \widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})\le 1,
\]
and \cref{obj:lem:target-value-unit-interval} gives
\[
-1\le \theta(K,e)\le 1 .
\]
Hence
\[
-2\le r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})\le 2 .
\]
Equivalently,
\[
0\le r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})+2,
\qquad
0\le 2-r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}}).
\]
Multiplying these two nonnegative quantities yields
\[
0\le
\bigl(r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})+2\bigr)
\bigl(2-r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})\bigr)
=
4-r_{\mathrm{err}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})^2,
\]
so
\[
\bigl(\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})-\theta(K,e)\bigr)^2
\le 4
\qquad
\text{for every }\omega_{\mathrm{obs}}\in\mathsf O_T .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.observedRisk_le_four

\item The function
\[
\omega_{\mathrm{obs}}
\longmapsto
\bigl(\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})-\theta(K,e)\bigr)^2
\]
is measurable because \(\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\cdot)\) is measurable by admissibility and the target value is constant in the observed path. The observed-path law \(\mathcal L_M^{\mathrm{obs}}\) is a probability law, and the pointwise bound from the previous step gives integrability together with
\[
\int
\bigl(\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})-\theta(K,e)\bigr)^2
\,d\mathcal L_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})
\le
\int 4\,d\mathcal L_M^{\mathrm{obs}}(\omega_{\mathrm{obs}})
=
4 .
\]
By the squared-risk definition in \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}, the left-hand side is exactly
\[
E_M\bigl[(\widehat\theta-\theta(K,e))^2\bigr].
\]
Therefore
\[
E_M\bigl[(\widehat\theta-\theta(K,e))^2\bigr]\le 4 .
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.observedRisk_le_four
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:observed-risk-bounded-by-four} places all admissible estimator losses on a common bounded scale. This supplies the integrability and range control used when the two-point comparison is embedded into the minimax risk.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:two-point-risk-floor}[Two-point risk floor]
Let \(T\ge 1\), \(t_0,\zeta,C\in\mathbb R\), and let \(M_0,M_1\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) as in \cref{obj:def:model-class}.  After identifying their common observed alphabet, write \(\mathbb P_j\) for the observed-data law of \(M_j\) and \(\theta_j\) for the target value of \(M_j\) from \cref{obj:def:target-value}, \(j\in\{0,1\}\).  Let \(s\) be a nonnegative separation radius and let \(\chi\) be a nonnegative KL upper bound.  Assume:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Common observable structure.)} The two models have the same observed-alphabet cardinality, the same behavior policy, and the same target policy.
\item \textbf{(Information bound.)} \(\mathbb P_0\ll\mathbb P_1\),
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\mathbb P_0\|\mathbb P_1)\le \chi,
\qquad
\operatorname{KL}(\mathbb P_0\|\mathbb P_1)<\infty .
\]
\item \textbf{(Target separation.)}
\[
2s\le |\theta_0-\theta_1| .
\]
\end{itemize}
Then every raw observable-data estimator \(\widehat\theta\), viewed as a real-valued measurable function of the observed-alphabet size, the policy pair, and the observed path, with \((\widehat\theta-\theta_j)^2\) integrable under \(\mathbb P_j\) at \(M_j\) for \(j=0,1\), satisfies
\[
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
\max\left\{
\mathbb E_{M_0}\!\left[(\widehat\theta-\theta_0)^2\right],
\mathbb E_{M_1}\!\left[(\widehat\theta-\theta_1)^2\right]
\right\}.
\]
Consequently, the minimax squared-error risk of \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk} satisfies
\[
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
R_T(t_0,\zeta,C).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor}]
Write the common observed alphabet size as \(n_X\), and use the common observable
structure to identify the behavior and target policy carriers of the two models.  Put
\[
P_j:=\mathbb P_j,\qquad
\theta_j:=\theta(M_j),\qquad j\in\{0,1\},
\]
where \(\theta(M_j)\) is the target value from \cref{obj:def:target-value}.  The
information bound and \(\chi\ge0\) give
\[
\operatorname{KL}(P_0\|P_1)_{\mathbb R}\le \chi ,
\]
where \(\operatorname{KL}(P_0\|P_1)_{\mathbb R}\) denotes the real value of the
finite extended-real KL divergence.  Hence
\[
\exp(-\chi)
\le
\exp\{-\operatorname{KL}(P_0\|P_1)_{\mathbb R}\}.
\]
By the Bretagnolle--Huber affinity inequality, using \(P_0\ll P_1\) and finite
KL divergence,
\[
\frac12\exp\{-\operatorname{KL}(P_0\|P_1)_{\mathbb R}\}
\le
1-\operatorname{TV}(P_0,P_1).
\]
Combining the two displays yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-point-risk-floor-affinity}
\frac{\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
\frac{1-\operatorname{TV}(P_0,P_1)}{2}.
\end{equation} % lean: Causalean.Stat.bretagnolle_huber_affinity

Fix a raw observable-data estimator \(\widehat\theta\) satisfying the measurability
and integrability hypotheses.  With the common observable inputs fixed, define
\[
\widehat f(o):=\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e,o),
\qquad o\in\mathsf O_T,
\]
and define the two error events
\[
E_j:=\{o\in\mathsf O_T:\ s\le |\widehat f(o)-\theta_j|\},
\qquad j\in\{0,1\}.
\]
The separation condition \(2s\le|\theta_0-\theta_1|\) and the two-point testing
bound for real-valued parameters give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-point-risk-floor-testing}
\frac{1-\operatorname{TV}(P_0,P_1)}{2}
\le
\max\{P_0(E_0),P_1(E_1)\}.
\end{equation} % lean: Causalean.Stat.real_two_point_lower_bound

Since \(s\ge0\), for each \(j\in\{0,1\}\),
\[
E_j
=
\{o\in\mathsf O_T:\ s^2\le(\widehat f(o)-\theta_j)^2\}.
\]
The squared-error integrability assumptions therefore allow the elementary
tail-to-mean comparison
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-point-risk-floor-tail-mean}
s^2 P_j(E_j)
\le
\int(\widehat f(o)-\theta_j)^2\,dP_j(o),
\qquad j\in\{0,1\}.
\end{equation} % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.two_point_floor_explicit

Combining \cref{eq:two-point-risk-floor-affinity,eq:two-point-risk-floor-testing} gives
\[
\frac{\exp(-\chi)}{4}\le \max\{P_0(E_0),P_1(E_1)\}.
\]
If \(P_0(E_0)\le P_1(E_1)\), then the last maximum is \(P_1(E_1)\), and \cref{eq:two-point-risk-floor-tail-mean} gives
\[
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
s^2P_1(E_1)
\le
\int(\widehat f(o)-\theta_1)^2\,dP_1(o)
\le
\max_{j\in\{0,1\}}
\int(\widehat f(o)-\theta_j)^2\,dP_j(o).
\]
If \(P_1(E_1)\le P_0(E_0)\), the same argument gives
\[
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
s^2P_0(E_0)
\le
\int(\widehat f(o)-\theta_0)^2\,dP_0(o)
\le
\max_{j\in\{0,1\}}
\int(\widehat f(o)-\theta_j)^2\,dP_j(o).
\]
These two cases prove
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-point-risk-floor-fixed-models}
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
\max\left\{
\mathbb E_{M_0}\!\left[(\widehat\theta-\theta_0)^2\right],
\mathbb E_{M_1}\!\left[(\widehat\theta-\theta_1)^2\right]
\right\}.
\end{equation} % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.two_point_floor_explicit

It remains to pass from the fixed two models to the minimax risk in
\cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}.  The observable-estimator class is nonempty, for
example it contains the constant-zero estimator.  We first record the bounded-risk input needed for the minimax reduction.  Fix an admissible observable estimator \(\widetilde\theta\).  For every model
\(M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\), with observed alphabet size \(n_X^M\), behavior carrier \(b_M\), target carrier \(e_M\), observed law \(P_M\), and target value \(\theta(M)\), \cref{obj:lem:observed-risk-bounded-by-four} gives
\[
\mathbb E_M\!\left[
\bigl(\widetilde\theta(n_X^M,b_M,e_M,\mathsf O_T)-\theta(M)\bigr)^2
\right]
=
\int\bigl(\widetilde\theta(n_X^M,b_M,e_M,o)-\theta(M)\bigr)^2\,dP_M(o)
\le 4.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\left\{
\mathbb E_M\!\left[
\bigl(\widetilde\theta(n_X^M,b_M,e_M,\mathsf O_T)-\theta(M)\bigr)^2
\right]:
M\in\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)
\right\}
\subset (-\infty,4],
\]
which is the boundedness hypothesis needed in the two-point minimax reduction. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.bddAbove_range_observedRisk
For such an estimator, the two integrability requirements used above hold at
\(M_0\) and \(M_1\).  These are two separate statements, one for each model, and
each is taken at that model's own observable inputs before any identification of
carriers: at \(M_j\) the estimator is measurable by admissibility and its range
condition gives
\[
|\widehat\theta(n_X,b_{M_j},e_{M_j},o)|\le 1,
\qquad o\in\mathsf O_T,
\]
so the fixed-model squared error is dominated by the constant
\((1+|\theta(M_j)|)^2\), since
\[
|\widehat\theta(n_X,b_{M_j},e_{M_j},o)-\theta(M_j)|
\le
1+|\theta(M_j)|,
\qquad j\in\{0,1\}.
\]
Only after both integrability statements are in hand do the common-policy
hypotheses \(b_{M_0}=b_{M_1}\) and \(e_{M_0}=e_{M_1}\) rewrite the two carriers
into the single pair \((b,e)\) used for \(\widehat f\) above, so that the two
integrals are integrals of one function against the two observed laws.
Applying \cref{eq:two-point-risk-floor-fixed-models} to each admissible observable estimator and then the standard
two-point reduction to the minimax value yields
\[
\frac{s^2\exp(-\chi)}{4}
\le
R_T(t_0,\zeta,C),
\]
which is the claimed minimax lower bound. % lean: Causalean.Stat.le_minimaxValue_of_two_point
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-target-value} sets the value separation for the singleton-state Rademacher pair, and \cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-observed-kl} gives the corresponding observed KL budget. The bounded-risk statement in \cref{obj:lem:observed-risk-bounded-by-four} fixes the loss scale for admissible clipped estimators, and the generic reduction in \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor} then translates a common-policy, low-divergence pair with target separation into a lower bound for every observable-data estimator.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:uniform-parametric-floor}[Uniform parametric floor]
Fix a positive mixing scale \(t_0>0\) and a positive log-overlap scale \(\zeta>0\). For every horizon \(T\in\mathbb N\) with \(T\ge 1\) and every radius \(C\ge 1\), let \(M_T^{-}\) and \(M_T^{+}\) be the two raw POMDP experiments generated by the singleton-state, singleton-observation Rademacher construction with rewards in \(\{-1,+1\}\), fair behavior and target policies \(b=e\), and the two opposite signs of the construction's parametric reward tilt. Then:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Class membership.)} Both \(M_T^{-}\) and \(M_T^{+}\) belong to the latent-overlap model class \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) of \cref{obj:def:model-class}.
\item \textbf{(Common policies.)} Each experiment has equal behavior and target policies, and the two experiments share these policies:
\[
b_{M_T^{-}}=e_{M_T^{-}},
\qquad
b_{M_T^{+}}=e_{M_T^{+}},
\qquad
b_{M_T^{-}}=b_{M_T^{+}},
\qquad
e_{M_T^{-}}=e_{M_T^{+}}.
\]
\item \textbf{(Uniform minimax floor.)} The minimax squared-error risk \(R_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) of \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk} satisfies
\[
\frac{\exp(-1/4)}{64T}\le R_T(t_0,\zeta,C).
\]
\item \textbf{(Pointwise two-experiment floor.)} For every observable-data estimator \(\widehat\theta\), viewed as a measurable function of the revealed alphabet and the policies, under the usual squared-error integrability conditions for the observed trajectory laws of \(M_T^{-}\) and \(M_T^{+}\),
\[
\frac{\exp(-1/4)}{64T}
\le
\max\left\{
\mathbb E_{M_T^{-}}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\left[
\left(\widehat\theta(1,b_{M_T^{-}},e_{M_T^{-}};\mathsf O_T)-\theta(K_{M_T^{-}},e_{M_T^{-}})\right)^2
\right],
\mathbb E_{M_T^{+}}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\left[
\left(\widehat\theta(1,b_{M_T^{+}},e_{M_T^{+}};\mathsf O_T)-\theta(K_{M_T^{+}},e_{M_T^{+}})\right)^2
\right]
\right\},
\]
where \(\theta(K,e)\) is the stationary target-policy mean reward of \cref{obj:def:target-value}.
\end{itemize}
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:uniform-parametric-floor}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Fix \(T\ge 1\) and put
\[
\eta_T:=\frac{1}{4\sqrt T}.
\]
Since \(T\ge1\), \(0\le \eta_T\le 1/4\).  For each sign
\(\sigma\in\{-1,+1\}\), define a singleton-state experiment with
\(\mathcal X=\{x_\star\}\), \(\mathcal H=\{h_\star\}\), fair policies
\[
b_\sigma(a\mid x_\star)=e_\sigma(a\mid x_\star)=\frac12,
\qquad a\in\{0,1\},
\]
and reward-transition kernel
\[
\Pr_\sigma\{Y_t=y,\ X_{t+1}=x_\star,\ H_{t+1}=h_\star
       \mid X_t=x_\star,H_t=h_\star,A_t=a\}
=
\frac{1+\sigma y\eta_T}{2},
\qquad y\in\{-1,+1\}.
\]
Let \(M_T^{-}\) be the experiment with \(\sigma=-1\), and \(M_T^{+}\) the
experiment with \(\sigma=+1\).  The weights above are nonnegative and sum to
one.  The hypotheses give the positive parameter-scale requirements
\(0<t_0\) and \(0<\zeta\) appearing in \cref{obj:def:model-class}.  The
Markov-kernel, sequential-ignorability, stationary-start, and bounded-reward
requirements in
\cref{obj:ass:pomdp-kernel,obj:ass:sequential-ignorability,obj:ass:stationary-start,obj:ass:bounded-reward}
hold by this generated singleton construction.  The policy-overlap condition
in \cref{obj:ass:policy-overlap} holds because \(b_\sigma=e_\sigma\) and
\(\exp(\zeta)\ge1\).  For either policy \(p\in\{b_\sigma,e_\sigma\}\),
\[
\nu P_p=\nu'P_p=\delta_{(x_\star,h_\star)}
\]
for all probability laws \(\nu,\nu'\) on the joint state space.  Put
\[
\alpha_{t_0}:=\exp(-1/t_0).
\]
Since \(t_0>0\), \(\alpha_{t_0}>0\), and therefore
\(\alpha_{t_0}\|\nu-\nu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge0\).  Thus
\[
\|\nu P_p-\nu'P_p\|_{\mathrm{TV}}
=0
\le
\alpha_{t_0}\|\nu-\nu'\|_{\mathrm{TV}},
\]
which is the uniform-contraction requirement in
\cref{obj:ass:uniform-contraction}.  Finally, the latent stationary overlap condition in
\cref{obj:ass:latent-stationary-overlap} is immediate:
\(d_{e_\sigma}=d_{b_\sigma}=\delta_{(x_\star,h_\star)}\), and hence
\(d_{e_\sigma}(s)\le C\,d_{b_\sigma}(s)\) for every state whenever \(C\ge1\).
Thus both alternatives belong to \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) as defined in
\cref{obj:def:model-class}.  The same displayed definition of the policies
also gives
\[
b_{M_T^-}=e_{M_T^-},\qquad
b_{M_T^+}=e_{M_T^+},\qquad
b_{M_T^-}=b_{M_T^+},\qquad
e_{M_T^-}=e_{M_T^+}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.uniform_parametric_floor_certificates

\item The singleton alternatives just constructed are the two alternatives covered
by \cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-target-value}.  With the sign convention of
Step 1, that result gives
\[
\theta_-
:=\theta(K_{M_T^-},e_{M_T^-})
=-\eta_T,
\qquad
\theta_+
:=\theta(K_{M_T^+},e_{M_T^+})
=\eta_T.
\]
Consequently
\[
2\eta_T
=
|\theta_{-}-\theta_{+}|.
\]
For the divergence calculation, let \(\mathsf r\in\{0,1\}\) be the reward code
and set
\[
\mathsf y(\mathsf r):=2\mathsf r-1,
\qquad
\mathsf y(0)=-1,
\qquad
\mathsf y(1)=+1.
\]
Define the one-epoch coded observed law
\(\Lambda_T^\sigma\in\Delta(\{x_\star\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\})\) by
\[
\Lambda_T^\sigma\{(x_\star,a,\mathsf r)\}
:=
\frac12\,\frac{1+\sigma\mathsf y(\mathsf r)\eta_T}{2},
\qquad a,\mathsf r\in\{0,1\}.
\]
Since \(0\le\eta_T\le1/4\), every displayed cell has positive mass.  Thus
\(\Lambda_T^-\ll\Lambda_T^+\), and the log likelihood ratio is integrable.  If
\(\Xi_T^-\) and \(\Xi_T^+\) denote the reward-code marginals of
\(\Lambda_T^-\) and \(\Lambda_T^+\), then
\[
\Xi_T^-\{1\}=\frac{1-\eta_T}{2},
\qquad
\Xi_T^+\{1\}=\frac{1+\eta_T}{2}.
\]
The fair action factor is common to the two signs, so the product rule for KL
with a common second factor gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\Lambda_T^-\|\Lambda_T^+)
=
\operatorname{KL}(\Xi_T^-\|\Xi_T^+).
\]
Writing out the two Bernoulli masses gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\Xi_T^-\|\Xi_T^+)
=
\frac{1-\eta_T}{2}\log\frac{1-\eta_T}{1+\eta_T}
+
\frac{1+\eta_T}{2}\log\frac{1+\eta_T}{1-\eta_T}
=
\eta_T\log\frac{1+\eta_T}{1-\eta_T}.
\]
For \(0\le u\le1/4\),
\[
\log\frac{1+u}{1-u}
=
\log(1+u)-\log(1-u)
\le
u+\frac{u}{1-u}
\le 4u,
\]
using \(\log(1+u)\le u\) and \(-\log(1-u)\le u/(1-u)\).  Applying this with
\(u=\eta_T\) yields the one-epoch bound
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\Lambda_T^-\|\Lambda_T^+)
\le 4\eta_T^2.
\]
Let
\[
\Pi_{\sigma,T}^{\mathrm{code}}
:=
\bigotimes_{i=1}^T\Lambda_T^\sigma
\quad\text{on}\quad
(\{x_\star\}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\})^T.
\]
The generated finite trajectory construction has this product as its coded
observed-word law.  By finite-product KL tensorization, with the absolute
continuity and integrability supplied by the positive one-epoch masses,
\[
\operatorname{KL}(\Pi_{-,T}^{\mathrm{code}}\|
\Pi_{+,T}^{\mathrm{code}})
\le
T\operatorname{KL}(\Lambda_T^-\|\Lambda_T^+)
\le
4T\eta_T^2.
\]
Because \(T\ge1\), \(\sqrt T>0\), and \(\eta_T=1/(4\sqrt T)\),
\[
4T\eta_T^2=\frac14.
\]
Finally, let \(\mathfrak d_T\) be the decoding map that replaces each reward
code \(\mathsf r\) by \(\mathsf y(\mathsf r)\) and keeps the observed state and
action coordinates.  The observed laws in the statement are the pushforwards
\[
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}
=(\mathfrak d_T)_\#\Pi_{-,T}^{\mathrm{code}},
\qquad
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}
=(\mathfrak d_T)_\#\Pi_{+,T}^{\mathrm{code}}.
\]
KL divergence is monotone under measurable pushforward, so the preceding coded
product bound gives
\[
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\middle\|
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right)
\le
\operatorname{KL}(\Pi_{-,T}^{\mathrm{code}}\|
\Pi_{+,T}^{\mathrm{code}})
\le
\frac14.
\]
This is the observed KL budget in \cref{obj:lem:parametric-pair-observed-kl}.
The positive one-epoch masses give
\(\Pi_{-,T}^{\mathrm{code}}\ll\Pi_{+,T}^{\mathrm{code}}\), hence also
\(\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}\ll\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}\), and the
displayed upper bound gives finite KL divergence. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.parametricPair_observed_klDiv_le

\item Let \(\widehat\theta\) be any observable-data estimator satisfying the
measurability and two integrability assumptions in the statement, and write
\[
\varphi(o):=
\widehat\theta(1,b_{M_T^-},e_{M_T^-};o)
=
\widehat\theta(1,b_{M_T^+},e_{M_T^+};o),
\]
where the equality uses the common policies from Step 1.  The two-point risk
inequality applied here is \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor}, used in its
raw-estimator form for the estimator \(\widehat\theta\).  Its structural
hypotheses are available here: the observed alphabets both have one state and
the behavior and target policies are common by Step 1.  Step 2 supplies
\[
2\eta_T\le |\theta_- - \theta_+|,
\qquad
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}\ll
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}},
\qquad
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\middle\|
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right)
\le \frac14,
\]
together with finite KL divergence.  The present step supplies the required
measurability of \(\widehat\theta(n_X,b,e;\cdot)\) for every input
\((n_X,b,e)\), and the two squared-error integrability assumptions for the two
displayed risks.

Apply \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor} directly to the raw estimator
\(\widehat\theta\), the two observed laws
\[
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}},
\qquad
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}},
\]
the target values
\[
\theta_-=
\theta(K_{M_T^-},e_{M_T^-}),
\qquad
\theta_+=
\theta(K_{M_T^+},e_{M_T^+}),
\]
and the numerical choices
\[
s_\star=\eta_T,
\qquad
\chi_{\mathrm{KL}}=\frac14.
\]
The separation condition is supplied by Step 2, and the absolute-continuity and
finite-KL hypotheses are supplied by the final display of Step 2.  Hence
\[
\frac{\eta_T^2\exp(-1/4)}{4}
\le
\max\left\{
\mathbb E_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\!\left[
\{\varphi(\mathsf O_T)-\theta_-\}^2
\right],
\mathbb E_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\!\left[
\{\varphi(\mathsf O_T)-\theta_+\}^2
\right]
\right\}.
\]
Finally,
\[
\frac{\eta_T^2\exp(-1/4)}{4}
=
\frac{\exp(-1/4)}{64T},
\]
which is exactly the pointwise two-experiment floor in the statement.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.two_point_floor_explicit

\item It remains to record the minimax lower bound.  For the supplied radius \(C\),
the two singleton alternatives with the same kernels and policies satisfy the
hypotheses of the minimax conclusion in \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor}:
Step 1 gives their common observable cardinality, common behavior policy,
common target policy, and membership in \(\mathcal M_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\); Step 2
gives
\[
2\eta_T
\le
|\theta_- - \theta_+|,
\qquad
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}\ll
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}},
\qquad
\operatorname{KL}\!\left(
\mathbb P_{M_T^-}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\middle\|
\mathbb P_{M_T^+}^{\mathrm{obs}}
\right)
\le
\frac14,
\]
with finite KL divergence.  Applying \cref{obj:lem:two-point-risk-floor} with
\[
s_\star=\eta_T,
\qquad
\chi_{\mathrm{KL}}=\frac14
\]
yields
\[
\frac{\eta_T^2\exp(-1/4)}{4}
\le
R_T(t_0,\zeta,C),
\]
where \(R_T(t_0,\zeta,C)\) is the observable minimax risk of
\cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}.  Since
\[
\frac{\eta_T^2\exp(-1/4)}{4}
=
\frac{\exp(-1/4)}{64T},
\]
the desired minimax floor follows.  Together with the membership and policy
identities from Step 1 and the pointwise inequality from Step 3, this proves
all four asserted conclusions.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.PomdpLatentOverlapMinimax.uniform_parametric_floor
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:uniform-parametric-floor} complements the signed-depth lower construction by giving a radius-uniform benchmark at the parametric scale. The experiments share behavior and target policies, so the displayed floor reflects value uncertainty in the observed rewards themselves and applies throughout the latent-overlap model class.
